\sqrt{n}\Big(\widehat{x}^{db}_{n,i}-x^{0}_{i}\Big)\to_{d}N(0,4\sigma_{i}^{2})\quad\text{or}\quad\sqrt{n}\Big(\widehat{x}^{db}_{n,i}+x^{0}_{i}\Big)\to_{d}N(0,4\sigma_{i}^{2})\quad(i=1,\ldots,p).

Thus, when
x^{0}_{i}=0
, the result follows immediately. On the other hand, when
x^{0}_{i}\neq 0
, the result follows from an application of Delta method.

Proof 17.2
(of Corollary
4.6
).

The proof follows from Propossition
4.3
and Lemma
17.4
.

Proof 17.3
(of Corollary
4.4
).

From Proposition
4.3
it follows that

n^{1/2}\begin{bmatrix}(\widehat{x}^{db}_{n})_{i}-x^{0}_{i}\\
(\widehat{x}^{db}_{n})_{j}-x^{0}_{j}\end{bmatrix}\to_{d}N_{2}\left(\begin{bmatrix}0\\
0\end{bmatrix},\underbrace{\begin{bmatrix}\sigma_{i}^{2}&\sigma_{ij}\\
\sigma_{ij}&\sigma_{j}^{2}\end{bmatrix}}_{\Sigma_{\text{pro}}}\right).

(58)

Consider the function
g(x,y)=xy
. If
x^{0}_{i}x^{0}_{j}\neq 0
, then either
x^{0}_{i}\neq 0
or
x^{0}_{j}\neq 0
, which implies

\dot{g}(x^{0}_{i},x^{0}_{j})^{T}\Sigma_{\text{pro}}\dot{g}(x^{0}_{i},x^{0}_{j})=4(x^{0}_{j})^{2}\sigma^{2}_{i}+4(x^{0}_{i})^{2}\sigma^{2}_{j}+8x^{0}_{i}x^{0}_{j}\sigma_{ij}>0.

Then by delta method,

n^{1/2}\Big(g((\widehat{x}^{db}_{n})_{i},(\widehat{x}^{db}_{n})_{j})-x^{0}_{i}x^{0}_{j}\Big)\to_{d}N\Big(0,\dot{g}(x^{0}_{i},x^{0}_{j})^{T}\Sigma_{\text{pro}}\dot{g}(x^{0}_{i},x^{0}_{j})\Big),

which completes the proof of the first part.
Now suppose both
x^{0}_{i}=0
and
x^{0}_{j}=0
. Then (
58
) reduces to

\underbrace{n^{1/2}\begin{bmatrix}(\widehat{x}^{db}_{n})_{i}\\
(\widehat{x}^{db}_{n})_{j}\end{bmatrix}}_{V_{n}}\to_{d}N_{2}(0,\Sigma_{\text{pro}}).

Fix
t\in\mathbb{R}
.
Let
C_{t}=\{y\in\mathbb{R}^{2}\ :\ y^{T}Ay\leq t\}
, where

A=2^{-1}\begin{bmatrix}0&1\\
1&0\end{bmatrix}.

Note that

P\Big(n(\widehat{x}^{db}_{n})_{i}(\widehat{x}^{db}_{n})_{j}\leq t\Big)=P(V_{n}^{T}AV_{n}\leq t)=P(V_{n}\in C_{t}).

Since
V_{n}\to_{d}N_{2}(0,\Sigma_{\text{pro}})
, if we can show that
C_{t}
is a continuity set of the latter distribution, it would follow that

P(V_{n}\in C_{t})\to P\Big(N_{2}(0,\Sigma_{\text{pro}})\in C_{t}\Big)=P(\mathbb{Z}_{i}\mathbb{Z}_{j}\leq t)

where
\mathbb{Z}_{i}\sim N(0,\sigma_{i}^{2})
and
\mathbb{Z}_{j}\sim N(0,\sigma_{j}^{2})
so that their covariance is
\sigma_{ij}
. Hence it remains to prove that
C_{t}
is a continuity point of
N_{2}(0,\Sigma_{\text{pro}})
for all
t\in\mathbb{R}
, which means
P(N_{2}(0,\Sigma_{\text{pro}})\in\partial C_{t})=0
for all
t\in\mathbb{R}
, where
\partial C_{t}
is the boundary of
C_{t}
, that is

\partial C_{t}=\{y\in\mathbb{R}^{2}\ :\ y^{T}Ay=t\}.

That
P(N_{2}(0,\Sigma_{\text{pro}})\in\partial C_{t})=0
will trivially follow if we can show that
\Sigma_{\text{pro}}
is a positive definite matrix. However, the latter follows from the fact that
\Sigma_{p}
is positive definite noting
\Sigma_{\text{pro}}
is a principal minor of
\Sigma_{p}
. Hence, the proof follows.

17.1
Additional lemmas for the proofs of Supplement
17

The next lemma is essential in proving Corollary
4.6
.

Lemma 17.4
.

Suppose
X
and
Y
are Gaussian. Then

4\sigma_{i}^{2}={\rho}_{0}(1-{\rho}_{0}^{2})\sum_{k=2}^{r}\frac{({\rho}_{0}^{2}+\Lambda_{k}^{2})}{({\rho}_{0}^{2}-\Lambda_{k}^{2})^{2}}(u_{k})_{i}^{2}+\frac{21{\rho}_{0}^{4}-13{\rho}_{0}^{2}+8}{32{\rho}_{0}^{2}}(x_{0})_{i}^{2}+(1-{\rho}_{0}^{2})\frac{\sum_{i=r+1}^{p}(u_{k})_{i}^{2}}{{\rho}_{0}}.

In particular,

4\sigma_{i}^{2}\geq\min\bigg(20.58945,\frac{1-{\rho}_{0}^{2}}{{\rho}_{0}}\bigg).

Proof 17.5
(of Lemma
17.4
).

Suppose
1\leq i\leq p
and as usual, we let
e_{1}
be a unit vector whose first element is one, whose length depends on the context.

To find the variance of
\mathcal{Z}(i)
, we will use Lemma
13.16
with

\displaystyle a={\rho}_{0}\Phi^{0}_{i,1}+\xi_{1}(i)x^{0},\quad b=x^{0},\quad c={\rho}_{0}\Phi^{0}_{i,2}+\xi_{2}(i)y^{0},d=y^{0},z=\Phi^{0}_{i,1},\gamma=\Phi^{0}_{i,2}.

Noting

\mathcal{Z}(i)=a^{T}XX^{T}b+c^{T}YY^{T}d-z^{T}XY^{T}d-b^{T}XY^{T}\gamma,

(x^{0})^{T}{\Sigma}_{x}x^{0}={\rho}_{0},\quad(y^{0})^{T}{\Sigma}_{y}y^{0}={\rho}_{0},\quad(x^{0})^{T}{\Sigma}_{xy}y^{0}={\rho}_{0}^{2},

and using Lemma
13.16
, we see that
\sigma^{2}_{i}=\text{var}(\mathcal{Z}(i))
equals

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ (\Phi^{0}_{i,1})^{T}{\Sigma}_{x}\Phi^{0}_{i,1}({\rho}_{0}-{\rho}_{0}^{3})+(\Phi^{0}_{i,2})^{T}{\Sigma}_{y}\Phi^{0}_{i,2}({\rho}_{0}-{\rho}_{0}^{3})

\displaystyle\ +2{\rho}_{0}^{2}(\xi_{1}(i)^{2}+\xi_{2}(i)^{2})+(14{\rho}_{0}^{4}+2-12{\rho}_{0}^{2})\xi_{1}(i)\xi_{2}(i),

where
\xi_{1}(i)=(\Phi^{0}_{i,1})^{T}{\Sigma}_{x}x^{0}
and
\xi_{2}(i)=(\Phi^{0}_{i,2})^{T}{\Sigma}_{y}y^{0}
. Note that Lemma
21.1
implies

\displaystyle\xi_{1}(i)=(\Phi^{0}_{i,1})^{T}{\Sigma}_{x}x^{0}=(2{\rho}_{0})^{-1}e_{i}^{T}(UO_{4}U^{T}+{\Sigma}_{x}^{-1}){\Sigma}_{x}x^{0}=

\displaystyle\ (2{\rho}_{0}^{1/2})^{-1}\Big(U_{*i}^{T}O_{4}e_{1}+(\alpha_{0})_{i}\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ (2{\rho}_{0}^{1/2})^{-1}\Big(-5(u_{1})_{i}/8+(\alpha_{0})_{i}\Big).

Since
u_{1}=\alpha_{0}
, we obtain that

(\Phi^{0}_{i,1})^{T}{\Sigma}_{x}x^{0}=3{\rho}_{0}^{-1/2}(\alpha_{0})_{i}/16=\frac{3(x_{0})_{i}}{16{\rho}_{0}}.

Lemma
21.1
also implies

\displaystyle\xi_{2}(i)=(\Phi^{0}_{i,2})^{T}{\Sigma}_{y}y^{0}=(2{\rho}_{0})^{-1}e_{i}^{T}(UO_{3}V^{T}){\Sigma}_{y}y^{0}=

\displaystyle\ (2{\rho}_{0}^{1/2})^{-1}U_{i*}^{T}O_{3}e_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ {\rho}_{0}^{-1/2}U_{i*}^{T}e_{1}/16=\frac{(x_{0})_{i}}{16{\rho}_{0}}.

Therefore
\xi_{1}(i)=3\xi_{2}(i)
, which implies

\displaystyle\sigma_{i}^{2}=

\displaystyle\ (\Phi^{0}_{i,1})^{T}{\Sigma}_{x}\Phi^{0}_{i,1}({\rho}_{0}-{\rho}_{0}^{3})+(\Phi^{0}_{i,2})^{T}{\Sigma}_{y}\Phi^{0}_{i,2}({\rho}_{0}-{\rho}_{0}^{3})+2(21{\rho}_{0}^{4}-8{\rho}_{0}^{2}+3)\xi_{2}(i)^{2}.

Therefore it suffices to find the values of
(\Phi^{0}_{i,1})^{T}{\Sigma}_{x}\Phi^{0}_{i,1}
and
(\Phi^{0}_{i,2})^{T}{\Sigma}_{y}\Phi^{0}_{i,2}
.
Lemma
21.1
yields that

\displaystyle(\Phi^{0}_{i,1})^{T}{\Sigma}_{x}\Phi^{0}_{i,1}=

\displaystyle\ \frac{e_{i}^{T}(UO_{4}U^{T}+{\Sigma}_{x}^{-1}){\Sigma}_{x}(UO_{4}U^{T}+{\Sigma}_{x}^{-1})e_{i}}{4{\rho}_{0}^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \frac{U^{T}_{i*}(O_{4}^{2}+2O_{4})U_{i*}+({\Sigma}_{x}^{-1})_{ii}}{4{\rho}_{0}^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \frac{(x_{0})^{2}_{i}(25/64-10/8)}{4{\rho}_{0}^{3}}+\sum_{k=2}^{r}\frac{\Big(\Lambda^{4}_{k}+2\Lambda_{k}^{2}({\rho}_{0}^{2}-\Lambda_{k}^{2})\Big)(u_{k})_{i}^{2}}{4{\rho}_{0}^{2}({\rho}_{0}^{2}-\Lambda_{k}^{2})^{2}}+\frac{({\Sigma}_{x}^{-1})_{ii}}{4{\rho}_{0}^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ -\frac{55(x_{0})_{i}^{2}}{16^{2}{\rho}_{0}^{3}}-\sum_{k=2}^{r}\frac{\Lambda_{k}^{4}(u_{k})_{i}^{2}}{4{\rho}_{0}^{2}({\rho}_{0}^{2}-\Lambda_{k}^{2})^{2}}+\sum_{k=2}^{r}\frac{\Lambda_{k}^{2}(u_{k})_{i}^{2}}{2({\rho}_{0}^{2}-\Lambda_{k}^{2})^{2}}+\frac{({\Sigma}_{x}^{-1})_{ii}}{4{\rho}_{0}^{2}},

and

\displaystyle(\Phi^{0}_{i,2})^{T}{\Sigma}_{y}\Phi^{0}_{i,2}=

\displaystyle\ \frac{e_{i}^{T}UO_{3}V^{T}{\Sigma}_{y}VO_{3}U^{T}e_{i}}{4{\rho}_{0}^{2}}=\frac{U_{i*}^{T}O_{3}^{2}U_{i*}}{4{\rho}_{0}^{2}}=\frac{(x_{0})_{i}^{2}}{16^{2}{\rho}_{0}^{3}}+\sum_{k=2}^{r}\frac{\Lambda_{k}^{2}(u_{k})_{i}^{2}}{4({\rho}_{0}^{2}-\Lambda_{k}^{2})^{2}}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\sigma^{2}_{i}=

\displaystyle\ ({\rho}_{0}-{\rho}_{0}^{3})\sum_{k=2}^{r}\frac{(3-\Lambda^{2}/{\rho}_{0}^{2})\Lambda_{k}^{2}(u_{k})_{i}^{2}}{4({\rho}_{0}^{2}-\Lambda_{k}^{2})^{2}}+({\rho}_{0}-{\rho}_{0}^{3})\frac{({\Sigma}_{x}^{-1})_{ii}}{4{\rho}_{0}^{2}}

\displaystyle\ +2(21{\rho}_{0}^{4}-8{\rho}_{0}^{2}+3)\frac{(x_{0})^{2}_{i}}{16^{2}{\rho}_{0}^{2}}-(1-{\rho}_{0}^{2})\frac{54(x_{0})_{i}^{2}}{16^{2}{\rho}_{0}^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ (1-{\rho}_{0}^{2})\sum_{k=2}^{r}\frac{3{\rho}_{0}^{2}\Lambda_{k}^{2}-\Lambda_{k}^{4}}{4{\rho}_{0}({\rho}_{0}^{2}-\Lambda_{k}^{2})^{2}}(u_{k})_{i}^{2}+(1-{\rho}_{0}^{2})\frac{({\Sigma}_{x}^{-1})_{ii}}{4{\rho}_{0}}+\frac{42{\rho}_{0}^{4}+38{\rho}_{0}^{2}-48}{16^{2}{\rho}_{0}^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ (1-{\rho}_{0}^{2})\sum_{k=2}^{r}\frac{3{\rho}_{0}^{2}\Lambda_{k}^{2}-\Lambda_{k}^{4}}{4{\rho}_{0}({\rho}_{0}^{2}-\Lambda_{k}^{2})^{2}}(u_{k})_{i}^{2}+(1-{\rho}_{0}^{2})\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{r}(u_{k})_{i}^{2}}{4{\rho}_{0}}+\frac{42{\rho}_{0}^{4}+38{\rho}_{0}^{2}-48}{16^{2}{\rho}_{0}^{2}}(x_{0})_{i}^{2}

\displaystyle\ +(1-{\rho}_{0}^{2})\frac{\sum_{i=r+1}^{p}(u_{k})_{i}^{2}}{4{\rho}_{0}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ (1-{\rho}_{0}^{2})\sum_{k=2}^{r}\frac{3{\rho}_{0}^{2}\Lambda_{k}^{2}-\Lambda_{k}^{4}+({\rho}_{0}^{2}-\Lambda_{k}^{2})^{2}}{4{\rho}_{0}({\rho}_{0}^{2}-\Lambda_{k}^{2})^{2}}(u_{k})_{i}^{2}+\frac{42{\rho}_{0}^{4}+38{\rho}_{0}^{2}-48+64-64{\rho}_{0}^{2}}{16^{2}{\rho}_{0}^{2}}(x_{0})_{i}^{2}

\displaystyle\ +(1-{\rho}_{0}^{2})\frac{\sum_{i=r+1}^{p}(u_{k})_{i}^{2}}{4{\rho}_{0}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ {\rho}_{0}(1-{\rho}_{0}^{2})\sum_{k=2}^{r}\frac{({\rho}_{0}^{2}+\Lambda_{k}^{2})}{4({\rho}_{0}^{2}-\Lambda_{k}^{2})^{2}}(u_{k})_{i}^{2}+\frac{42{\rho}_{0}^{4}-26{\rho}_{0}^{2}+16}{16^{2}{\rho}_{0}^{2}}(x_{0})_{i}^{2}+(1-{\rho}_{0}^{2})\frac{\sum_{i=r+1}^{p}(u_{k})_{i}^{2}}{4{\rho}_{0}}

Thus
4\sigma_{i}^{2}
equals

{\rho}_{0}(1-{\rho}_{0}^{2})\sum_{k=2}^{r}\frac{({\rho}_{0}^{2}+\Lambda_{k}^{2})}{({\rho}_{0}^{2}-\Lambda_{k}^{2})^{2}}(u_{k})_{i}^{2}+\frac{21{\rho}_{0}^{4}-13{\rho}_{0}^{2}+8}{32{\rho}_{0}^{2}}(x_{0})_{i}^{2}+(1-{\rho}_{0}^{2})\frac{\sum_{i=r+1}^{p}(u_{k})_{i}^{2}}{{\rho}_{0}}

which is bounded below by

\displaystyle\frac{(1-{\rho}_{0}^{2})}{{\rho}_{0}}(1-(\alpha_{0})_{i}^{2})+\frac{42{\rho}_{0}^{4}-26{\rho}_{0}^{2}+16}{64{\rho}_{0}}(\alpha_{0})_{i}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \frac{64-64{\rho}_{0}^{2}+(42{\rho}_{0}^{4}+38{\rho}_{0}^{2}-48)(\alpha_{0})_{i}^{2}}{64{\rho}_{0}}

The quadratic
21x^{2}+19x-24
has only positive root at

x_{r}=\frac{-19\pm(19^{2}+4*21*24)}{2*21}
, which is approximately
0.708
. Therefore
21{\rho}_{0}^{4}+19{\rho}_{0}^{2}-24
has only positive root at
(x_{r})^{1/2}
. which is approximately
0.841
. This polynomial is positive to the right of
(x_{r})^{1/2}
, and negative to the left of it. Therefore, for
{\rho}_{0}\geq(x_{r})^{1/2}
,

4\sigma_{i}^{2}\geq\frac{1-{\rho}_{0}^{2}}{{\rho}_{0}}

which is bounded away from
0
because
{\rho}_{0}
is bounded away from one and zero. On the other hand, for
{\rho}_{0}<(x_{r})^{1/2}
, we have
42{\rho}_{0}^{4}+38{\rho}_{0}^{2}-48<0
, which, noting
(\alpha_{0})_{i}^{2}\leq 1
, leads to

\displaystyle 4\sigma_{i}^{2}=

\displaystyle\ \frac{64-64{\rho}_{0}^{2}+(42{\rho}_{0}^{4}+38{\rho}_{0}^{2}-48)(\alpha_{0})_{i}^{2}}{64{\rho}_{0}}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\ \frac{64-64{\rho}_{0}^{2}+42{\rho}_{0}^{4}+38{\rho}_{0}^{2}-48}{64{\rho}_{0}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \underbrace{\frac{42{\rho}_{0}^{4}-26{\rho}_{0}^{2}+16}{64{\rho}_{0}}}_{h_{\rho}({\rho}_{0})}.

The function
h_{\rho}:[0,1]\mapsto\mathbb{R}
is positive non-increasing in the interval
(0,1)
. Hence, for
{\rho}_{0}\in(0,(x_{r})^{1/2})
, we have
h_{\rho}({\rho}_{0})>h_{\rho}[(x_{r})^{1/2}]\approx 20.58945
.
Therefore for all
{\rho}_{0}\in(0,1)
, we have

4\sigma_{i}^{2}\geq\min\bigg(20.58945,\frac{1-{\rho}_{0}^{2}}{{\rho}_{0}}\bigg).

18
Proof of Theorem
4.7

Note that if we can show
\widehat{\rho}_{n}^{2,\text{raw}}
satisfies

n^{1/2}(\widehat{\rho}_{n}^{2,\text{raw}}-{\rho}_{0}^{2})\to_{d}N(0,\sigma_{\rho}^{2}),

(59)

then
\widehat{\rho}_{n}^{2,\text{raw}}={\rho}_{0}^{2}+O_{p}(n^{-1/2})
would follow. The latter implies
P(\widehat{\rho}_{n}^{2,\text{raw}}\in(0,1))\to 1
, which leads to
P(\widehat{\rho}_{n}^{2,\text{raw}}=\widehat{\rho}_{n}^{2,db})\to 1
as
n\to\infty
. The latter, in conjunction with (
59
), would complete the proof. Hence it suffices to show (
59
) holds.

Proof 18.1
(of Theorem
4.7
).

Suppose
w_{1}=\argmin_{w\in\{\pm 1\}}\|w\widehat{x}_{n}-x^{0}\|_{2}
and

w_{2}=\argmin_{w\in\{\pm 1\}}\|w\widehat{y}_{n}-y^{0}\|_{2}
. The proof of Theorem
4.7
requires the following two lemmas to address the sign flip. Both these lemmas are proved in Subsection
21.1
.

Lemma 18.2
.

Suppose
\widehat{x}_{n}
and
\widehat{y}_{n}
satisfy
(\widehat{x}_{n})^{T}\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,xy}\widehat{y}_{n}>0
for all
n
. Then for sufficiently large
n
,
P(w_{1}w_{2}=-1)\to 0
.

Lemma 18.3
.

Suppose
w_{1}=w_{2}=w
. Then the estimator
\widehat{\rho}_{n}^{2,\text{raw}}
constructed using
w\widehat{x}_{n}
and
w\widehat{y}_{n}
equals that constructed using
\widehat{x}_{n}
and
\widehat{y}_{n}
.

Since we take
\widehat{x}_{n}
and
\widehat{y}_{n}
so as to satisfy
\widehat{x}_{n}^{T}\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,xy}\widehat{y}_{n}>0
, by Lemma
18.2
, for sufficiently large
n
,
(w_{1},w_{2})
equals either
(1,1)
or
(-1,-1)
with high probability. However, Fact
18.3
implies that the estimator
\widehat{\rho}_{n}^{2,\text{raw}}
constructed with
\widehat{x}_{n}
,
\widehat{y}_{n}
and
-\widehat{x}_{n}
,
-\widehat{y}_{n}
are the same. Hence, without loss of generality, we assume that
w_{1}=w_{2}=1
, and thus by Lemma
13.2
,

\|\widehat{x}_{n}-x^{0}\|_{1}+\|\widehat{y}_{n}-y^{0}\|_{1}=O_{p}(s^{\kappa+1/2}\lambda),\quad\|\widehat{x}_{n}-x^{0}\|_{2}+\|\widehat{y}_{n}-y^{0}\|_{2}=O_{p}(s^{\kappa}\lambda).

Now we state another Lemma, which will be used to prove the asymptotic expansion of
\widehat{\rho}_{n}^{2,\text{raw}}
.

Lemma 18.4
.

Suppose
\mathcal{L}_{(1)}
and
\mathcal{L}_{(2)}
are as in (
14
). Then it follows that

-\mathcal{L}_{1}^{T}{\Sigma}_{xy}y^{0}-\mathcal{L}_{2}^{T}{\Sigma}_{yx}x^{0}+(x^{0})^{T}(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,xy}-\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,xy})y^{0}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{(Z_{i}-E[Z_{i}])}{n}

where
Z_{i}=-{\rho}_{0}(X_{i}^{T}x^{0})^{2}-{\rho}_{0}(Y_{i}^{T}y^{0})^{2}+2(X_{i}^{T}x^{0})(Y_{i}^{T}y^{0})
.

Now note that

\displaystyle\widehat{x}_{n}^{T}\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,xy}\widehat{y}_{n}-{\rho}_{0}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \widehat{x}_{n}^{T}\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,xy}\widehat{y}_{n}-(x^{0})^{T}{\Sigma}_{xy}(y^{0})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \widehat{x}_{n}^{T}\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,xy}(\widehat{y}_{n}-y^{0})+(\widehat{x}_{n}-x^{0})^{T}\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,xy}y^{0}+(x^{0})^{T}(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,xy}-{\Sigma}_{xy})y^{0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \widehat{x}_{n}^{T}\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,xy}(\widehat{y}_{n}-y^{0})+(\widehat{x}_{n}-x^{0})^{T}\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,xy}(y^{0}-\widehat{y}_{n})

\displaystyle\ +\widehat{y}_{n}^{T}\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,yx}(\widehat{x}_{n}-x^{0})+(x^{0})^{T}(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,xy}-{\Sigma}_{xy})y^{0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \widehat{x}_{n}^{T}\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,xy}(\widehat{y}_{n}-\widehat{y}^{db}_{n})+\widehat{x}_{n}^{T}\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,xy}(\widehat{y}^{db}_{n}-y^{0})+(\widehat{x}_{n}-x^{0})^{T}\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,xy}(y^{0}-\widehat{y}_{n})

\displaystyle\ +\widehat{y}_{n}^{T}\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,yx}(\widehat{x}_{n}-\widehat{x}^{db}_{n})+\widehat{y}_{n}^{T}\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,yx}(\widehat{x}^{db}_{n}-x^{0})+(x^{0})^{T}(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,xy}-{\Sigma}_{xy})y^{0}.

(60)

Since
w_{1}=1
, and
w_{2}=1
, Theorem
4.1
implies that

\displaystyle\text{rem}=\widehat{x}^{db}_{n}-x^{0}+\mathcal{L}_{(1)}

(61)

satisfies
\|\text{rem}\|_{\infty}=o_{p}(s^{2\kappa}\lambda^{2})
.
We can write

\displaystyle\widehat{y}_{n}^{T}\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,yx}(\widehat{x}^{db}_{n}-x^{0})=

\displaystyle\ (\widehat{y}_{n}-y^{0})^{T}\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,yx}(\widehat{x}^{db}_{n}-x^{0})-(y^{0})^{T}{\Sigma}_{yx}\mathcal{L}_{(1)}-(y^{0})^{T}(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,yx}-{\Sigma}_{yx})\mathcal{L}_{(1)}

\displaystyle\ +(y^{0})^{T}\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,yx}\text{rem}

which implies

\displaystyle\absolutevalue{ \hy^T\hSyx(\hdai-x^0)+ (y^0)^T\hSyx\mathcal{L}_{(1)}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\ |(\widehat{y}_{n}-y^{0})^{T}\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,yx}\mathcal{L}_{(1)}|+|(\widehat{y}_{n}-y^{0})^{T}\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,yx}\text{rem}|+|(y^{0})^{T}(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,yx}-{\Sigma}_{yx})\mathcal{L}_{(1)}|+|(y^{0})^{T}\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,yx}\text{rem}|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\ \|\widehat{y}_{n}-y^{0}\|_{1}|\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,yx}|_{\infty}(\|\mathcal{L}_{1}\|_{\infty}+\|\text{rem}\|_{\infty})+\|y^{0}\|_{1}|\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,yx}-{\Sigma}_{yx}|_{\infty}\|\mathcal{L}_{(1)}\|_{\infty}+\|y^{0}\|_{1}|\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,yx}|_{\infty}\|\text{rem}\|_{\infty}.

(62)

From Lemma
13.2
it follows that

\|\widehat{y}_{n}-y^{0}\|_{1}
is
O_{p}(s^{\kappa+1/2}\lambda)
. Lemma
13.9
implies
|\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,xy}-{\Sigma}_{xy}|_{\infty}
is
O_{p}(\lambda)
and
|\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,xy}|_{\infty}
is
O_{p}(1)
, and (
61
) indicates that
\|\text{rem}\|_{\infty}=O_{p}(s^{2\kappa}\lambda^{2})
. Lemma
13.7
entails that
\|y^{0}\|_{1}=O_{p}(s^{1/2})
. The definition of
\mathcal{L}_{1}
in (
14
) implies that
\|\mathcal{L}_{1}\|_{\infty}
is of the order
O_{p}(\|(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,x}-{\Sigma}_{x})x^{0}\|_{\infty})+O_{p}(\|(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,y}-{\Sigma}_{y})y^{0}\|_{\infty})
. Using Lemma
13.7
and Lemma
13.9
, therefore, we can show that

\|\mathcal{L}_{1}\|_{\infty}=O_{p}(\lambda)
. Using these rates in the bound derived in (
18.1
), we obtain that

\displaystyle\absolutevalue{ \hy^T\hSyx(\hdai-x^0)+ (y^0)^T\Syx\mathcal{L}_{(1)}}\leq

\displaystyle\ O_{p}(s^{3\kappa+1}\lambda^{3})+O_{p}(s^{\kappa+1/2}\lambda^{2})+O_{p}(s^{1/2}\lambda^{2})+O_{p}(s^{2\kappa+1/2}\lambda^{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ O_{p}(s^{3\kappa+1}\lambda^{3})+O_{p}(s^{2\kappa+1/2}\lambda^{2}).

By Fact
1
,
s^{\kappa+1/2}\lambda\to 0
. Thus, the above bound is of order
O_{p}(s^{2\kappa+1/2}\lambda^{2})
, which is
o_{p}(n^{-1/2})
by our assumption on
s
.
By symmetry, we also have

\absolutevalue{ \hx^T\hSxy(\hdbi-y^0)+ (x^0)^T\Sxy\mathcal{L}_{(2)}}=o_{p}(n^{-1/2}),

where
\mathcal{L}_{2}
is as defined in (
14
).
Therefore, (
18.1
) leads to

\displaystyle\widehat{x}_{n}^{T}\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,xy}\widehat{y}^{db}_{n}+(\widehat{x}^{db}_{n})^{T}\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,xy}\widehat{y}_{n}-\widehat{x}_{n}^{T}\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,xy}\widehat{y}_{n}-{\rho}_{0}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ -(y^{0})^{T}{\Sigma}_{yx}\mathcal{L}_{(1)}-(x^{0})^{T}{\Sigma}_{xy}\mathcal{L}_{(2)}+(x^{0})^{T}(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,xy}-{\Sigma}_{xy})y^{0}-(\widehat{x}_{n}-x^{0})^{T}\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,xy}(\widehat{y}_{n}-y^{0})+o_{p}(n^{-1/2}).

(63)

We will show that the
quadratic term is also
o_{p}(n^{-1/2})
. To that end, notice that

\displaystyle\absolutevalue{(\hx-x^0)^T\hSxy(\hy-y^0)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\ \absolutevalue{(\hx-x^0)^T(\hSxy-\Sxy)(\hy-y^0)}+\absolutevalue{(\hx-x^0)^T\Sxy(\hy-y^0)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\ \|\widehat{x}_{n}-x^{0}\|_{1}|\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,xy}-{\Sigma}_{xy}|_{\infty}\|\widehat{y}_{n}-y^{0}\|_{1}+M\|\widehat{x}_{n}-x^{0}\|_{2}\|\widehat{y}_{n}-y^{0}\|_{2}

by Assumption
2.1
. From Lemma
13.2
and Lemma
13.9
it follows that

\|\widehat{x}_{n}-x^{0}\|_{1}|\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,xy}-{\Sigma}_{xy}|_{\infty}\|\widehat{y}_{n}-y^{0}\|_{1}=O_{p}(s^{2\kappa+1}\lambda^{3}).

By Fact
1
,
s\lambda\to 0
. Therefore,
s^{2\kappa+1}\lambda^{3}=o_{p}(s^{2\kappa}\lambda^{2})
. Therefore,

\|\widehat{x}_{n}-x^{0}\|_{1}|\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,xy}-{\Sigma}_{xy}|_{\infty}\|\widehat{y}_{n}-y^{0}\|_{1}=o_{p}(s^{2\kappa}\lambda^{2}).

Lemma
13.2
implies, on the other hand, that

\|\widehat{x}_{n}-x^{0}\|_{2}\|\widehat{y}_{n}-y^{0}\|_{2}=O_{p}(s^{2\kappa}\lambda^{2}).

Since

s^{2\kappa}\lambda^{2}\leq s^{2\kappa+1/2}\lambda^{2}=o(n^{-1/2}),

it follows that

\absolutevalue{(\hx-x^0)^T\hSxy(\hy-y^0)}=o_{p}(n^{-1/2}).

Now
(
18.1
) indicates that

\displaystyle\widehat{x}_{n}^{T}\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,xy}\widehat{y}^{db}_{n}+(\widehat{x}^{db}_{n})^{T}\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,xy}\widehat{y}_{n}-\widehat{x}_{n}^{T}\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,xy}\widehat{y}_{n}-{\rho}_{0}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ -(x^{0})^{T}{\Sigma}_{xy}\mathcal{L}_{2}-(y^{0})^{T}{\Sigma}_{yx}\mathcal{L}_{1}+(x^{0})^{T}(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,xy}-{\Sigma}_{xy})y^{0}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\ {\rho}_{0}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{(Z_{i}-E[Z_{i}])}{n}

where

Z_{i}=-{\rho}_{0}(X_{i}^{T}x^{0})^{2}-{\rho}_{0}(Y_{i}^{T}y^{0})^{2}+2(X_{i}^{T}x^{0})(Y_{i}^{T}y^{0}).

Here (a) follows from Lemma
18.4
.
Note that
Z_{i}
’s are
n
independent copies of the random variable
Z=-{\rho}_{0}(X^{T}x^{0})^{2}-{\rho}_{0}(Y^{T}y^{0})^{2}+2(X^{T}x^{0})(Y^{T}y^{0})
.
If we can show that
EZ^{4}<\infty
, then
\xi
’s satisfy the Lyaponov’s condition
(
Billingsley 2008
, cf. Theorem 27.3 of )
, which leads to

\displaystyle\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n}(Z_{i}-E[Z_{i}])}{(n\text{var}(Z))^{1/2}}\to_{d}N(0,1).

(64)

Now note that

E[Z^{4}]\leq C(E[(X_{i}^{T}x^{0})^{8}]+E[(Y_{i}^{T}y^{0})^{8}])\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{\leq}}C(\|x^{0}\|^{8}_{2}+\|y^{0}\|^{8}_{2})

where (a) follows from (
45
).
Hence, by Lemma
13.7
,
E[Z^{4}]<\infty
. Thus (
64
) holds with
\sigma^{2}_{\rho}=\text{var}(Z)
. Hence, first part of the proof follows.

The second part of the proof will be devoted towards obtaining the form of
\sigma_{\rho}^{2}
when
X
and
Y
are multivariate Gaussian vectors.
To that end, we note that

\displaystyle\sigma^{2}_{\rho}=\text{var}(Z)=

\displaystyle\ {\rho}_{0}^{2}\text{var}((X^{T}x^{0})^{2})+{\rho}_{0}^{2}\text{var}((Y^{T}y^{0})^{2})+4\text{var}((X^{T}x^{0})(Y^{T}y^{0}))+2{\rho}_{0}^{2}\text{cov}((X^{T}x^{0})^{2},(Y^{T}y^{0})^{2})

\displaystyle\ -4{\rho}_{0}\text{cov}((X^{T}x^{0})^{2},(X^{T}x^{0})(Y^{T}y^{0}))-4{\rho}_{0}\text{cov}((Y^{T}y^{0})^{2},(X^{T}x^{0})(Y^{T}y^{0}))

Let us denote
\mathcal{X}_{1}=X^{T}x^{0}
and
\mathcal{X}_{2}=Y^{T}y^{0}
. Then we have

(\mathcal{X}_{1},\mathcal{X}_{2})\equiv(X^{T}x^{0},Y^{T}y^{0})\sim N_{2}\left(0,\begin{bmatrix}{\rho}_{0}&{\rho}_{0}^{2}\\
{\rho}_{0}^{2}&{\rho}_{0}\end{bmatrix}\right).

Since
\mathcal{X}_{1}^{2}\sim{\rho}_{0}\chi^{2}_{1}
, it follows that
\text{var}(\mathcal{X}_{1}^{2})=2{\rho}_{0}^{2}
. Similarly,
\text{var}(\mathcal{X}_{2}^{2})=2{\rho}_{0}^{2}
. On the other hand,

\mathcal{X}_{2}|\mathcal{X}_{1}\sim N({\rho}_{0}\mathcal{X}_{1},{\rho}_{0}(1-{\rho}_{0}^{2})).

Note that

Var(Z)=4{\rho}_{0}^{4}+4\text{var}(\mathcal{X}_{1}\mathcal{X}_{2})+2{\rho}_{0}^{2}\text{cov}(\mathcal{X}_{1}^{2},\mathcal{X}_{2}^{2})-4{\rho}_{0}\text{cov}(\mathcal{X}_{1}^{2}+\mathcal{X}_{2}^{2},\mathcal{X}_{1}\mathcal{X}_{2}).

Noting
E[\mathcal{X}_{1}^{2}\mathcal{X}_{2}^{2}]={\rho}_{0}^{2}+2{\rho}_{0}^{4}
by Fact
6
, we calculate

\text{cov}(\mathcal{X}_{1}^{2},\mathcal{X}_{2}^{2})=E[\mathcal{X}_{1}^{2}\mathcal{X}_{2}^{2}]-E[\mathcal{X}_{1}^{2}]E[\mathcal{X}_{2}^{2}]=({\rho}_{0}^{2}+2{\rho}_{0}^{4})-{\rho}_{0}^{2}=2{\rho}_{0}^{4}

and

\text{var}(\mathcal{X}_{1}\mathcal{X}_{2})=E[\mathcal{X}_{1}^{2}\mathcal{X}_{2}^{2}]-E[\mathcal{X}_{1}\mathcal{X}_{2}]^{2}={\rho}_{0}^{2}+2{\rho}_{0}^{4}-({\rho}_{0}^{2})^{2}={\rho}_{0}^{4}+{\rho}_{0}^{2}.

Fact
6
also implies
E[\mathcal{X}_{1}^{3}\mathcal{X}_{2}]=3{\rho}_{0}^{3}
, leading to

\text{cov}(\mathcal{X}_{1}^{2},\mathcal{X}_{1}\mathcal{X}_{2})=E[\mathcal{X}_{1}^{3}\mathcal{X}_{2}]-E[\mathcal{X}_{1}^{2}]E[\mathcal{X}_{1}\mathcal{X}_{2}]=3{\rho}_{0}^{3}-{\rho}_{0}^{3}=2{\rho}_{0}^{3}.

Then the proof follows noting

\displaystyle\text{var}(Z)=

\displaystyle\ 4{\rho}_{0}^{4}+(4{\rho}_{0}^{2}+4{\rho}_{0}^{4})+4{\rho}_{0}^{6}-16{\rho}_{0}^{4}=4{\rho}_{0}^{2}(1-2{\rho}_{0}^{2}+{\rho}_{0}^{4})=4{\rho}_{0}^{2}(1-{\rho}_{0}^{2})^{2}.

(65)

19
Proofs of Supplement
9

19.1
Proof of Theorem
9.1

We will prove the theorem only for
\widehat{\alpha}_{n}
because the proof for
\widehat{\beta}_{n}
will follow similarly. In particular, we will show that

\|\widehat{\alpha}_{n}-\alpha_{0}\|_{2}=\begin{cases}O_{p}(s_{U}^{1/2}\lambda)&\text{if }r=1\\
O_{p}(s_{U}\lambda)&\text{o.w.}\end{cases}

and

\|(\widehat{\alpha}_{n}-\alpha_{0})_{S_{U}}\|_{1}\leq s_{U}^{1/2}\|(\widehat{\alpha}_{n}-\alpha_{0})\|_{1}\quad\text{and}\quad\|(\widehat{\alpha}_{n}-\alpha_{0})_{S^{c}_{U}}\|_{1}=O_{p}(s_{U}\lambda).

First note that since
s\lambda\to 0
,
\lambda\to 0
. Then by (
4
),
\log(p+q)=o(n)
follows.
The proof of Theorem
9.1
hinges on Theorem
19.1
, which we will prove later this section. Theorem
19.1
collects the rate of
\|\widehat{F}_{n}-F_{0}\|_{F}
.

Theorem 19.1
.

Under the set-up of Theorem
9.1
,

\|\widehat{F}_{n}-F_{0}\|_{F}=O_{p}(s\lambda).

Theorem
19.1
is similar to that of Theorem 4.1 of
Gao et al. 2017
.

The importance of Theorem
19.1
will be clear very soon. Since
\widehat{\alpha}_{n}^{(0)}
and
\widehat{\beta}_{n}^{(0)}
are the first pair of singular vectors of
\widehat{F}_{n}
, we can find their rate of convergence to
\alpha_{0}
and
\beta_{0}
, respectively, from the rate of convergence of
\widehat{F}_{n}
using the Davis-Kahan sin
\theta
theorem.
We will use the version of Davis-Kahan Sin
\theta
theorem given by Theorem 4 of
Yu et al. 2015
because it is suited for general
p\times q
matrices. Since
\widehat{\beta}_{n}^{(0)}
and
\beta_{0}
are the respective left singular vectors of
\widehat{F}_{n}
and
F_{0}
, Theorem 4 of
Yu et al. 2015
entails that

\min_{s=\pm 1}\|s\widehat{\beta}_{n}^{(0)}-\beta_{0}\|_{2}\leq C(2+\|\widehat{F}_{n}-F_{0}\|_{F})\|\widehat{F}_{n}-F_{0}\|_{F},

where
C
is a universal constant. Under our Assumption
4.1
, Theorem
19.1
implies that
\|\widehat{F}_{n}-F_{0}\|_{F}
is
o_{p}(1)
, which indicates

\min_{s=\pm 1}\|s\widehat{\beta}_{n}^{(0)}-\beta_{0}\|_{2}\leq 3C\|\widehat{F}_{n}-F_{0}\|_{F}=O_{p}(s\lambda).

(66)

As a side result, we also obtain

\|\widehat{\beta}_{n}^{(0)}\|_{2}\leq M+o_{p}(1)

(67)

which follows from Lemma
13.7
since

|\|\widehat{\beta}_{n}^{(0)}\|_{2}-\|\beta_{0}\|_{2}|=o_{p}(1)
.

We define the quantity

u^{*}=U\Lambda V^{T}{\Sigma}_{y}\widehat{\beta}_{n}^{(0)}\quad\text{.}

(68)

Note that
u^{*}
is dependent, through
\widehat{\beta}_{n}^{(0)}
, only on the first part of the data. Thus
u^{*}
is independent of
\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,xy}^{(1)}
,
\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,x}^{(1)}
, and
\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,y}^{(1)}
because the above-mentioned matrices are computed from the second part of the data.

Now we will present some key lemmas which will be useful in proving Theorem
9.1
. The proof of these lemmas can be found in Subsection
21.2
.
We begin by noting some properties of
u^{*}
.

Lemma 19.2
.

Under the set up of Theorem
9.1
, the vector
u^{*}
defined in (
68
) satisfy

\absolutevalue{(\tg)^T\Sx\tg-\rhk^2}=o_{p}(1)

Moreover,

{\rho}_{0}/(2M^{1/2})\leq\|u^{*}\|_{2}\leq 2{\rho}_{0}M^{1/2},

where
M
is as in Assumption
2.1
.

Our next lemma establishes that
\widehat{\alpha}_{n}=(\tilde{x}_{n}^{T}{\Sigma}_{x}\tilde{x}_{n})^{-1/2}\tilde{x}_{n}
with high probability for large
n
.

Lemma 19.3
.

Under the set up of Theorem
9.1
,

\displaystyle\absolutevalue{\tx^T(\hSx^{(1)}-\Sx)\tx}=O_{p}(s_{U}^{1/2}\lambda),

(69)

where
\tilde{x}_{n}
is as in Algorithm
1
.
Also, the
\widehat{\alpha}_{n}
defined in (
21
) satisfies

\displaystyle P\bigg(\widehat{\alpha}_{n}=(\tilde{x}_{n}^{T}\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,x}\tilde{x}_{n})^{-1/2}\tilde{x}_{n}\bigg)\to 1\quad\text{ as }n\to\infty,

(70)

and

\displaystyle\|\widehat{\alpha}_{n}-(\tilde{x}_{n}^{T}{\Sigma}_{x}\tilde{x}_{n})^{-1/2}\tilde{x}_{n}\|_{2}=O_{p}(s_{U}^{1/2}\lambda).

(71)

The next lemma will be essential in bounding
\inf_{w\in\{\pm 1\}}\|w\widehat{\alpha}_{n}-\alpha_{0}\|_{2}
.

Lemma 19.4
.

Consider the set up of of Theorem
9.1
.
Let us denote
\tilde{U}={\Sigma}_{x}^{1/2}U
and
\tilde{V}={\Sigma}_{y}^{1/2}V
. Suppose
x\in\mathbb{R}^{p}
has unit norm and
y\in\mathbb{R}^{q}
.

A.

If the rank of
\Lambda
, i.e.
r=1
, then

\|P_{x}-P_{\tilde{u}_{1}}\|_{F}\leq\|P_{x}-P_{\tilde{U}\Lambda(\tilde{V})^{T}y}\|_{F}.

B.

Suppose in addition,
\inf_{w\in\{\pm 1\}}\|wy-\tilde{v}_{1}\|_{2}=O_{p}(s\lambda)
. Then for
r>1
,

\|P_{x}-P_{\tilde{u}_{1}}\|_{F}\leq 5\|P_{x}-P_{\tilde{U}\Lambda(\tilde{V})^{T}y}\|_{F}+O_{p}(s\lambda).

Now that we have collected all the tools necessary, we are ready to start the main proof.

Proof 19.5
(of Theorem
9.1
).

We denote
\Delta=\tilde{x}_{n}-u^{*}
. In the first step of the proof, we show that
\|\Delta\|_{2}
is small. The second step is devoted towards showing that if
\|\Delta\|_{2}
is small, then
\inf_{w\in\pm 1}\|w\widehat{\alpha}_{n}-\alpha_{0}\|_{2}
is negligible as well.

In the first step, we begin by deriving a bound on
tr(\Delta^{T}\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,x}^{(1)}\Delta)
.
First, since
\tilde{x}_{n}
is a solution to (
20
), we have

\tilde{x}_{n}^{T}\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,x}^{(1)}\tilde{x}_{n}-2\tilde{x}_{n}^{T}\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,xy}^{(1)}\widehat{\beta}_{n}^{(0)}+\lambda_{2}\|\tilde{x}_{n}\|_{1}\leq(u^{*})^{T}\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,x}^{(1)}u^{*}-2(u^{*})^{T}\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,xy}^{(1)}\widehat{\beta}_{n}^{(0)}+\lambda_{2}\|u^{*}\|_{1}.

Rearranging the terms give

\displaystyle\Delta^{T}\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,x}^{(1)}\Delta\leq

\displaystyle\ \lambda_{2}(\|u^{*}\|_{1}-\|\tilde{x}_{n}\|_{1})+2\Delta^{T}\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,xy}^{(1)}\widehat{\beta}_{n}^{(0)}+2(u^{*})^{T}(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,x}^{(1)}u^{*}-\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,x}^{(1)}\tilde{x}_{n})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \lambda_{2}(\underbrace{\|u^{*}\|_{1}-\|\tilde{x}_{n}\|_{1}}_{T_{1}})-\underbrace{2\Delta^{T}(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,x}^{(1)}u^{*}-\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,xy}^{(1)}\widehat{\beta}_{n}^{(0)})}_{T_{2}}.

(72)

For the second term
T_{2}
, using the definition of
u^{*}
, we have

{\Sigma}_{x}u^{*}={\Sigma}_{x}U\Lambda V^{T}{\Sigma}_{y}\widehat{\beta}_{n}^{(0)}={\Sigma}_{xy}\widehat{\beta}_{n}^{(0)},

which implies

\displaystyle|T_{2}/2|=|\Delta^{T}(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,x}^{(1)}u^{*}-\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,xy}^{(1)}\widehat{\beta}_{n}^{(0)})|\leq

\displaystyle\ \|\Delta\|_{1}\|\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,x}^{(1)}u^{*}-{\Sigma}_{x}u^{*}\|_{\infty}+\|\Delta\|_{1}\|({\Sigma}_{xy}-\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,xy}^{(1)})\widehat{\beta}_{n}^{(0)}\|_{\infty}.

(73)

Because
\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,x}^{(1)}
and
u^{*}
are independent,
Lemma
13.9
can be applied to show that

\|\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,x}^{(1)}u^{*}-{\Sigma}_{x}u^{*}\|_{\infty}=O_{p}(\|u^{*}\|_{2}\lambda),

(74)

which is
O_{p}(\lambda)
because
\|u^{*}\|_{2}=O_{p}(1)
by Lemma
19.2
.
Similarly, because
\widehat{\beta}_{n}^{(0)}
is independent of
\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,xy}^{(1)},
using Lemma
13.9
again, we can show that

\|({\Sigma}_{xy}-\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,xy}^{(1)})\widehat{\beta}_{n}^{(0)}\|_{\infty}=\|\widehat{\beta}_{n}^{(0)}\|_{2}O_{p}(\lambda)

which is
O_{p}(\lambda)
because
by (
67
),
\|\widehat{\beta}_{n}^{(0)}\|_{2}=O_{p}(1)
.
The above, in conjunction with (
73
) and (
74
) imply that there exists
C>0
such that

\displaystyle|T_{2}|\leq C_{2}\|\Delta\|_{1}\lambda

(75)

with high probability.

Recalling that we denoted
S_{U}
to be the indices of the non-zero rows of
U
, we note

u^{*}_{S_{U}^{c}}=(U\Lambda V^{T}{\Sigma}_{y}\widehat{\beta}_{n}^{(0)})_{S_{U}^{c}}=U_{S_{U}^{c}}\Lambda V^{T}{\Sigma}_{y}\widehat{\beta}_{n}^{(0)}=0.

Therefore, the support of
u^{*}
is not larger than
S_{U}
.
For
T_{1}
, it thus follows that

\|u^{*}\|_{1}-\|\tilde{x}_{n}\|_{1}=\|u^{*}_{S_{U}}\|_{1}-\|u^{*}_{S_{U}}+\Delta_{S_{U}}\|_{1}-\|\Delta_{S_{U}^{c}}\|_{1}\leq\|\Delta_{S_{U}}\|_{1}-\|\Delta_{S_{U}^{c}}\|_{1}.

Noting
\lambda_{2}=C\lambda
, we choose
C>C_{2}
. Then we have with probability tending to one,

\displaystyle\Delta^{T}\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,x}^{(1)}\Delta\leq

\displaystyle\ C\lambda\Big(\|\Delta_{S_{U}}\|_{1}-\|\Delta_{S_{U}^{c}}\|_{1}\Big)+C_{2}\lambda\|\Delta\|_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ C_{2}\lambda\bigg\{C/C_{2}\Big(\|\Delta_{S_{U}}\|_{1}-\|\Delta_{S_{U}^{c}}\|_{1}\Big)+\|\Delta_{S_{U}}\|_{1}+\|\Delta_{S_{U}^{c}}\|_{1}\bigg\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle C_{2}\lambda\bigg\{(1+C/C_{2})\|\Delta_{S_{U}}\|_{1}-(C/C_{2}-1)\|\Delta_{S_{U}^{c}}\|_{1}\bigg\}.

Recalling we chose
C>C_{2}
, we have
C/C_{2}-1>0
. There are some important consequences of the above inequality. First, because
\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,x}^{(1)}
is non-negative definite, we obtain the cone condition

\|\Delta_{S_{U}^{c}}\|_{1}\leq\frac{C/C_{2}+1}{C/C_{2}-1}\|\Delta_{S_{U}}\|_{1}.

(76)

Second, we derive

\Delta^{T}\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,x}^{(1)}\Delta\leq(C_{2}+C)\lambda\|\Delta_{S_{U}}\|_{1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{\leq}}(C_{2}+C)s_{U}^{1/2}\lambda\|\Delta_{S_{U}}\|_{2}

(77)

where (a) follows by Cauchy Schwarz inequality.
Now we will show that the bound on
\Delta^{T}\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,x}^{(1)}\Delta
induces a bound on
\|\Delta\|_{2}
.

Let
I_{1}=\{i_{1},\ldots,i_{t}\}
be the index set of the
t
elements with largest absolute values in
S_{U}^{c}
. Let us denote
\tilde{S}_{U}=S_{U}\cup I_{1}
.
Note that

\displaystyle\Delta^{T}\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,x}^{(1)}\Delta\geq\Delta_{\tilde{S}_{U}}^{T}\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,x}^{(1)}\Delta_{\tilde{S}_{U}}-\Delta_{\tilde{S}_{U}^{c}}^{T}\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,x}^{(1)}\Delta_{\tilde{S}_{U}^{c}}.

Lemma 6.5 of
Gao et al. 2017
implies that

\Delta_{\tilde{S}_{U}}^{T}\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,x}^{(1)}\Delta_{\tilde{S}_{U}}\geq(M^{-1}-C((s_{U}+t)\log p/n)^{1/2})\|\Delta_{\tilde{S}_{U}}\|_{2}^{2},

and

\Delta_{\tilde{S}_{U}}^{T}\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,x}^{(1)}\Delta_{\tilde{S}_{U}^{c}}\leq(M+C(s_{U}\log p/n)^{1/2})\|\Delta_{\tilde{S}_{U}^{c}}\|_{2}^{2}.

Note that
s_{U}\log p/n=o_{p}(1)
because
s_{U}\lambda\to 0
.
Now using the cone condition (
76
), and
proceeding like the Step 2 of the proof of Theorem 4.2 of
Gao et al. 2017
, we can show that

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{\tilde{S}_{U}^{c}}\|_{2}\leq 3\frac{s_{U}}{t}\|\Delta_{\tilde{S}_{U}}\|_{2}.

(78)

Since the proof is identical to that of the Step 2 of Theorem 4.2 of
Gao et al. 2017
, it is skipped.
When
s_{U}=o(p)
, we can take
t=c_{1}s_{U}
where
c_{1}
is a large constant. Then
\|\Delta_{\tilde{S}_{U}^{c}}\|_{2}\leq 3\|\Delta_{\tilde{S}_{U}}\|_{2}/c_{1}
. Therefore, combining all the pieces above give us

\|\Delta_{\tilde{S}_{U}}\|_{2}^{2}(M^{-1}-9M/c_{1}^{2}+o_{p}(1))\leq\Delta^{T}\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,x}^{(1)}\Delta.

When
c_{1}>3M
, the multiplicative constant with
\|\Delta_{\tilde{S}_{U}}\|_{2}^{2}
is positive.
Therefore, using (
77
) we obtain the following inequality:

\|\Delta_{\tilde{S}_{U}}\|_{2}^{2}=O_{p}(s_{U}^{1/2}\lambda)\|\Delta_{S_{U}}\|_{2}.

Because
S_{U}\subset\tilde{S}_{U}
, the above implies
\|\Delta_{\tilde{S}_{U}}\|_{2}^{2}\leq O_{p}(s_{U}^{1/2}\lambda)\|\Delta_{\tilde{S}_{U}}\|_{2}
,
which entails
\|\Delta_{\tilde{S}_{U}}\|_{2}
is
O_{p}(s_{U}^{1/2}\lambda)
.
Finally, (
78
) and the fact that
t=c_{1}s_{u}
implies
\|\Delta_{\tilde{S}^{c}_{U}}\|_{2}
is also
O_{p}(s_{U}^{1/2}\lambda)
. Since
\|\Delta\|_{2}^{2}
equals
\|\Delta_{\tilde{S}_{U}}\|_{2}^{2}+\|\Delta_{\tilde{S}^{c}_{U}}\|_{2}^{2}
, we have

\displaystyle\|\Delta\|_{2}=\|\tilde{x}_{n}-u^{*}\|_{2}=O_{p}((s_{U}\log(p+q)/n)^{1/2}).

(79)

Now we are ready to compute the rate of
\inf_{w\in\{\pm 1\}}\|w\widehat{\alpha}_{n}-\alpha_{0}\|_{2}
. To this end, note that

\displaystyle\inf_{w\in\{\pm 1\}}\|w\widehat{\alpha}_{n}-\alpha_{0}\|_{2}\leq

\displaystyle\underbrace{\|\widehat{\alpha}_{n}-(\tilde{x}_{n}^{T}{\Sigma}_{x}\tilde{x}_{n})^{-1/2}\tilde{x}_{n}\|_{2}}_{T_{1}}+\underbrace{\inf_{w\in\{\pm 1\}}\|w(\tilde{x}_{n}^{T}{\Sigma}_{x}\tilde{x}_{n})^{-1/2}\tilde{x}_{n}-\alpha_{0}\|_{2}}_{T_{2}}

(80)

Lemma
19.3
shows that
T_{1}=O_{p}(s_{U}^{1/2}\lambda)
.
To control the term
T_{2}
, first note that

T_{2}\leq M^{1/2}\inf_{w\in\{\pm 1\}}\|w(\tilde{x}_{n}^{T}{\Sigma}_{x}\tilde{x}_{n})^{-1/2}{\Sigma}_{x}^{1/2}\tilde{x}_{n}-{\Sigma}_{x}^{1/2}\alpha_{0}\|_{2}.

(81)

Since the normalized vectors
a=(\tilde{x}_{n}^{T}{\Sigma}_{x}\tilde{x}_{n})^{-1/2}{\Sigma}_{x}^{1/2}\tilde{x}_{n}
and
\tilde{u}_{1}={\Sigma}_{x}^{1/2}\alpha_{0}
have unit norm, they are easier to work with than
(\tilde{x}_{n}^{T}{\Sigma}_{x}\tilde{x}_{n})^{-1/2}\tilde{x}_{n}
and
\alpha_{0}
. By Fact
4
, we then obtain that

\inf_{w\in\{\pm 1\}}\|wa-\tilde{u}_{1}\|_{2}^{2}\leq\|P_{a}-P_{\tilde{u}_{1}}\|_{F}^{2}.

(82)

We will now use Lemma
19.4
to bound
\|P_{a}-P_{\tilde{u}_{1}}\|_{F}^{2}
, and we will see that the rate of this term depends on the rank
r
.
Before applying Lemma
19.4
, we notice (
66
) and Assumption
2.1
imply

\|{\Sigma}_{y}^{1/2}(w\widehat{\beta}_{n}^{(0)}-\beta_{0})\|_{2}\leq M^{1/2}O_{p}(s\lambda).

Therefore, we can take the
y
in Lemma
19.4
to be
{\Sigma}_{y}^{1/2}\widehat{\beta}_{n}^{(0)}
.

We first consider the case when
r=1
. An application of Lemma
19.4
with
x=a
and
y={\Sigma}_{y}^{1/2}\widehat{\beta}_{n}^{(0)}
then yields

\|P_{a}-P_{\tilde{u}_{1}}\|_{F}^{2}\leq\|P_{a}-P_{{\Sigma}_{x}^{1/2}U\Lambda V^{T}{\Sigma}_{y}\widehat{\beta}_{n}^{(0)}}\|_{F}^{2}.

However,

\|P_{a}-P_{{\Sigma}_{x}^{1/2}U\Lambda V^{T}{\Sigma}_{y}\widehat{\beta}_{n}^{(0)}}\|_{F}^{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{\leq}}\|a-{\Sigma}_{x}^{1/2}U\Lambda V^{T}{\Sigma}_{y}\widehat{\beta}_{n}^{(0)}\|_{2}^{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(b)}}{{\leq}}M\|\tilde{x}_{n}-u^{*}\|_{2}^{2},

where (a) follows from Fact
4
and (b) follows from the definition of
a
,
u^{*}
, and Assumption
2.1
. The term
\|\tilde{x}_{n}-u^{*}\|_{2}^{2}

is
O_{p}(s_{U}\lambda^{2})
by (
79
).
Hence, (
81
) and (
82
) imply that when
r=1
,

T_{2}=O_{p}(s_{U}^{1/2}\lambda)
. Then (
80
) and Lemma
19.3
entail that for
r=1
,
\inf_{w\in\{\pm 1\}}\|w\widehat{\alpha}_{n}-\alpha_{0}\|_{2}=O_{p}(s_{U}^{1/2}\lambda)
.

Now consider
r>2
. Proceeding like the previous case, we apply Lemma
19.4
with
x=a
and
y={\Sigma}_{y}^{1/2}\widehat{\beta}_{n}^{(0)}
to obtain

\|P_{a}-P_{\tilde{u}_{1}}\|_{F}\leq 5\|P_{a}-P_{{\Sigma}_{x}^{1/2}U\Lambda V^{T}{\Sigma}_{y}\widehat{\beta}_{n}^{(0)}}\|_{F}+O_{p}(s\lambda).

Since we just showed that

\|P_{a}-P_{{\Sigma}_{x}^{1/2}U\Lambda V^{T}{\Sigma}_{y}\widehat{\beta}_{n}^{(0)}}\|_{F}=O_{p}(s_{U}^{1/2}\lambda),

the above implies
\|P_{a}-P_{\tilde{u}_{1}}\|_{F}=O_{p}(s_{U}\lambda)
.

To infer on the
l_{1}
error, first observe that
u^{*}_{S^{c}_{U}}=U_{S^{c}_{U}}\Lambda V^{T}{\Sigma}_{y}\widehat{\beta}_{n}^{(0)}=0
, where
S_{U}
is denotes set of indexes of the non-zero rows in
U
. Also,
(\alpha_{0})_{S_{U}^{c}}=0
because
\alpha_{0}
is the first column of
U
.
By Lemma
19.3
, we also have
\widehat{\alpha}_{n}=\tilde{x}_{n}(\tilde{x}_{n}^{T}(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,x}^{(1)})\tilde{x}_{n})^{-1/2}
with probability tending to one.
Therefore, with probability tending to one,

\|(\widehat{\alpha}_{n}-\alpha_{0})_{S_{U}^{c}}\|_{1}=\|(\widehat{\alpha}_{n})_{S_{U}^{c}}\|_{1}=(\tilde{x}_{n}^{T}(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,x}^{(1)})\tilde{x}_{n})^{-1/2}\|(\tilde{x}_{n})_{S_{U}^{c}}\|_{1}=(\tilde{x}_{n}^{T}(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,x}^{(1)})\tilde{x}_{n})^{-1/2}\|(\tilde{x}_{n}-u^{*})_{S_{U}^{c}}\|_{1}.

Observe that (
76
) implies there exists
c>0
so that

\|(\tilde{x}_{n}-u^{*})_{S_{U}^{c}}\|_{1}=\|\Delta_{S_{U}^{c}}\|_{1}\leq c\|\Delta_{S_{U}}\|_{1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{\leq}}cS_{U}^{1/2}\|\Delta_{S_{U}}\|_{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(b)}}{{=}}O_{p}(s_{U}\lambda),

where (a) follows by Cauchy-Schwarz inequality and (b) follows because
\|\Delta\|_{2}=O_{p}(S_{U}^{1/2}\lambda)
by (
79
). Moreover, equation
116
of Lemma
19.3
implies
(\tilde{x}_{n}^{T}(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,x}^{(1)})\tilde{x}_{n})^{-1/2}=O_{p}(1)
. Therefore,

\|(\widehat{\alpha}_{n}-\alpha_{0})_{s_{U}^{c}}\|_{1}=O_{p}(s_{U}\lambda).

Also, by Cauchy-Schwarz inequality,

\|(\widehat{\alpha}_{n}-\alpha_{0})_{S_{U}}\|_{1}\leq\sqrt{s_{U}}\|(\widehat{\alpha}_{n}-\alpha_{0})_{S_{U}}\|_{2}.

Hence, the proof follows.

19.2
Proof of Theorem
19.1

We begin by introducing some new notations. Let us define

\overline{U}=U(U^{T}\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,x}^{(0)}U)^{-1/2}\quad\text{and}\quad\overline{V}=V(V^{T}\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,y}^{(0)}V)^{-1/2},

(83)

and denote
\overline{\alpha}=\overline{U}_{1}
and
\overline{\beta}=\overline{V}_{1}
.
Several times we will use without stating the fact that
\overline{\alpha}^{T}\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,x}^{(0)}\overline{\alpha}=\overline{\beta}^{T}\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,y}^{(0)}\overline{\beta}=1
.
We also denote

\overline{\Lambda}=(U^{T}\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,x}^{(0)}U)^{1/2}\Lambda(V^{T}\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,y}^{(0)}V)^{1/2}

(84)

and
\tilde{F}_{n}=\overline{\alpha}\overline{\beta}^{T}
. For notational convenience, we define

\epsilon_{n,u}=n^{-1/2}\bigg(s+\log\frac{ep}{s_{x}}\bigg)^{1/2},\quad\epsilon_{n,v}=n^{-1/2}\bigg(s+\log\frac{eq}{s_{y}}\bigg)^{1/2}.

We denote

\tilde{\Sigma}_{x,y}=\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,x}^{(0)}U\Lambda V^{T}\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,y}^{(0)}.

(85)

Finally, let
\Delta^{(F)}=\widehat{F}_{n}-\tilde{F}_{n}
.

Now we state some lemmas which will be required for the proof of Theorem
19.1
. These lemmas are proved in Subsection
21.3
.
The first lemma shows that
\tilde{F}_{n}
is a good approximation of
F_{0}
because
\|\tilde{F}_{n}-F_{0}\|_{F}
is small.

Lemma 19.6
.

Under the set up of Theorem
19.1
,

\|\tilde{F}_{n}-F_{0}\|_{F}=O_{p}(\epsilon_{n,u}+\epsilon_{n,v})

The next Lemma shows that
\tilde{F}_{n}=\overline{\alpha}\overline{\beta}^{T}
is a feasible solution to the step 1 optimization problem in Algorithm
19
.

Lemma 19.7
.

When
\tilde{F}_{n}
exists,

\|(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,x}^{(0)})^{1/2}\tilde{F}_{n}(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,y}^{(0)})^{1/2}\|_{*}=1\quad\text{ and }\quad\|(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,x}^{(0)})^{1/2}\tilde{F}_{n}(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,y}^{(0)})^{1/2}\|_{op}=1.

The next lemma exploits the convexity of the unpenalized version of (
19
) at
F_{0}
and establishes a strong convexity type result at
F_{0}
.

Lemma 19.8
.

Let
A\in\mathcal{O}(p,r)
and
G\in\mathcal{O}(q,r)
. Suppose
\tilde{D}\in\mathbb{R}^{r\times r}
and
D
are two diagonal matrices in
\mathbb{R}^{r\times r}
which diagonal entries
D_{11}\geq D_{22},\ldots,\geq D_{rr}\geq 0
. Further suppose
d_{12}=D_{11}-D_{22}>0
. Let
E=e_{1}e_{1}^{T}
. If
F
satisfies
\|F\|_{op}\leq 1
and
\|F\|_{*}\leq 1
, then

\langle A\tilde{D}G^{T},AEG^{T}-F\rangle\geq\frac{d_{12}}{2}\|AEG^{T}-F\|_{F}^{2}-\|\tilde{D}-D\|_{F}\|AEG^{T}-F\|_{F}.

Now we are ready to start the main proof.

Proof 19.9
(of Theorem
19.1
).

Lemma
19.7
implies that
\tilde{F}_{n}\in\mathcal{G}
, that is
\tilde{F}_{n}
is a feasible solution of (
19
). Therefore,

\langle\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,yx}^{(0)},\tilde{F}_{n}\rangle-\lambda_{1}\|\tilde{F}_{n}\|_{1}\leq\langle\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,yx}^{(0)},\widehat{F}_{n}\rangle-\lambda_{1}\|\widehat{F}_{n}\|_{1},

which leads to

\displaystyle-\langle\tilde{\Sigma}_{x,y},\Delta^{(F)}\rangle\leq\lambda_{1}(\|\tilde{F}_{n}\|_{1}-\|\tilde{F}_{n}+\Delta^{(F)}\|_{1})+\langle\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,xy}^{(0)}-\tilde{\Sigma}_{x,y},\Delta^{(F)}\rangle,

(86)

where
\tilde{\Sigma}_{x,y}
is as defined in (
85
). Observe that

\displaystyle\|\tilde{F}_{n}\|_{1}-\|\tilde{F}_{n}+\Delta^{(F)}\|_{1}=\|(\overline{\alpha})_{S_{x}}(\overline{\beta})_{S_{y}}^{T}\|_{1}-\|(\overline{\alpha})_{S_{x}}(\overline{\beta})_{S_{y}}^{T}+\Delta_{S_{x},S_{y}}^{(F)}\|_{1}-\|\Delta^{(F)}_{(S_{x},S_{y})^{c}}\|_{1}

where
\Delta^{(F)}_{S_{u},S_{v}}=(\Delta^{(F)}_{i,j})_{i\in S_{x},j\in S_{y}}
and
\Delta^{(F)}_{(S_{u},S_{v})^{c}}=(\Delta^{(F)}_{i,j})_{(i,j)\in(S_{x}\times S_{y})^{c}}
.
Note that

\displaystyle\|(\overline{\alpha})_{S_{x}}(\overline{\beta})_{S_{y}}^{T}\|_{1}-\|(\overline{\alpha})_{S_{x}}(\overline{\beta})_{S_{y}}^{T}+\Delta_{S_{x},S_{y}}^{(F)}\|_{1}-\|\Delta^{(F)}_{(S_{x},S_{y})^{c}}\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\ \|\Delta_{S_{x},S_{y}}^{(F)}\|_{1}-\|\Delta^{(F)}_{(S_{x},S_{y})^{c}}\|_{1}.

On the other hand, the second term on the right hand side of (
86
) satisfies

\langle\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,xy}^{(0)}-\tilde{\Sigma}_{x,y},\Delta^{(F)}\rangle\leq|\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,xy}^{(0)}-\tilde{\Sigma}_{x,y}|_{\infty}\|\Delta^{(F)}\|_{1}.

Therefore, for
\lambda_{1}\geq 2|(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,xy}^{(0)})-\tilde{\Sigma}_{x,y}|_{\infty}
, (
86
) implies that

\displaystyle-\langle\tilde{\Sigma}_{x,y},\Delta^{(F)}\rangle\leq\frac{3\lambda_{1}}{2}\|\Delta_{S_{x}S_{y}}\|_{1}-\frac{\lambda_{1}}{2}\|\Delta_{(S_{x}S_{y})^{c}}\|_{1}.

(87)

We will provide a lower bound on
-\langle\tilde{\Sigma}_{x,y},\Delta^{(F)}\rangle
using Lemma
19.8
.
Denoting
E=e_{1}e_{1}^{T}
and
{\delta=\|\tilde{\Lambda}-\Lambda\|_{F}}
, we obtain

\displaystyle-\langle\tilde{\Sigma}_{x,y},\Delta^{(F)}\rangle=

\displaystyle\ \langle(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,x}^{(0)})^{1/2}U\Lambda V^{T}(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,y}^{(0)})^{1/2},(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,x}^{(0)})^{1/2}(\tilde{F}_{n}-\widehat{F}_{n})(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,y}^{(0)})^{1/2}\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \langle(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,x}^{(0)})^{1/2}\overline{U}\overline{\Lambda}\overline{V}^{T}(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,y}^{(0)})^{1/2},(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,x}^{(0)})^{1/2}(\overline{U}E\overline{V}^{T}-\widehat{F}_{n})(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,y}^{(0)})^{1/2}\rangle

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{\geq}}

\displaystyle\ \frac{\Lambda_{1}-\Lambda_{2}}{2}\|(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,x}^{(0)})^{1/2}(\tilde{F}_{n}-\widehat{F}_{n})(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,y}^{(0)})^{1/2}\|_{F}^{2}-\delta\|(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,x}^{(0)})^{1/2}(\tilde{F}_{n}-\widehat{F}_{n})(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,y}^{(0)})^{1/2}\|_{F}

(88)

where (a) follows by Lemma
19.8
.
Here we used the fact that
d_{12}=\Lambda_{1}-\Lambda_{2}>0
by the Assumption
2.1
.
Now Fact
3
implies
\delta\leq\sqrt{r}\|\tilde{\Lambda}-\Lambda\|_{op}
, but Lemma 6.1 of
Gao et al. 2017
entails that
\|\tilde{\Lambda}-\Lambda\|_{op}=O_{p}(s^{1/2}\lambda)
.
Because
r
is less than the number of non-zero rows in
U
, and the number of non-zero rows is
s_{U}\leq s
, we can say
\delta=O_{p}(s\lambda)
.

Combining the upper and lower bounds derived in (
87
) and (
19.9
), and denoting
\Lambda_{1}-\Lambda_{2}
by
d_{12}
, we obtain that

\displaystyle d_{12}\|(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,x}^{(0)})^{1/2}\Delta^{(F)}(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,y}^{(0)})^{1/2}\|_{F}^{2}\leq 3\lambda_{1}\|\Delta_{S_{U}S_{V}}\|_{1}-\lambda_{1}\|\Delta_{(S_{U}S_{V})^{c}}\|_{1}+2\delta\|(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,x}^{(0)})^{1/2}\Delta^{(F)}(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,y}^{(0)})^{1/2}\|_{F}

(89)

which leads to

\displaystyle d_{12}\|(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,x}^{(0)})^{1/2}\Delta^{(F)}(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,y}^{(0)})^{1/2}\|_{F}^{2}\leq 3\lambda_{1}\|\Delta_{S_{U}S_{V}}\|_{1}+2\delta\|(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,x}^{(0)})^{1/2}\Delta^{(F)}(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,y}^{(0)})^{1/2}\|_{F}.

(90)

Solving the quadratic equation (see equation 53 of
Gao et al. 2017
) gives

\|(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,x}^{(0)})^{1/2}\Delta^{(F)}(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,y}^{(0)})^{1/2}\|_{F}^{2}\leq 6\lambda_{1}\|\Delta_{S_{U}S_{V}}\|_{1}/d_{12}+4\delta^{2}/d_{12}^{2}.

(91)

We derive two conclusions from (
91
). First, using
\|\Delta_{S_{U}S_{V}}\|_{1}\leq\sqrt{s_{x}s_{y}}\|\Delta_{S_{U}S_{V}}\|_{F}
, we derive

\|(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,x}^{(0)})^{1/2}\Delta^{(F)}(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,y}^{(0)})^{1/2}\|_{F}^{2}\leq 6\lambda_{1}\sqrt{s_{U}s_{V}}\|\Delta_{S_{U}S_{V}}\|_{F}/d_{12}+4\delta^{2}/d_{12}^{2}.

(92)

Second, noting
ax^{2}-bx
achieves minima at
x=b/(2a)
, we obtain that

d_{12}\|(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,x}^{(0)})^{1/2}\Delta^{(F)}(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,y}^{(0)})^{1/2}\|_{F}^{2}-2\delta\|(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,x}^{(0)})^{1/2}\Delta^{(F)}(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,y}^{(0)})^{1/2}\|_{F}\geq-\frac{\delta^{2}}{d_{12}}.

Therefore, (
89
) yields the generalized cone condition

0\leq 3\|\Delta_{S_{U}S_{V}}\|_{1}-\|\Delta_{(S_{U}S_{V})^{c}}\|_{1}+\frac{\delta^{2}}{\lambda_{1}d_{12}}.

(93)

Although the constants in our inequality (
93
) are a little bit sharper than the cone condition inequality (56) of
Gao et al. 2017
, both cone conditions are equivalent. By Lemma
13.9
, we have

|(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,xy}^{(0)})-{\Sigma}_{xy}|_{\infty}\leq C_{1}\lambda.

Let us set
\lambda_{1}=C\lambda
with
C\geq C_{1}
. The rest of the proof follows from step 2 of the proof of Theorem 4.1 in
Gao et al. 2017
, which indicates that for this choice of
C
, when (
92
) and (
93
) hold, and
\delta=O_{p}(s\lambda)
, there exists
C^{\prime}>0
so that

\displaystyle\|\Delta^{(F)}\|_{F}\leq C^{\prime}(s_{U}s_{V})^{1/2}\lambda_{1}/d_{12}.

(94)

with high probability. The proof of the current theorem follows combining (
94
) and Lemma
19.6
.

20
Proof of Theorem
10.3

20.1
Proof of the main theorem

The proof relies on the proximity of
\widehat{x}_{n}

and
\widehat{y}_{n}
to
x^{0}
and
y^{0}
, respectively.
Note that Lemma
13.2
imply
\|\widehat{x}_{n}\|_{i}=\|x^{0}\|_{i}+o_{p}(1)
and
\|\widehat{y}_{n}\|_{i}=\|y^{0}\|_{i}+o_{p}(1)

(i=1,2)

because
s^{\kappa+1/2}\lambda=o(1)
by Fact
1
. These facts will be used often times in proving our lemmas and claims.

We will begin by introducing some notations and stating some lemmas.
First we state a lemma that gives bound on the maximum and minimum eigenvalues of
\Phi^{0}
and
H^{0}
. The proof can be found in Subsection
21.4
.

Lemma 20.1
.

Under Assumption
2.1
and Assumption
2.1
,

\displaystyle\Lambda_{max}(H^{0})\leq 8{\rho}_{0}M,

\displaystyle\Lambda_{max}(\Phi^{0})\leq 2^{-1}M/({\rho}_{0}-\Lambda_{2}),

\displaystyle\Lambda_{min}(H^{0})\geq 2({\rho}_{0}-\Lambda_{2})/M

\displaystyle\Lambda_{min}(\Phi^{0})\geq(8{\rho}_{0}M)^{-1}

\displaystyle\tau_{j}^{2}\geq 2({\rho}_{0}-\Lambda_{2})/M.

where

\tau_{j}^{0}
is as defined in (
29
).

Recall the term
\eta_{j}^{0}
defined in (
28
) in Supplement
10.4
.
For the time being, let us also denote

\Gamma^{0}_{j}=(-({\eta^{0}}_{j})_{1},\ldots,-({\eta^{0}}_{j})_{j-1},1,-({\eta^{0}}_{j})_{j+1},\ldots,-({\eta^{0}}_{j})_{p+q}).

(95)

Lemma
20.2
establishes that
\max_{1\leq j\leq p+q}\|\Gamma^{0}_{j}\|_{2}
is bounded. The proof can be found in Subsection
21.4
.

Lemma 20.2
.

The
\Gamma^{0}_{j}
defined in (
95
) satisfies
\max_{1\leq j\leq p+q}\|\Gamma^{0}_{j}\|_{0}=O(s)
. Moreover,
there exists
C>0
so that

\max_{1\leq j\leq p+q}\|\Gamma^{0}_{j}\|_{2}\leq C
.

We denote the sample version of
\eta_{j}^{0}
to be
\widehat{\eta}_{j}
, which is given by the step NL1 in Algorithm
2
when
A=\widehat{H}_{n}(\widehat{x}_{n},\widehat{y}_{n})
, where
\widehat{x}_{n}=|\widehat{\rho}_{n}|^{1/2}\widehat{\alpha}_{n}
and
\widehat{\beta}_{n}=|\widehat{\rho}_{n}|^{1/2}\widehat{\beta}_{n}
.
Let us denote
\Delta(j)={\eta^{0}}_{j}-\widehat{\eta}_{j}
.
Recall from (
25
) in Algorithm
2
also that

\widehat{\Gamma}_{j}=(-(\widehat{\eta}_{j})_{1},\ldots,-(\widehat{\eta}_{j})_{j-1},1,-(\widehat{\eta}_{j})_{j+1},\ldots,-(\widehat{\eta}_{j})_{p+q}).

Let us denote

\Delta_{\Gamma}(j)=\widehat{\Gamma}_{j}-\Gamma^{0}_{j}.

(96)

We will now state a key lemma for the proof of Theorem
10.3
, which is proved in Subsection
20.2
.

Lemma 20.3
.

Under the set up of Theorem
10.3
, we can find
C>0
so that the followings hold with high probability for all sufficiently large
p
,
q
, and
n
:

\displaystyle\absolutevalue{\Delta_{\Gamma}(j)^T(\widehat{H}_n(\hx,\hy)-H^0)\Delta(j)}\leq C\lambda(\|\Delta(j)\|_{1}+s^{1/2}\|\Delta_{\Gamma,1}(j)\|_{2}+s^{\kappa}\|\Delta_{\Gamma}(j)\|_{2}^{2})\quad(j=1,\ldots,p+q),

\absolutevalue{\Delta_{\Gamma}(j)^T(\{\widehat{H}_n(\hx,\hy)\}-H^0)\Gamma^0_j}\leq C(\lambda\|\Delta(j)\|_{1}+s^{\kappa}\lambda\|\Delta(j)\|_{2})\quad(j=1,\ldots,p+q),

and

\absolutevalue{\Delta_{\Gamma}(j)^T(\{\widehat{H}_n(\hx,\hy)\}-H^0)e_j}\leq C(\lambda\|\Delta(j)\|_{1}+s^{\kappa}\lambda\|\Delta(j)\|_{2})\quad(j=1,\ldots,p+q).

Lemma
20.4
, which is proved in Subsection
20.2
, conveys a similar result.

Lemma 20.4
.

Under the set up of Theorem
10.3
, we can find
C>0
so that for sufficiently large
p
,
q
and
n
, the following holds with high probability:

\max_{1\leq j\leq p+q}\absolutevalue{e^T_j(\widehat H_n(\hx,\hy)-H^0){\Gamma}^0_j}=O_{p}(s^{\kappa}\lambda).

(97)

Now we will start the proof of Theorem
10.3
.
The proof has two main steps.
The first step establishes the proximity between
\eta_{j}^{0}
and
\widehat{\eta}_{j}
. In the second step, we establish that
|\widehat{\tau}^{2}_{j}-(\tau^{0}_{j})^{2}|=O_{p}(s^{\kappa}\lambda)
. Then using Lemma
10.2
, we show that
\Phi^{0}_{j}
and
(\widehat{\Phi}_{n})_{j}
are close.
Now we state and prove a lemma which establishes that the
l_{1}
and
l_{2}
norms of
\widehat{\eta}_{j}-{\eta^{0}}_{j}
are small.

Lemma 20.5
.

\max_{1\leq j\leq p+q}\|\widehat{\eta}_{j}-{\eta^{0}}_{j}\|_{2}=O_{p}(s^{\kappa}\lambda)\quad\text{and}\quad\max_{1\leq j\leq p+q}\|\widehat{\eta}_{j}-{\eta^{0}}_{j}\|_{1}=O_{p}(s^{\kappa+1/2}\lambda).

Proof 20.6
(of Lemma
20.5
).

We will denote

L(\eta)=\eta^{T}H^{0}_{-j,-j}\eta+H^{0}_{j,j}-2\eta^{T}H^{0}_{-j,j},\quad\eta\in\mathbb{R}^{p+q}.

Observe that

\dot{L}(\eta)=2H^{0}_{-j,-j}\eta-2H^{0}_{-j,j},\quad\eta\in\mathbb{R}^{p+q}.

The sample version of
L(\eta)
writes as

L_{n}(\eta)=\eta^{T}\{\widehat{H}_{n}(\widehat{x}_{n},\widehat{y}_{n})\}_{-j,-j}\eta+\widehat{H}_{n}(\widehat{x}_{n},\widehat{y}_{n})_{j,j}-2\eta^{T}\{\widehat{H}_{n}(\widehat{x}_{n},\widehat{y}_{n})\}_{-j,j},\quad\eta\in\mathbb{R}^{p+q}.

(98)

We can show that

\dot{L}_{n}(\eta)=2\{\widehat{H}_{n}(\widehat{x}_{n},\widehat{y}_{n})\}_{-j,-j}\eta-\{\widehat{H}_{n}(\widehat{x}_{n},\widehat{y}_{n})\}_{-j,j}\quad\eta\in\mathbb{R}^{p+q}.

Note that (
98
) is also the unpenalized objective function of (
23
).

Since
B_{j}\geq\|{\eta^{0}}_{j}\|_{1}
,
{\eta^{0}}_{j}
is in the feasible region of (
23
), where
\widehat{\eta}_{j}
is a stationary point of (
23
). Because (
23
) is a convex program, the following inequality holds:

(\dot{L}_{n}(\widehat{\eta}_{j})+\lambda^{nl}_{j}\widehat{Z}_{n})^{T}({\eta^{0}}_{j}-\widehat{\eta}_{j})\geq 0,

(99)

where
\widehat{Z}_{n}
is the subdifferential of the
l_{1}
norm evaluated at
\widehat{\eta}_{j}
. On the other hand, since
L
is a quadratic function in
\eta
,

L({\eta^{0}}_{j})-L(\widehat{\eta}_{j})=\dot{L}(\widehat{\eta}_{j})^{T}\Delta(j)+\frac{1}{2}\Delta(j)^{T}H^{0}_{-j,-j}\Delta(j).

(100)

Using Lemma
20.1
we obtain that

\displaystyle\Lambda_{min}(H^{0}_{-j,-j})\geq\Lambda_{min}(H^{0})\geq 2({\rho}_{0}-\Lambda_{2}),

(101)

which is positive by
Assumption
2.1
, which indicates
L:\mathbb{R}^{p+q-1}\mapsto\mathbb{R}
is strongly convex at
{\eta^{0}}_{j}
with positive definite Hessian
H^{0}_{-j,-j}
. Therefore (
100
) leads to

\displaystyle L(\widehat{\eta}_{j})-L({\eta^{0}}_{j})\leq-\dot{L}(\widehat{\eta}_{j})^{T}\Delta(j).

(102)

Recalling
\Delta(j)={\eta^{0}}_{j}-\widehat{\eta}_{j}
, and adding (
99
) and (
102
), we obtain an upper bound of
L(\widehat{\eta}_{j})-L({\eta^{0}}_{j})
:

L(\widehat{\eta}_{j})-L({\eta^{0}}_{j})\leq(\dot{L}_{n}(\widehat{\eta}_{j})-\dot{L}(\widehat{\eta}_{j}))^{T}\Delta(j)+\lambda^{nl}_{j}\widehat{Z}_{n}^{T}\Delta(j).

We can also find a lower bound on
L(\widehat{\eta}_{j})-L({\eta^{0}}_{j})
.

Let us define
C_{M}=({\rho}_{0}-\Lambda_{2})/M
. Equation
14
and
101
indicate that

L(\eta)-L({\eta^{0}}_{j})\geq\dot{L}({\eta^{0}}_{j})+C_{M}\|\eta-{\eta^{0}}_{j}\|^{2}_{2}.

By the definition of
{\eta^{0}}_{j}
in (
28
), it is the minimizer of
L
, i.e.
\dot{L}({\eta^{0}}_{j})=0
. Therefore,

L(\eta)-L({\eta^{0}}_{j})\geq C_{M}\|\eta-{\eta^{0}}_{j}\|^{2}_{2}.

Hence,

C_{M}\|\Delta(j)\|_{2}^{2}\leq L(\eta)-L({\eta^{0}}_{j})\leq(\dot{L}_{n}(\widehat{\eta}_{j})-\dot{L}(\widehat{\eta}_{j}))^{T}\Delta(j)+\lambda^{nl}_{j}\widehat{Z}_{n}^{T}\Delta(j).

Now let us denote
S=s({\eta^{0}}_{j})
.
By definition of
\widehat{Z}_{n}
, we have
\widehat{Z}_{n}^{T}\widehat{\eta}_{j}=\|\widehat{\eta}_{j}\|_{1}
and
\widehat{Z}_{n}^{T}{\eta^{0}}_{j}\leq\|{\eta^{0}}_{j}\|_{1}
, yielding

\displaystyle C_{M}\|\Delta(j)\|_{2}^{2}-(\dot{L}_{n}(\widehat{\eta}_{j})-\dot{L}(\widehat{\eta}_{j}))^{T}\Delta(j)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\ \lambda^{nl}_{j}(\|{\eta^{0}}_{j}\|_{1}-\|\widehat{\eta}_{j}\|_{1})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \lambda^{nl}_{j}(\|({\eta^{0}}_{j})_{S}\|_{1}-\|\Delta(j)+{\eta^{0}}_{j}\|_{1})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \lambda^{nl}_{j}\{\|({\eta^{0}}_{j})_{S}\|_{1}-\|\Delta(j)_{S}+({\eta^{0}}_{j})_{S}\|_{1}-\|\Delta(j)_{S^{c}}\|_{1}\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\ \lambda^{nl}_{j}(\|\Delta(j)_{S}\|_{1}-\|\Delta(j)_{S^{c}}\|_{1}).

Thus

\displaystyle C_{M}\|\Delta(j)\|_{2}^{2}\leq\lambda^{nl}_{j}(\|\Delta(j)_{S}\|_{1}-\|\Delta(j)_{S^{c}}\|_{1})+(\dot{L}_{n}(\widehat{\eta}_{j})-\dot{L}(\widehat{\eta}_{j}))^{T}\Delta(j).

(103)

Our next step is to find the rate of decay of the cross-term
(\dot{L}_{n}(\widehat{\eta}_{j})-\dot{L}(\widehat{\eta}_{j}))^{T}\Delta(j)
, which equals

\displaystyle\Delta(j)^{T}(\dot{L}_{n}(\widehat{\eta}_{j})-\dot{L}(\widehat{\eta}_{j}))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ 2\Delta(j)^{T}(\widehat{H}_{n}(\widehat{x}_{n},\widehat{y}_{n})_{-j,-j}-H^{0}_{-j,-j})\widehat{\eta}_{j}+\Delta(j)^{T}(\widehat{H}_{n}(\widehat{x}_{n},\widehat{y}_{n})_{-j,j}-H^{0}_{-j,j})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\ 2\Delta_{\Gamma}(j)^{T}(\widehat{H}_{n}(\widehat{x}_{n},\widehat{y}_{n})-H^{0})\widehat{\Gamma}_{j}+\Delta_{\Gamma}(j)^{T}(\widehat{H}_{n}(\widehat{x}_{n},\widehat{y}_{n})-H^{0})e_{j}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ 2\Delta_{\Gamma}(j)^{T}(\widehat{H}_{n}(\widehat{x}_{n},\widehat{y}_{n})-H^{0})\Delta_{\Gamma}(j)+2\Delta_{\Gamma}(j)^{T}(\widehat{H}_{n}(\widehat{x}_{n},\widehat{y}_{n})-H^{0})\Gamma^{0}_{j}+\Delta_{\Gamma}(j)^{T}(\widehat{H}_{n}(\widehat{x}_{n},\widehat{y}_{n})\widehat{H}^{0})e_{j}.

where
(a)
follows because
\Delta_{\Gamma}(j)_{j}=0
.
The above decomposition, combined with
Lemma
20.3
, indicates that there exists a large positive constant
C
such that the following holds with high probability for large
p
,
q
, and
n
:

\displaystyle\Delta(j)^{T}(\dot{L}_{n}(\widehat{\eta}_{j})-\dot{L}(\widehat{\eta}_{j}))\leq

\displaystyle\ C\lambda\Big(\|\Delta(j)\|_{1}+s^{\kappa}(\|\Delta(j)\|_{2}+\|\Delta(j)\|_{2}^{2})\Big)\quad(j=1,\ldots,p+q).

Lemma
20.3
also had some
s^{1/2}
terms, which we ignored because
s^{\kappa}
is greater than
s^{1/2}
since
\kappa\geq 1/2
by Condition
4.1
.
Since
\kappa\leq 1
, and
s\lambda=o(1)
by Fact
1
for sufficiently large
p
,
q
, and
n
, (
103
) implies

\displaystyle\frac{C_{M}}{2}\|\Delta(j)\|^{2}_{2}\leq(\lambda^{nl}_{j}+C\lambda)\|\Delta(j)_{S}\|_{1}-(\lambda^{nl}_{j}-C\lambda)\|\Delta(j)_{S^{c}}\|_{1}+Cs^{\kappa}\lambda\|\Delta(j)\|_{2}\quad(j=1,\ldots,p+q).

(104)

Suppose
\lambda^{nl}_{j}=C_{1}\lambda
where
C_{1}>C
.
Equation
104
then leads to some important consequences. First, note that

\|\Delta(j)_{S}\|_{1}\leq s^{1/2}\|\Delta(j)_{S}\|_{2}\leq s^{\kappa}\|\Delta(j)_{S}\|_{2}

because
\kappa\geq 1/2
. Using the above inequality,
104
reduces to

\|\Delta(j)\|^{2}_{2}\leq 2(2C+C_{1})s^{1/2}\lambda\|\Delta(j)\|_{2}/C_{M}\quad(j=1,\ldots,p+q),

which implies
\max_{1\leq j\leq p+q}\|\Delta(j)\|_{2}=O(s^{\kappa}\lambda)
.
Using the rate of
\|\Delta(j)\|_{2}
, from equation
104
, we conclude that there exists
C^{\prime}>0
so that

\displaystyle(C_{1}-C)\lambda\|\Delta(j)_{S^{c}}\|_{1}\leq(C_{1}+C)\lambda\|\Delta(j)_{S}\|_{1}+C^{\prime}s^{2\kappa}\lambda^{2}\quad(j=1,\ldots,p+q)

(105)

with high probability for sufficiently large
n
. Since
\|\Delta(j)_{S}\|_{1}\leq s^{1/2}\|\Delta(j)_{S}\|_{2}
, the above implies

\max_{1\leq j\leq p+q}\|\Delta(j)\|_{1}=O(s^{\kappa+1/2}\lambda+s^{2\kappa}\lambda^{2})
.
Now because
s\leq 1
,

s^{2\kappa}\lambda^{2}=s^{\kappa+1/2}\lambda(s^{\kappa-1/2}\lambda)\leq s^{\kappa+1/2}\lambda s^{1/2}\lambda=o(s^{\kappa+1/2}\lambda)

because
s^{1/2}\lambda=o(1)
by Fact
1
.
Hence, the proof follows.

Our next step is to find the rate of convergence of
\widehat{\tau}_{j}^{2}
defined in (
24
).

Lemma 20.7
.

Under the set-up of Theorem
10.3
,
\widehat{\tau}_{j}
implies

\max_{1\leq j\leq p+q}|\widehat{\tau}_{j}^{2}-(\tau^{0})^{2}_{j}|=O_{p}(s^{\kappa}\lambda).

Proof 20.8
(of Lemma
20.7
).

By (
31
), we have

\widehat{\tau}_{j}^{2}=\widehat{H}_{n}(\widehat{x}_{n},\widehat{y}_{n})_{j}^{T}\widehat{\Gamma}_{j}
. Also, (
27
) implies
\Gamma^{0}_{j}/(\tau^{0}_{j})^{2}=\Phi^{0}_{j}
. Noting

(\Phi^{0}_{j})^{T}H^{0}_{j}=1
, We derive the relation
(\tau^{0}_{j})^{2}=(H^{0}_{j})^{T}\Gamma^{0}_{j}
. Therefore we can write

\displaystyle|\widehat{\tau}_{j}^{2}-(\tau^{0})^{2}_{j}|=

\displaystyle\ \absolutevalue{e^T_j\slb H_n(\hx,\hy)\widehat{\Gamma}_j-H^0\Gamma^0_j\srb}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\ \absolutevalue{e^T_j( \widehat H_n(\hx,\hy)-H^0)\widehat{\Gamma}_j}+\absolutevalue{e^T_jH^0(\widehat{\Gamma}_j-\Gamma^0_j)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\ \absolutevalue{e^T_j( \widehat H_n(\hx,\hy)-H^0)\Delta_{\Gamma}(j)}+\absolutevalue{e^T_j(\widehat H_n(\hx,\hy)-H^0){\Gamma}^0_j}+\absolutevalue{e^T_jH^0(\widehat{\Gamma}_j-\Gamma^0_j)}

where
\Delta_{\Gamma}(j)=\widehat{\Gamma}_{j}-\Gamma^{0}_{j}
.
Lemma
20.4
implies

\max_{1\leq j\leq p+q}\absolutevalue{e^T_j(\widehat H_n(\hx,\hy)-H^0){\Gamma}^0_j}=O_{p}(s^{\kappa}\lambda).

By Lemma
20.3
and Lemma
20.5
,

\max_{1\leq j\leq p+q}\absolutevalue{e^T_j( \widehat H_n(\hx,\hy)-H^0)\Delta_{\Gamma}(j)}=O_{p}((s^{\kappa+1/2}+s^{2\kappa})\lambda^{2})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{=}}O_{p}(s^{2\kappa}\lambda^{2})

where (a) follows because
\kappa\geq 1/2
.
Since
s^{\kappa}\lambda\leq s\lambda=o(1)
by Fact
1
, we have

\max_{1\leq j\leq p+q}\absolutevalue{e^T_j( \widehat H_n(\hx,\hy)-H^0)\Delta_{\Gamma}(j)}=o_{p}(s^{\kappa}\lambda).

On the other hand, there exist positive constants
C
and
C^{\prime}
so that

\max_{1\leq j\leq p+q}\absolutevalue{e^T_jH^0(\widehat{\Gamma}_j-\Gamma^0_j)}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{\leq}}\max_{1\leq j\leq p+q}C\|\widehat{\Gamma}_{j}-\Gamma^{0}_{j}\|_{2}\leq C^{\prime}\max_{1\leq j\leq p+q}\|\widehat{\eta}_{j}-{\eta^{0}}_{j}\|_{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(b)}}{{=}}O_{p}(s^{\kappa}\lambda)

where (a) and (b) follow from Lemma
20.1
and Lemma
20.5
, respectively.
Hence, the proof follows.

The definition of
\widehat{\Gamma}_{j}
in (
25
) implies
(\widehat{\Phi}_{n})_{j}=\widehat{\tau}_{j}^{-2}\widehat{\Gamma}_{j}

and (
27
) implies
\Phi^{0}_{j}=(\tau^{0}_{j})^{-2}\Gamma^{0}_{j}
.
Hence, for
i=1,2
, we have

\|(\widehat{\Phi}_{n})_{j}-\Phi^{0}_{j}\|_{i}=\|\widehat{\tau}_{j}^{-2}(\widehat{\Gamma}_{j}-\Gamma^{0}_{j})\|_{i}+|\widehat{\tau}_{j}^{-2}-(\tau_{j}^{0})^{-2}|\|\Gamma^{0}_{j}\|_{i}.

(106)

Recall from Lemma
20.1

that
\min_{1\leq j\leq p+q}(\tau_{j}^{0})^{2}
is bounded below by a positive constant, say
C>0
. Writing

\widehat{\tau}_{j}^{-2}-(\tau_{j}^{0})^{-2}=\frac{|\widehat{\tau}_{j}^{2}-(\tau^{0}_{j})^{2}|}{\widehat{\tau}_{j}^{2}(\tau^{0}_{j})^{2}}\leq\frac{|\widehat{\tau}_{j}^{2}-(\tau^{0}_{j})^{2}|}{(\tau^{0}_{j})^{2}\Big((\tau^{0}_{j})^{2}-|\widehat{\tau}_{j}^{2}-(\tau^{0}_{j})^{2}|\Big)},

we thus obtain

\max_{1\leq j\leq p+q}|\widehat{\tau}_{j}^{-2}-(\tau_{j}^{0})^{-2}|\leq\frac{\max_{1\leq j\leq p+q}|\widehat{\tau}_{j}^{2}-(\tau^{0}_{j})^{2}|}{C\Big(C-\max_{1\leq j\leq p+q}|\widehat{\tau}_{j}^{2}-(\tau^{0}_{j})^{2}|\Big)},

which is
O_{p}(s^{\kappa}\lambda)
by
Lemma
20.7
.
As a corollary,

\max_{1\leq j\leq p+q}|\widehat{\tau}_{j}^{-2}|\leq\max_{1\leq j\leq p+q}|(\tau^{0}_{j})^{-2}|+O_{p}(s^{\kappa}\lambda)\leq 2/C.

Noting

\|\widehat{\Gamma}_{j}-\Gamma^{0}_{j}\|_{i}=\|\widehat{\eta}_{j}-{\eta^{0}}_{j}\|_{i}\quad(i=1,2,j=1,\ldots,p+q),

(107)

(
106
) can be used to obtain

\max_{1\leq j\leq p+q}\|(\widehat{\Phi}_{n})_{j}-\Phi^{0}_{j}\|_{2}\leq 2\|\widehat{\eta}_{j}-{\eta^{0}}_{j}\|_{2}/C+O_{p}(s^{1/2}\lambda)\|\Gamma^{0}_{j}\|_{2}\quad(j=1,\ldots,p+q),

which is
O_{p}(s^{\kappa}\lambda)
since
|(\widehat{\Phi}_{n})_{j}-\Phi^{0}_{j}\|_{2}=O_{p}(s^{\kappa}\lambda)
by Lemma
20.5
and
\max_{1\leq j\leq p+q}\|\Gamma^{0}_{j}\|_{2}=O_{p}(1)
by Lemma
20.2
.

For the
l_{1}
-error, (
106
) and (
107
) yield

\max_{1\leq j\leq p+q}\|(\widehat{\Phi}_{n})_{j}-\Phi^{0}_{j}\|_{1}\leq 2\|\widehat{\eta}_{j}-{\eta^{0}}_{j}\|_{1}/C+O_{p}(s^{\kappa}\lambda)\|\Gamma^{0}_{j}\|_{1},

which is
O_{p}(s^{\kappa+1/2}\lambda)
since
\|\widehat{\eta}_{j}-{\eta^{0}}_{j}\|_{1}=O_{p}(s^{\kappa+1/2}\lambda)
by Lemma
20.5
, and

\max_{1\leq j\leq p+q}\|\Gamma^{0}_{j}\|_{1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{\leq}}O(s^{1/2})\max_{1\leq j\leq p+q}\|\Gamma^{0}_{j}\|_{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(b)}}{{=}}O(s^{1/2})

where (a) follows because

\max_{1\leq j\leq p+q}\|\Gamma^{0}_{j}\|_{0}=\max_{1\leq j\leq p+q}\|{\eta^{0}}_{j}\|_{0}+1,

which is
O(s^{1/2})

by Assumption
10.4
, and (b) follows by Lemma
20.2
.
Thus the proof of Theorem
10.3
follows.

Proof 20.9
(of Lemma

).

Our first step is to find an expression for
\Delta_{\Gamma}(j)^{T}(\widehat{H}-H^{0})z
for a general
z\in\mathbb{R}^{p\times q}
, and then use this expression to find the rates for the special cases when
z=\Delta_{\Gamma}(j)
,
\Gamma^{0}_{j}
, or
e_{j}
. Now let us introduce some new notations.
Let
\Delta_{\Gamma}(j)=(\Delta_{\Gamma,1}(j),\Delta_{\Gamma,2}(j))
and
z=(z_{1},z_{2})
where
\Delta_{\Gamma,1}(j),z_{1}\in\mathbb{R}^{p}
and
\Delta_{\Gamma,2}(j),z_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{q}
. Also for the sake of simplicity, we denote
\widehat{H}_{n}=\widehat{H}(\widehat{x}_{n},\widehat{y}_{n})
.
For
A=\widehat{H}_{n}
and
H^{0}
, let us partition
A
into

A=\begin{bmatrix}A_{11}&A_{12}\\
A_{21}&A_{22}\end{bmatrix},\quad A_{11}\in\mathbb{R}^{p\times p},A_{12}\in\mathbb{R}^{p\times q},A_{21}\in\mathbb{R}^{q\times p},A_{22}\in\mathbb{R}^{q\times q}.

Using these new notations, we write

\displaystyle\Delta_{\Gamma}(j)^{T}(\widehat{H}-H^{0})z=

\displaystyle\ \Delta_{\Gamma,1}(j)^{T}(\widehat{H}_{11}-H^{0}_{11})z_{1}+\Delta_{\Gamma,1}(j)^{T}(\widehat{H}_{12}-H^{0}_{12})z_{2}

\displaystyle\ +\Delta(j)_{2}^{T}(\widehat{H}_{21}-H^{0}_{21})z_{1}+\Delta(j)_{2}^{T}(\widehat{H}_{22}-H^{0}_{22})z_{2}.

Now observe
\Delta_{\Gamma,1}(j)^{T}(\widehat{H}_{11}-H^{0}_{11})z_{1}
can be further decomposed into

\displaystyle\Delta_{\Gamma,1}(j)^{T}(\widehat{H}_{11}-H^{0}_{11})z_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ 2\Delta(j)_{1}^{T}\Big((\widehat{x}_{n}^{T}\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,x}\widehat{x}_{n})\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,x}+2\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,x}\widehat{x}_{n}\widehat{x}_{n}^{T}\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,x}-{\rho}_{0}{\Sigma}_{x}-2{\Sigma}_{x}x^{0}(x^{0})^{T}{\Sigma}_{x}\Big)z_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ 2\underbrace{\Big(\widehat{x}_{n}^{T}\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,x}\widehat{x}_{n}-{\rho}_{0}\Big)\Delta_{\Gamma,1}(j)^{T}(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,x}-{\Sigma}_{x})z_{1}}_{T_{1}(z;j)}+2\underbrace{\Big(\widehat{x}_{n}^{T}\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,x}\widehat{x}_{n}-{\rho}_{0}\Big)\Delta_{\Gamma,1}(j)^{T}{\Sigma}_{x}z_{1}}_{T_{2}(z;j)}

\displaystyle\ +2\underbrace{{\rho}_{0}\Delta_{\Gamma,1}(j)^{T}(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,x}-{\Sigma}_{x})z_{1}}_{T_{3}(z;j)}+4\underbrace{\Delta_{\Gamma,1}(j)^{T}(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,x}-{\Sigma}_{x})\widehat{x}_{n}\widehat{x}_{n}^{T}(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,x}-{\Sigma}_{x})z_{1}}_{T_{4}(z;j)}

\displaystyle\ +4\underbrace{\Delta_{\Gamma,1}(j)^{T}(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,x}-{\Sigma}_{x})\widehat{x}_{n}\widehat{x}_{n}^{T}{\Sigma}_{x}z_{1}}_{T_{5}(z;j)}+4\underbrace{\Delta_{\Gamma,1}(j)^{T}{\Sigma}_{x}\widehat{x}_{n}\widehat{x}_{n}^{T}(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,x}-{\Sigma}_{x})z_{1}}_{T_{6}(z;j)}

\displaystyle\ +4\underbrace{\Delta_{\Gamma,1}(j)^{T}{\Sigma}_{x}(\widehat{x}_{n}\widehat{x}_{n}^{T}-x^{0}(x^{0})^{T}){\Sigma}_{x}z_{1}}_{T_{7}(z;j)}.

Also,

\displaystyle\Delta_{\Gamma,1}(j)^{T}(\widehat{H}_{12}-H^{0}_{12})z_{2}=

\displaystyle\ 2\underbrace{\Delta(j)_{1}^{T}({\Sigma}_{xy}-\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,xy})z_{2}}_{T_{8}(z;j)}.

To find the rate of
\Delta_{\Gamma}(j)^{T}(\widehat{H}-H^{0})z
for any
z
, it suffices to look at the rate of
\Delta(j)_{1}^{T}(\widehat{H}_{11}-H^{0}_{11})z_{1}
and
\Delta(j)_{1}^{T}(\widehat{H}_{12}-H^{0}_{12})z_{2}
only because the calculations for the other two terms will be similar. Hence, it is sufficient to find the rate of
\sum_{i=1}^{8}T_{i}(z,j)
when
z=\Delta(j)
,
e_{j}
, and
\Gamma^{0}_{j}
.

First, let us consider the case when

. Claim
10
and the above decomposition implies

\Delta_{\Gamma}(j)^{T}(\widehat{H}-H^{0})\Delta_{\Gamma}(j)=O_{p}(\lambda)\|\Delta(j)\|_{1}+O_{p}(s^{1/2}\lambda)\|\Delta(j)\|_{2}+O_{p}(s^{1/2}\lambda)\|\Delta(j)\|_{2}^{2}

uniformly across

.

,

, and

.
Claim

handles the case when

or
\Gamma_{j}^{0}

Claim 11
.

Suppose

for some

with high probability for sufficiently large

.

That

. Condition

Lemma

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{8}(|T_{i}(\Gamma^{0}_{j};j)|+|T_{i}(e_{j};j)|)\leq C(\lambda\|\Delta(j)\|_{1}+s^{\kappa}\lambda\|\Delta(j)\|_{2})\quad(j=1,\ldots,p+q)

(108)

with high probability for all sufficiently large

,

, and

. The proof follows combining the above result with Claim

because
\kappa\geq 1/2
,.
