21
Proof of Technical Lemmas

21.1
Proof of technical lemmas for Theorem
4.7

Our next lemma, which gives the form of
\Phi^{0}
, is required for obtaining the form of
\sigma_{\rho}^{2}
.

Lemma 21.1
.

Suppose
\Phi^{0}=(H^{0})^{-1}
, where
H^{0}
is as defined in (
7
). Then

\Phi^{0}=(2{\rho}_{0})^{-1}\begin{bmatrix}UO_{4}U^{T}+{\Sigma}_{x}^{-1}&UO_{3}V^{T}\\
VO_{3}U^{T}&VO_{4}V^{T}+{\Sigma}_{y}^{-1}\end{bmatrix},

where

O_{3}=\text{Diag}(1/8,{\rho}_{0}\Lambda_{2}/({\rho}_{0}^{2}-\Lambda_{2}^{2}),\ldots,{\rho}_{0}\Lambda_{r}/({\rho}_{0}^{2}-\Lambda_{r}^{2}))\in\mathbb{R}^{r\times r},

and

O_{4}=O_{1}-I_{r}=\text{Diag}\Big(-5/8,\Lambda_{2}^{2}/({\rho}_{0}^{2}-\Lambda^{2}_{2}),\ldots,\Lambda_{r}^{2}/({\rho}_{0}^{2}-\Lambda_{r}^{2})\Big).

Proof 21.2
(of Lemma
21.1
).

Let us denote
\tilde{u}_{i}={\Sigma}_{x}^{1/2}u_{i}

(i=1,\ldots,r)
and
and
\tilde{v}_{i}={\Sigma}_{y}^{1/2}v_{i}

(i=1,\ldots,r)
. Letting

D=\text{Diag}({\Sigma}_{x}^{1/2},{\Sigma}_{y}^{1/2}),\quad A=\begin{bmatrix}I_{p}+2\tilde{u}_{1}\tilde{u}_{1}^{T}&-{\Sigma}_{x}^{1/2}U\Lambda V^{T}{\Sigma}_{y}^{1/2}/{\rho}_{0}\\
-{\Sigma}_{y}^{1/2}V\Lambda U^{T}{\Sigma}_{x}^{1/2}/{\rho}_{0}&I_{q}+2\tilde{v}_{1}\tilde{v}_{1}^{T}\end{bmatrix},

and using (
36
), we obtain that
H^{0}=2{\rho}_{0}DAD
.
If
A
is invertible, then
\Phi^{0}=(2{\rho}_{0})^{-1}D^{-1}A^{-1}D^{-1}
. We will now show that
A
is invertible, and find its inverse.

To that end, first we introduce some notations. Since the columns of
\tilde{U}
are orthogonal, we can extend
\tilde{U}=[\tilde{u}_{1},\ldots,\tilde{u}_{r}]
to
\tilde{U}_{*}=[\tilde{u}_{1},\ldots,\tilde{u}_{p}]
so that
\tilde{U}_{*}\tilde{U}_{*}^{T}=\tilde{U}_{*}^{T}\tilde{U}_{*}=I_{p}
. Similarly, we can extend
\tilde{V}
to a basis
\tilde{V}_{*}=[\tilde{v}_{1},\ldots,\tilde{v}_{q}]
. Now if we let

\Lambda_{*}=\begin{bmatrix}\Lambda_{r\times r}&0_{r\times(q-r)}\\
0_{(p-r)\times r}&0_{(p-r)\times(q-r)}\end{bmatrix},

then it follows that

\tilde{U}\Lambda\tilde{V}^{T}=\tilde{U}_{*}\Lambda_{*}\tilde{V}^{T}_{*},\quad I_{p}=\tilde{U}_{*}\tilde{U}_{*}^{T},\quad I_{q}=\tilde{V}_{*}\tilde{V}_{*}^{T}.

Let us denote

F=\text{Diag}(3,\underbrace{1,\ldots,1}_{p-1\text{ times }})\quad\text{ and }\quad G=\text{Diag}(3,\underbrace{1,\ldots,1}_{q-1\text{ times }}).

Note that

\tilde{U}_{*}F\tilde{U}^{T}_{*}=I_{p}+2\tilde{u}_{1}\tilde{u}_{1}^{T},\quad\tilde{U}_{*}G\tilde{U}^{T}_{*}=I_{q}+2\tilde{v}_{1}\tilde{v}_{1}^{T}.

Therefore,
A
can be written as

\displaystyle A=\begin{bmatrix}\tilde{U}_{*}F\tilde{U}^{T}_{*}&-\tilde{U}_{*}(\Lambda_{*}/{\rho}_{0})\tilde{V}^{T}_{*}\\
-\tilde{V}_{*}(\Lambda^{T}_{*}/{\rho}_{0})\tilde{U}^{T}_{*}&\tilde{V}_{*}G\tilde{V}^{T}_{*}\end{bmatrix}.

Further simplification of
A
is possible. To that end,
we define

D_{2}=\text{Diag}(\tilde{U}_{*},\tilde{V}_{*})\quad\text{and}\quad J=\begin{bmatrix}F&-\Lambda_{*}/{\rho}_{0}\\
-\Lambda^{T}_{*}/{\rho}_{0}&G\end{bmatrix}.

(112)

It is easy to see that
A=D_{2}JD_{2}^{T}
. Because
F
,
G
, and the corresponding Schur components
F-\Lambda_{*}G^{-1}\Lambda^{T}_{*}/{\rho}_{0}^{2}
and
G-\Lambda_{*}^{T}F^{-1}\Lambda_{*}/{\rho}_{0}^{2}
are diagonal, they are invertible. Therefore,
J
is also invertible, which implies

A^{-1}=(D_{2}^{T})^{-1}J^{-1}D_{2}^{-1}
where

D_{2}^{-1}=\text{Diag}(\tilde{U}^{T}_{*},\tilde{V}^{T}_{*})=D_{2}^{T}
.
Thus
A^{-1}=D_{2}J^{-1}D_{2}^{T}
.
To find
J^{-1}
, we first write it in a block matrix form:

J^{-1}=\begin{bmatrix}J^{11}&J^{12}\\
(J^{12})^{T}&J^{22}\end{bmatrix}.

Here we used the fact that
J^{-1}
is symmetric which follows since
J
is symmetric. Now using the formula for block matrix inversion, we obtain that

\displaystyle J^{11}=(F-\Lambda_{*}G^{-1}\Lambda^{T}_{*}/{\rho}_{0}^{2})^{-1},

\displaystyle J^{12}=J^{11}\Lambda_{*}G^{-1}/{\rho}_{0},

\displaystyle J^{22}=(G-\Lambda_{*}^{T}F^{-1}\Lambda_{*}/{\rho}_{0}^{2})^{-1}.

Now we compute that

\displaystyle(J^{11})^{-1}=

\displaystyle\ \text{Diag}(3-{\rho}_{0}^{2}/(3{\rho}_{0}^{2}),1-\Lambda_{2}^{2}/{\rho}_{0}^{2},\ldots,1-\Lambda_{r}^{2}/{\rho}_{0}^{2},\underbrace{1,\ldots,1}_{p-r\text{ times}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \text{Diag}(8/3,1-\Lambda_{2}^{2}/{\rho}_{0}^{2},\ldots,1-\Lambda_{r}^{2}/{\rho}_{0}^{2},\underbrace{1,\ldots,1}_{p-r\text{ times}})

Therefore,

J^{11}=\text{Diag}(3/8,{\rho}_{0}^{2}/({\rho}_{0}^{2}-\Lambda_{2}^{2}),\ldots,{\rho}_{0}^{2}/({\rho}_{0}^{2}-\Lambda_{r}^{2}),\underbrace{1,\ldots,1}_{p-r\text{ times}}).

Letting

O_{1}=\text{Diag}(3/8,{\rho}_{0}^{2}/({\rho}_{0}^{2}-\Lambda_{2}^{2}),\ldots,{\rho}_{0}^{2}/({\rho}_{0}^{2}-\Lambda_{r}^{2}))\in\mathbb{R}^{r\times r},\quad\text{we obtain}\quad J^{11}=\begin{bmatrix}O_{1}&0\\
0&I_{p-r}\end{bmatrix}.

Similarly, observe that

\Lambda_{*}G^{-1}/{\rho}_{0}=\begin{bmatrix}O_{2}&0\\
0&0\end{bmatrix},\quad\text{where}\quad O_{2}=\text{Diag}(1/3,\Lambda_{2}/{\rho}_{0},\ldots,\Lambda_{r}/{\rho}_{0})\in\mathbb{R}^{r\times r}.

Thus

\displaystyle J^{12}=\begin{bmatrix}O_{1}&0\\
0&I_{p-r}\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}O_{2}&0\\
0&0\end{bmatrix}=\begin{bmatrix}O_{3}&0\\
0&0\end{bmatrix},

where

O_{3}=O_{1}O_{2}=\text{Diag}\Big(1/8,{\rho}_{0}\Lambda_{2}/({\rho}_{0}^{2}-\Lambda_{2}^{2}),\ldots,{\rho}_{0}\Lambda_{r}/({\rho}_{0}^{2}-\Lambda_{r}^{2})\Big).

By symmetry,

J^{-1}=\begin{bmatrix}O_{1}&0&O_{3}&0\\
0&I_{p-r}&0&0\\
O_{3}&0&O_{1}&0\\
0&0&0&I_{q-r}\end{bmatrix}.

Hence using (
112
), we obtain that

\displaystyle\Phi^{0}=

\displaystyle\ (2{\rho}_{0})^{-1}D^{-1}D_{2}J^{-1}D_{2}^{T}D^{-1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ (2{\rho}_{0})^{-1}\begin{bmatrix}{\Sigma}_{x}^{-1/2}U_{*}&0\\
0&{\Sigma}_{y}^{-1/2}V_{*}\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}O_{1}&0&O_{3}&0\\
0&I_{p-r}&0&0\\
O_{3}&0&O_{1}&0\\
0&0&0&I_{q-r}\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}\tilde{U}_{*}^{T}{\Sigma}_{x}^{-1/2}&0\\
0&\tilde{V}_{*}^{T}{\Sigma}_{y}^{-1/2}\end{bmatrix}.

If we denote
\tilde{U}_{-r}=[\tilde{u}_{r+1},\ldots,\tilde{u}_{p}]
, and
\tilde{V}_{-r}=[\tilde{v}_{r+1},\ldots,\tilde{v}_{q}]
, then it follows that

\displaystyle\Phi^{0}=

\displaystyle\ (2{\rho}_{0})^{-1}\begin{bmatrix}{\Sigma}_{x}^{-1/2}\tilde{U}&{\Sigma}_{x}^{-1/2}\tilde{U}_{-r}&0&0\\
0&0&{\Sigma}_{y}^{-1/2}\tilde{V}&{\Sigma}_{y}^{-1/2}\tilde{V}_{-r}\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}O_{1}&0&O_{3}&0\\
0&I_{p-r}&0&0\\
O_{3}&0&O_{1}&0\\
0&0&0&I_{q-r}\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}\tilde{U}^{T}{\Sigma}_{x}^{-1/2}&0\\
\tilde{U}_{-r}^{T}{\Sigma}_{x}^{-1/2}&0\\
0&\tilde{V}^{T}{\Sigma}_{y}^{-1/2}\\
0&\tilde{V}_{-r}^{T}{\Sigma}_{y}^{-1/2}\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ (2{\rho}_{0})^{-1}\begin{bmatrix}{\Sigma}_{x}^{-1/2}\tilde{U}O_{1}&{\Sigma}_{x}^{-1/2}\tilde{U}_{-r}&{\Sigma}_{x}^{-1/2}\tilde{U}O_{3}&0\\
{\Sigma}_{y}^{-1/2}\tilde{V}O_{3}&0&{\Sigma}_{y}^{-1/2}\tilde{V}O_{1}&{\Sigma}_{y}^{-1/2}\tilde{V}_{-r}\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}\tilde{U}^{T}{\Sigma}_{x}^{-1/2}&0\\
\tilde{U}_{-r}^{T}{\Sigma}_{x}^{-1/2}&0\\
0&\tilde{V}^{T}{\Sigma}_{y}^{-1/2}\\
0&\tilde{V}_{-r}^{T}{\Sigma}_{y}^{-1/2}\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ (2{\rho}_{0})^{-1}\begin{bmatrix}{\Sigma}_{x}^{-1/2}\tilde{U}O_{1}\tilde{U}^{T}{\Sigma}_{x}^{-1/2}+{\Sigma}_{x}^{-1/2}\tilde{U}_{-r}\tilde{U}_{-r}^{T}{\Sigma}_{x}^{-1/2}&{\Sigma}_{x}^{-1/2}\tilde{U}O_{3}\tilde{V}^{T}{\Sigma}_{y}^{-1/2}\\
{\Sigma}_{y}^{-1/2}\tilde{V}O_{3}\tilde{U}^{T}{\Sigma}_{x}^{-1/2}&{\Sigma}_{y}^{-1/2}\tilde{V}O_{1}\tilde{V}^{T}{\Sigma}_{y}^{-1/2}+{\Sigma}_{y}^{-1/2}\tilde{V}_{-r}\tilde{V}^{T}_{-r}{\Sigma}_{y}^{-1/2}\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\ (2{\rho}_{0})^{-1}D^{-1}\begin{bmatrix}\tilde{U}O_{1}\tilde{U}^{T}+I_{p}-\tilde{U}\tilde{U}^{T}&\tilde{U}O_{3}\tilde{V}^{T}\\
\tilde{V}O_{3}\tilde{U}^{T}&\tilde{V}O_{1}\tilde{V}^{T}+I_{q}-\tilde{V}\tilde{V}^{T}\end{bmatrix}D^{-1}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(b)}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\ (2{\rho}_{0})^{-1}D^{-1}\begin{bmatrix}\tilde{U}O_{4}\tilde{U}^{T}+I_{p}&\tilde{U}O_{3}\tilde{V}^{T}\\
\tilde{V}O_{3}\tilde{U}^{T}&\tilde{V}O_{4}\tilde{V}^{T}+I_{q}\end{bmatrix}D^{-1}

where (a) follows because
\tilde{U}\tilde{U}^{T}=I_{p}-\tilde{U}_{-r}\tilde{U}_{-r}^{T}
,
\tilde{V}\tilde{V}^{T}=I_{q}-\tilde{V}_{-r}\tilde{V}_{-r}^{T}
, and in (b), we used the notation

O_{4}=O_{1}-I_{r}=\text{Diag}\Big(-5/8,\Lambda_{2}^{2}/({\rho}_{0}^{2}-\Lambda^{2}_{2}),\ldots,\Lambda_{r}^{2}/({\rho}_{0}^{2}-\Lambda_{r}^{2})\Big).

Since
{\Sigma}_{x}^{-1/2}\tilde{U}=U
and
{\Sigma}_{y}^{-1/2}\tilde{V}=V
, we have

\Phi^{0}=(2{\rho}_{0})^{-1}\begin{bmatrix}UO_{4}U^{T}+{\Sigma}_{x}^{-1}&UO_{3}V^{T}\\
VO_{3}U^{T}&VO_{4}V^{T}+{\Sigma}_{y}^{-1}\end{bmatrix}

Proof 21.3
(of Lemma
18.4
).

First we will find the expression of
\mathcal{L}_{1}
. To that end, using Lemma
21.1
, we calculate that

\displaystyle 2\Phi^{0}\begin{bmatrix}{\rho}_{0}(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,x}-{\Sigma}_{x})x^{0}-(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,xy}-{\Sigma}_{xy})y^{0}+\Big\{(x^{0})^{T}(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,x}-{\Sigma}_{x})x^{0}\Big\}{\Sigma}_{x}x^{0}\\
{\rho}_{0}(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,y}-{\Sigma}_{y})y^{0}-(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,yx}-{\Sigma}_{yx})x^{0}+\Big\{(y^{0})^{T}(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,y}-{\Sigma}_{y})y^{0}\Big\}{\Sigma}_{y}y^{0}\\
\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ 2(2{\rho}_{0})^{-1}\begin{bmatrix}UO_{4}U^{T}+{\Sigma}_{x}^{-1}&UO_{3}V^{T}\\
VO_{3}U^{T}&VO_{4}V^{T}+{\Sigma}_{y}^{-1}\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}{\rho}_{0}(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,x}-{\Sigma}_{x})x^{0}-(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,xy}-{\Sigma}_{xy})y^{0}\\
+\Big\{(x^{0})^{T}(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,x}-{\Sigma}_{x})x^{0}\Big\}{\Sigma}_{x}x^{0}\\
\\
{\rho}_{0}(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,y}-{\Sigma}_{y})y^{0}-(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,yx}-{\Sigma}_{yx})x^{0}\\
+\Big\{(y^{0})^{T}(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,y}-{\Sigma}_{y})y^{0}\Big\}{\Sigma}_{y}y^{0}\\
\end{bmatrix}.

Thus

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{1}=

\displaystyle\ UO_{4}U^{T}(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,x}-{\Sigma}_{x})x^{0}+{\Sigma}_{x}^{-1}(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,x}-{\Sigma}_{x})x^{0}-{\rho}_{0}^{-1}UO_{4}U^{T}(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,xy}-{\Sigma}_{xy})y^{0}

\displaystyle\ -{\rho}_{0}^{-1}{\Sigma}_{x}^{-1}(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,xy}-{\Sigma}_{xy})y^{0}+{\rho}_{0}^{-1}\Big\{(x^{0})^{T}(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,x}-{\Sigma}_{x})x^{0}\Big\}UO_{4}U^{T}{\Sigma}_{x}x^{0}

\displaystyle\ +{\rho}_{0}^{-1}\Big\{(x^{0})^{T}(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,x}-{\Sigma}_{x})x^{0}\Big\}x^{0}+UO_{3}V^{T}(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,y}-{\Sigma}_{y})y^{0}

\displaystyle\ -{\rho}_{0}^{-1}UO_{3}V^{T}(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,yx}-{\Sigma}_{yx})x^{0}+{\rho}_{0}^{-1}\Big\{(y^{0})^{T}(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,y}-{\Sigma}_{y})y^{0}\Big\}UO_{3}V^{T}{\Sigma}_{y}y^{0}.

Noting
{\Sigma}_{xy}y^{0}={\rho}_{0}{\Sigma}_{x}x^{0}
, we deduce

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{1}^{T}{\Sigma}_{xy}y^{0}=

\displaystyle\ \underbrace{{\rho}_{0}(x^{0})^{T}(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,x}-{\Sigma}_{x})UO_{4}U^{T}{\Sigma}_{x}x^{0}}_{T_{1}}+\underbrace{{\rho}_{0}(x^{0})^{T}(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,x}-{\Sigma}_{x})x^{0}}_{T_{2}}

\displaystyle\ -\underbrace{(y^{0})^{T}(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,yx}-{\Sigma}_{yx})UO_{4}U^{T}{\Sigma}_{x}x^{0}}_{T_{3}}-\underbrace{(y^{0})^{T}(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,yx}-{\Sigma}_{yx})x^{0}}_{T_{4}}

\displaystyle\ +\underbrace{\Big\{(x^{0})^{T}(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,x}-{\Sigma}_{x})x^{0}\Big\}(x^{0})^{T}{\Sigma}_{x}UO_{4}U^{T}{\Sigma}_{x}x^{0}}_{T_{5}}+\underbrace{\Big\{(x^{0})^{T}(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,x}-{\Sigma}_{x})x^{0}\Big\}(x^{0})^{T}{\Sigma}_{x}x^{0}}_{T_{6}}

\displaystyle\ +\underbrace{{\rho}_{0}(y^{0})^{T}(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,y}-{\Sigma}_{y})VO_{3}U^{T}{\Sigma}_{x}x^{0}}_{T_{7}}-\underbrace{(x^{0})^{T}(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,xy}-{\Sigma}_{xy})VO_{3}U^{T}{\Sigma}_{x}x^{0}}_{T_{8}}

\displaystyle\ +\underbrace{\Big\{(y^{0})^{T}(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,y}-{\Sigma}_{y})y^{0}\Big\}(y^{0})^{T}{\Sigma}_{y}VO_{3}U^{T}{\Sigma}_{x}x^{0}}_{T_{9}}.

Note that

\displaystyle T_{1}={\rho}_{0}(x^{0})^{T}(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,x}-{\Sigma}_{x})UO_{4}U^{T}{\Sigma}_{x}x^{0}={\rho}_{0}^{3/2}(x^{0})^{T}(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,x}-{\Sigma}_{x})UO_{4}e_{1}={\rho}_{0}(O_{4})_{11}(x^{0})^{T}(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,x}-{\Sigma}_{x})x^{0}.

Similarly, we can show that

\displaystyle T_{3}=(O_{4})_{11}(y^{0})^{T}(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,yx}-{\Sigma}_{yx})x^{0},

\displaystyle T_{5}={\rho}_{0}(O_{4})_{11}(x^{0})^{T}(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,x}-{\Sigma}_{x})x^{0},

\displaystyle T_{6}={\rho}_{0}(x^{0})^{T}(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,x}-{\Sigma}_{x})x^{0},

\displaystyle T_{7}={\rho}_{0}(O_{3})_{11}(y^{0})^{T}(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,y}-{\Sigma}_{y})y^{0},

\displaystyle T_{8}=(O_{3})_{11}(x^{0})^{T}(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,xy}-{\Sigma}_{xy})y^{0},

\displaystyle T_{9}={\rho}_{0}(O_{3})_{11}(y^{0})^{T}(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,y}-{\Sigma}_{y})y^{0}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{1}^{T}{\Sigma}_{xy}y^{0}=

\displaystyle\ 2{\rho}_{0}\{1+(O_{4})_{11}\}(x^{0})^{T}(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,x}-{\Sigma}_{x})x^{0}+2{\rho}_{0}(O_{3})_{11}(y^{0})^{T}(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,y}-{\Sigma}_{y})y^{0}

\displaystyle\ -\{(O_{4})_{11}+(O_{3})_{11}+1\}(x^{0})^{T}(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,xy}-{\Sigma}_{xy})y^{0}.

By symmetry,

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{2}^{T}{\Sigma}_{yx}x^{0}=

\displaystyle\ 2{\rho}_{0}\{1+(O_{4})_{11}\}(y^{0})^{T}(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,y}-{\Sigma}_{y})y^{0}+2{\rho}_{0}(O_{3})_{11}(x^{0})^{T}(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,x}-{\Sigma}_{x})x^{0}

\displaystyle\ -\{(O_{4})_{11}+(O_{3})_{11}+1\}(x^{0})^{T}(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,xy}-{\Sigma}_{xy})y^{0}.

Noting

\displaystyle 2(1+(O_{4})_{11}+(O_{3})_{11})=2(1-5/8+1/8)=1,

we obtain

\displaystyle-\mathcal{L}_{1}^{T}{\Sigma}_{xy}y^{0}+\mathcal{-}L_{2}^{T}{\Sigma}_{yx}x^{0}+(x^{0})^{T}(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,xy}-\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,xy})y^{0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ -{\rho}_{0}\{(x^{0})^{T}(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,x}-{\Sigma}_{x})x^{0}-{\rho}_{0}(y^{0})^{T}(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,y}-{\Sigma}_{y})y^{0}\}+2(x^{0})^{T}(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,xy}-\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,xy})y^{0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ {\rho}_{0}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{(Z_{i}-E[Z_{i}])}{n}

\text{where}\quad Z_{i}=-{\rho}_{0}(X_{i}^{T}x^{0})^{2}-{\rho}_{0}(Y_{i}^{T}y^{0})^{2}+2(X_{i}^{T}x^{0})(Y_{i}^{T}y^{0}).

Proof 21.4
(of Lemma
18.2
).

Suppose
w_{1}=1
but
w_{2}=-1
. Fix
\epsilon>0
. Lemma
13.2
implies that if
n
is large enough, then

\|\widehat{x}_{n}-x^{0}\|_{1}+\|\widehat{y}_{n}+y^{0}\|_{1}<\epsilon,\quad\|\widehat{x}_{n}-x^{0}\|_{2}+\|\widehat{y}_{n}+y^{0}\|_{2}<\epsilon

with high probability.
Proceeding as in the proof of Lemma
13.1
, we can then show that if
n
is sufficiently large, then

\displaystyle|\widehat{x}_{n}^{T}\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,xy}\widehat{y}_{n}+x^{0}{\Sigma}_{xy}y^{0}|<C\epsilon,

where
C
is an absolute constant. Therefore,

\widehat{x}_{n}^{T}\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,xy}\widehat{y}_{n}<-{\rho}_{0}^{2}+C\epsilon
. Taking
\epsilon={\rho}_{0}^{2}/(2C)
, we can therefore show that

\limsup_{n}P(w_{1}=1,w_{2}=-1)\leq\limsup_{n}P(\widehat{x}_{n}^{T}\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,xy}\widehat{y}_{n}<-{\rho}_{0}^{2}/2).

However,
\widehat{x}_{n}^{T}\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,xy}\widehat{y}_{n}>0
for all
n
. Therefore,
P(w_{1}=1,w_{2}=-1)\to 0
. Similarly we can show that
P(w_{1}=-1,w_{2}=1)\to 0
, and the proof follows.

Proof 21.5
(of Lemma
18.3

).

Let us define
\widehat{x}_{n}^{*}=w_{1}\widehat{x}_{n}
and
\widehat{y}_{n}^{*}=w_{2}\widehat{y}_{n}
. Suppose
(\widehat{x}^{db}_{n},\widehat{y}^{db}_{n})
is the de-biased estimator constructed using
\widehat{x}_{n}
and
\widehat{y}_{n}
. Since
\widehat{\Phi}_{n}
does not depend on the sign of
\widehat{x}_{n}
and
\widehat{y}_{n}
, (
3.2
) and (
9
) indicate that if
w_{1}=w_{2}=w
, the de-biased estimators constructed using
\widehat{x}_{n}^{*}
and
\widehat{y}_{n}^{*}
equal
w\widehat{x}^{db}_{n}
and
w\widehat{y}^{db}_{n}
, respectively. Therefore,
the estimator
\widehat{\rho}_{n}^{2,\text{raw}}
constructed using
\widehat{x}_{n}^{*}
and
\widehat{y}_{n}^{*}
equals

\displaystyle w_{1}\widehat{x}_{n}^{T}\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,xy}w_{2}\widehat{y}^{db}_{n}+(w_{1}\widehat{x}^{db}_{n})^{T}\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,xy}w_{2}\widehat{y}_{n}-w_{1}\widehat{x}_{n}^{T}\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,xy}w_{2}\widehat{y}_{n}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ w^{2}\Big(\widehat{x}_{n}^{T}\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,xy}\widehat{y}^{db}_{n}+(\widehat{x}^{db}_{n})^{T}\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,xy}w_{2}\widehat{y}_{n}-\widehat{x}_{n}^{T}\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,xy}w_{2}\widehat{y}_{n}\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \widehat{x}_{n}^{T}\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,xy}\widehat{y}^{db}_{n}+(\widehat{x}^{db}_{n})^{T}\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,xy}w_{2}\widehat{y}_{n}-\widehat{x}_{n}^{T}\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,xy}w_{2}\widehat{y}_{n},

which is the
\widehat{\rho}_{n}^{2,\text{raw}}
constructed using
\widehat{x}_{n}
and
\widehat{y}_{n}
.

21.2
Proof of technical lemmas for Theorem
9.1

Proof 21.6
(of Lemma
19.2
).

We will first establish that
(u^{*})^{T}{\Sigma}_{x}u^{*}-{\rho}_{0}^{2}
is
o_{p}(1)
.
To that end, first we derive the expression of
(u^{*})^{T}{\Sigma}_{x}u^{*}
. Note that

\displaystyle(u^{*})^{T}{\Sigma}_{x}u^{*}=(\widehat{\beta}_{n}^{(0)})^{T}{\Sigma}_{y}V\Lambda U^{T}{\Sigma}_{x}U\Lambda V^{T}{\Sigma}_{y}\widehat{\beta}_{n}^{(0)}=(\widehat{\beta}_{n}^{(0)})^{T}{\Sigma}_{y}V\Lambda^{2}V^{T}{\Sigma}_{y}\widehat{\beta}_{n}^{(0)}

because
U^{T}{\Sigma}_{x}U=I_{r}
. Now let us denote

w=\argmin_{w^{\prime}\in\{\pm 1\}}\|w^{\prime}\widehat{\beta}_{n}^{(0)}-\beta_{0}\|_{2}.

Now

\displaystyle(\widehat{\beta}_{n}^{(0)})^{T}{\Sigma}_{y}V\Lambda^{2}V^{T}{\Sigma}_{y}\widehat{\beta}_{n}^{(0)}=\sum_{i=1}^{r}\Lambda_{i}^{2}((\widehat{\beta}_{n}^{(0)})^{T}{\Sigma}_{y}v_{i})^{2}=\sum_{i=1}^{r}\Lambda_{i}^{2}(w(\widehat{\beta}_{n}^{(0)})^{T}{\Sigma}_{y}v_{i})^{2}.

We have thus obtained

\displaystyle\absolutevalue{(\tg)^T\Sx\tg-\rhk^2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \absolutevalue{\sum_{i=1}^r\Lambda_i^2(\{\bk+w\hbz-\bk\}^T\Sy v_i)^2-\rhk^2 }

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \absolutevalue{\sum_{i=1}^r\Lambda_i^2\lbt(\bk^T\Sy v_i)^2 +\{(w\hbz-\bk)^T\Sy v_i\}^2+ 2(w\hbz-\bk)^T\Sy v_i(\bk^T\Sy v_i)\rbt-\rhk^2}.

(113)

Because
v_{1}=\beta_{0}
and
\Lambda_{1}={\rho}_{0}
, we have
\beta_{0}^{T}{\Sigma}_{y}v_{i}=0
for
i=2,\ldots,r
, leading to

\sum_{i=1}^{r}\Lambda_{i}^{2}(\beta_{0}^{T}{\Sigma}_{y}v_{i})^{2}-{\rho}_{0}^{2}=0.

Also, Cauchy Schwarz inequality implies that

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{r}\Lambda_{i}^{2}\{(w\widehat{\beta}_{n}^{(0)}-\beta_{0})^{T}{\Sigma}_{y}v_{i}\}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(w\widehat{\beta}_{n}^{(0)}-\beta_{0})^{T}{\Sigma}_{y}\bigg(\sum_{i=1}^{r}\Lambda_{i}^{2}v_{i}v_{i}^{T}\bigg){\Sigma}_{y}(w\widehat{\beta}_{n}^{(0)}-\beta_{0})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ (w\widehat{\beta}_{n}^{(0)}-\beta_{0})^{T}{\Sigma}_{y}V\Lambda^{2}V^{T}{\Sigma}_{y}(w\widehat{\beta}_{n}^{(0)}-\beta_{0})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\ \|{\Sigma}_{y}\|_{op}^{2}\|V\|_{op}^{2}\|\|\Lambda\|_{op}^{2}\|w\widehat{\beta}_{n}^{(0)}-\beta_{0}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\ M^{2}{\rho}_{0}^{2}\|w\widehat{\beta}_{n}^{(0)}-\beta_{0}\|^{2}_{2}

by Assumption
2.1
. Since
\|w\widehat{\beta}_{n}^{(0)}-\beta_{0}\|^{2}_{2}=O_{p}(s^{2}\lambda^{2})
by (
66
),
we have

\sum_{i=1}^{r}\Lambda_{i}^{2}\{(w\widehat{\beta}_{n}^{(0)}-\beta_{0})^{T}{\Sigma}_{y}v_{i}\}^{2}=O_{p}(s^{2}\lambda^{2}).

Finally, because
\beta_{0}^{T}{\Sigma}_{y}v_{i}=0
for
i\geq 2
,

\sum_{i=1}^{r}\Lambda_{i}^{2}\absolutevalue{(w\hbz-\bk)^T\Sy v_i(\bk^T\Sy v_i)}={\rho}_{0}^{2}\absolutevalue{(w\hbz-\bk)^T\Sy\bk}\leq M^{1/2}\|w\widehat{\beta}_{n}^{(0)}-\beta_{0}\|_{2},

where the last step follows from Cauchy Schwarz inequality, Assumption
2.1
and the fact that
\beta_{0}^{T}{\Sigma}_{y}\beta_{0}=1
. The right hand side of the above display is
o_{p}(1)
by (
66
). Thus we have established that the right hand side of (
21.6
) is
o_{p}(1)
. Assumption
2.1
then implies that

{\rho}_{0}/M^{1/2}-o_{p}(1)\leq\|u^{*}\|_{2}\leq{\rho}_{0}M^{1/2}+o_{p}(1),

which completes the proof.

Proof 21.7
(of Lemma
19.3
).

To show (
70
),
we first bound the difference

\displaystyle\|(\tilde{x}_{n}^{T}\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,x}^{(1)}\tilde{x}_{n})^{-1/2}\tilde{x}_{n}-(\tilde{x}_{n}^{T}{\Sigma}_{x}\tilde{x}_{n})^{-1/2}\tilde{x}_{n}\|_{2}=

\displaystyle\ \|\tilde{x}_{n}\|_{2}\absolutevalue{ (\tx^T\Sx\tx)^{-1/2}-(\tx^T\hSx^{(1)}\tx)^{-1/2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\ (\|\tilde{x}_{n}-u^{*}\|_{2}+\|u^{*}\|_{2})\frac{\absolutevalue{\tx^T(\hSx^{(1)}-\Sx)\tx}}{(\tilde{x}_{n}^{T}{\Sigma}_{x}\tilde{x}_{n})^{1/2}+(\tilde{x}_{n}^{T}\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,x}^{(1)}\tilde{x}_{n})^{1/2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\ (\|\tilde{x}_{n}-u^{*}\|_{2}+\|u^{*}\|_{2})\frac{\absolutevalue{\tx^T(\hSx^{(1)}-\Sx)\tx}}{(\tilde{x}_{n}^{T}{\Sigma}_{x}\tilde{x}_{n})^{1/2}}.

Now by Lemma
19.2
,
\|u^{*}\|_{2}=O_{p}(1)
. Also by (
79
), the difference term
\|\Delta\|_{2}=\|\tilde{x}_{n}-u^{*}\|_{2}
is
O_{p}(s_{U}^{1/2}\lambda)
, which is
o_{p}(1)
because
s_{U}^{1/2}\lambda\to 0
. Also, Assumption
2.1
implies

\tilde{x}_{n}^{T}{\Sigma}_{x}\tilde{x}_{n}\geq\|\tilde{x}_{n}\|_{2}/M
. Since
\|\tilde{x}_{n}-u^{*}\|_{2}=o_{p}(1)
, Lemma
19.2
implies that
(\tilde{x}_{n}^{T}{\Sigma}_{x}\tilde{x}_{n})^{-1/2}=O_{p}(1)
. Hence, we have derived that

\displaystyle\|(\tilde{x}_{n}^{T}\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,x}^{(1)}\tilde{x}_{n})^{-1/2}\tilde{x}_{n}-(\tilde{x}_{n}^{T}{\Sigma}_{x}\tilde{x}_{n})^{-1/2}\tilde{x}_{n}\|_{2}=O_{p}(1)\absolutevalue{\tx^T(\hSx^{(1)}-\Sx)\tx}.

(114)

Since
\Delta=\tilde{x}_{n}-u^{*}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\absolutevalue{\tx^T(\hSx^{(1)}-\Sx)\tx-(u^*)^T((\hSx^{(1)})-\Sx)u^*}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\ \absolutevalue{\Delta^T(\hSx^{(1)}-\Sx)\Delta}+2\absolutevalue{\Delta^T(\hSx-\Sx)u^*}

From Lemma
13.10
and the cone condition
76
it follows that

\absolutevalue{\Delta^T(\hSx^{(1)}-\Sx)\Delta}\leq s_{U}\|\Delta\|_{2}^{2}O_{p}(\lambda).

From (
79
) it follows that
\|\Delta\|^{2}_{2}=O_{p}(s_{U}\lambda^{2})
. Therefore,

\absolutevalue{\Delta^T(\hSx^{(1)}-\Sx)\Delta}=O_{p}(s_{U}^{2}\lambda^{3}),

which is
o_{p}(\lambda)
since
s_{U}\lambda\to 0
.
On the other hand

\absolutevalue{\Delta^T(\hSx^{(1)}-\Sx)u^*}\leq\|\Delta\|_{1}\|(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,x}^{(1)}-{\Sigma}_{x})u^{*}\|_{\infty}\leq\|\Delta\|_{1}\|u^{*}\|_{2}O_{p}(\lambda)

where the last inequality follows from Lemma
13.9
because
u^{*}
only depends on the first sample part, which is independent of
\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,x}^{(1)}
. On the other hand, (
76
) implies that

\|\Delta\|_{1}=O_{p}(s_{U}\lambda).

Since
\|u^{*}\|_{2}=O_{p}(1)
, by Lemma
19.2
,

\absolutevalue{\Delta^T(\hSx^{(1)}-\Sx)u^*}=O_{p}(s_{U}\lambda^{2}),

where the last term is
o_{p}(\lambda)
because
s_{U}\lambda\to 0
.

Combining all the pieces, we obtain that

\displaystyle\absolutevalue{\tx^T(\hSx^{(1)}-\Sx)\tx-(u^*)^T(\hSx^{(1)}-\Sx)u^*}=o_{P}(\lambda).

Now note that
u^{*}_{S_{U}^{c}}=U_{S_{U}^{c}}\Lambda V^{T}{\Sigma}_{y}\widehat{\beta}_{n}^{(0)}=0
. Therefore

\displaystyle(u^{*})^{T}(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,x}^{(1)}-{\Sigma}_{x})u^{*}=

\displaystyle\ (u^{*})^{T}(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,x}^{(1)}-{\Sigma}_{x})_{S_{U}\times S_{U}}u^{*}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\ \|(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,x}^{(1)}-{\Sigma}_{x})_{S_{U}\times S_{U}}\|_{op}\|u^{*}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ O_{p}((s_{U}\log(p)/n)^{1/2})\|u^{*}\|_{2}^{2}

by Theorem 5.31 of
Vershynin 2010

(
Gao et al. 2015
, see also Lemma 12 of)
. Because
\|u^{*}\|_{2}=O_{p}(1)
,
it follows that

\displaystyle\absolutevalue{\tx^T(\hSx^{(1)}-\Sx)\tx}=O_{p}((s_{U}\log(p)/n)^{1/2}).

(115)

Note that (
69
) follows from (
115
) because
\log p/n\leq\lambda^{2}
. Since
S\lambda\to 0
by our assumption on
s
, (
115
) implies

\tilde{x}_{n}^{T}\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,x}^{(1)}\tilde{x}_{n}=\tilde{x}_{n}^{T}{\Sigma}_{x}\tilde{x}_{n}+o_{p}(1)
.
Hence, by Assumption
2.1
,

\tilde{x}_{n}^{T}\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,x}^{(1)}\tilde{x}_{n}\geq\|\tilde{x}_{n}\|_{2}^{2}/M+o_{p}(1).

Noting

\|\tilde{x}_{n}\|_{2}\geq\|u^{*}\|_{2}-\|\Delta\|_{2}=\|u^{*}\|_{2}+o_{p}(1),

and using
Lemma
19.2
, we find that

\tilde{x}_{n}^{T}\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,x}^{(1)}\tilde{x}_{n}>{\rho}_{0}/(2M^{2})+o_{p}(1).

(116)

Hence (
21
) implies as
n\to\infty
,
\widehat{\alpha}_{n}=\tilde{x}_{n}(\tilde{x}_{n}^{T}\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,x}^{(1)}\tilde{x}_{n})^{-1/2}
with probability tending to one. Hence (
70
) is proved.
This fact implies, with high probability,

\|\widehat{\alpha}_{n}-(\widehat{x}_{n}^{T}{\Sigma}_{x}\widehat{x}_{n})^{-1/2}\widehat{x}_{n}\|_{2}=\|(\widehat{x}_{n}^{T}\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,x}\widehat{x}_{n})^{-1/2}\widehat{x}_{n}-(\widehat{x}_{n}^{T}{\Sigma}_{x}\widehat{x}_{n})^{-1/2}\widehat{x}_{n}\|_{2},

which, by
(
114
) and (
115
), is
O_{p}(s_{U}^{1/2}\lambda)
.
Thus (
71
) follows, which completes the proof.

Proof 21.8
(of Lemma
19.4
).

For sake of simplicity, we denote
z=\tilde{U}\Lambda(\tilde{V})^{T}y
.
Note that Fact
2
implies

\displaystyle\|P_{x}-P_{\tilde{u}_{1}}\|^{2}_{F}=2-2\text{tr}(P_{x}P_{\tilde{u}_{1}})\quad\text{and}\quad\|P_{x}-P_{z}\|^{2}_{F}=2-2\text{tr}(P_{x}P_{z}).

Therefore,

\|P_{x}-P_{\tilde{u}_{1}}\|_{F}^{2}-\|P_{x}-P_{z}\|_{2}^{2}=2\text{tr}(P_{x}P_{z})-2\text{tr}(P_{x}P_{\tilde{u}_{1}}).

(117)

Because
\tilde{u}_{1}
and
x
have unit norm,

\displaystyle\text{tr}(P_{x}P_{\tilde{u}_{1}})=\text{tr}(xx^{T}\tilde{u}_{1}\tilde{u}_{1}^{T})={(x^{T}\tilde{u}_{1})^{2}}.

Also since
\tilde{U}^{T}\tilde{U}=I_{r}
, we have

\displaystyle\text{tr}(P_{x}P_{z})=

\displaystyle\ \frac{\text{tr}\Big(xx^{T}\tilde{U}\Lambda V^{T}yy^{T}V\Lambda\tilde{U}^{T}\Big)}{x^{T}x\Big(y^{T}\tilde{V}\Lambda^{2}\tilde{V}^{T}y\Big)}=\frac{(x^{T}\tilde{U}\Lambda\tilde{V}^{T}y)^{2}}{\|\Lambda V^{T}y\|_{2}^{2}}\leq c{\|x^{T}\tilde{U}\|_{2}^{2}}

(118)

by Cauchy-Schwarz inequality. Therefore when
r=1
,
\tilde{U}=\tilde{u}_{1}
, and we have

tr(P_{x}P_{z})\leq tr(P_{x}P_{\tilde{u}_{1}})
, which, combined with (
117
), implies that

\|P_{x}-P_{\tilde{u}_{1}}\|_{F}^{2}\leq\|P_{x}-P_{z}\|_{F}^{2}
. This completes the proof of part A of the current lemma.

Now we turn our attention to part B of the current lemma. When
r\geq 2
, using (
118
), we obtain that

\text{tr}(P_{x}P_{z})\leq{\|x^{T}\tilde{U}\|_{2}^{2}}={\sum_{i=1}^{r}(x^{T}\tilde{u}_{i})^{2}}=tr(P_{x}P_{\tilde{u}_{1}})+{\sum_{i=2}^{r}(x^{T}\tilde{u}_{i})^{2}},

implying that for any
w\in\{\pm 1\}
,

\text{tr}(P_{x}P_{z})-\text{tr}(P_{x}P_{\tilde{u}_{1}})\leq{\sum_{i=2}^{r}(wx^{T}\tilde{u}_{i})^{2}}.

Therefore using (
117
), we can write

\|P_{x}-P_{\tilde{u}_{1}}\|_{F}^{2}-\|P_{x}-P_{z}\|_{F}^{2}\leq 2\inf_{w\in\{\pm 1\}}{\sum_{i=2}^{r}(wx^{T}\tilde{u}_{i})^{2}}.

Letting
\tilde{z}=z/\|z\|_{2}
, and noting
\tilde{u}_{1}^{T}\tilde{u}_{i}=0
for
i=2,\ldots,r
,
the term on the right hand side of (
117
) can be bounded since

\displaystyle 2\inf_{w\in\{\pm 1\}}{\sum_{i=2}^{r}(wx^{T}\tilde{u}_{i})^{2}}=

\displaystyle\ 2\inf_{w,w^{\prime}\in\{\pm 1\}}{\sum_{i=2}^{r}\Big((wx-z)^{T}\tilde{u}_{i}+(z-w^{\prime}\tilde{u}_{1})^{T}\tilde{u}_{i}\Big)^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\ 4\inf_{w,w^{\prime}\in\{\pm 1\}}\sum_{i=2}^{r}\bigg\{((wx-\tilde{z})^{T}\tilde{u}_{i})^{2}+((w^{\prime}\tilde{z}-\tilde{u}_{1})^{T}\tilde{u}_{i})^{2}\bigg\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ 4\inf_{w\in\{\pm 1\}}\sum_{i=2}^{r}((wx-\tilde{z})^{T}\tilde{u}_{i})^{2}+4\inf_{w^{\prime}\in\{\pm 1\}}\sum_{i=2}^{r}((w^{\prime}\tilde{z}-\tilde{u}_{1})^{T}\tilde{u}_{i})^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\ 4\inf_{w\in\{\pm 1\}}\|wx-\tilde{z}\|_{2}^{2}+4\inf_{w\in\{\pm 1\}}\|w\tilde{z}-\tilde{u}_{1}\|_{2}^{2}

where the last step follows because
\tilde{u}_{i}
’s are orthogonal vectors. Since
x
and
\tilde{u}_{1}
have unit norm, by
Fact
4
,

\inf_{w\in\{\pm 1\}}{\|wx-\tilde{z}\|_{2}^{2}}\leq\|P_{x}-P_{\tilde{z}}\|_{F}^{2}=\|P_{x}-P_{z}\|_{F}^{2}

because
P_{\tilde{z}}=P_{z}
.
Therefore,

\|P_{x}-P_{\tilde{u}_{1}}\|_{F}^{2}\leq 5\|P_{x}-P_{z}\|^{2}_{F}+4\inf_{w\in\{\pm 1\}}\|w\tilde{z}-\tilde{u}_{1}\|_{2}^{2}.

(119)

Next we will bound
\inf_{w^{\prime}\in\{\pm 1\}}\|w\tilde{z}-\tilde{u}_{1}\|_{2}
using the rate of decay of
\|wy-\tilde{v}_{1}\|_{2}
. To this end, we first show that
\|z\|_{2}
is asymptotically equivalent to
{\rho}_{0}
.
Noting
z=\tilde{U}\Lambda\tilde{V}^{T}y
, for any
w\in\{\pm 1\}
, we have

\displaystyle\|z\|^{2}-{\rho}_{0}^{2}=

\displaystyle\ y^{T}\tilde{V}\Lambda^{2}\tilde{V}^{T}y-\tilde{v}_{1}^{T}\tilde{V}\Lambda^{2}\tilde{V}^{T}\tilde{v}_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ wy^{T}\tilde{V}\Lambda^{2}\tilde{V}^{T}yw-\tilde{v}_{1}^{T}\tilde{V}\Lambda^{2}\tilde{V}^{T}\tilde{v}_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ (wy-\tilde{v}_{1})^{T}\tilde{V}\Lambda^{2}\tilde{V}^{T}wy+(wy-\tilde{v}_{1})^{T}\tilde{V}\Lambda^{2}\tilde{V}^{T}\tilde{v}_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ (wy-\tilde{v}_{1})^{T}\tilde{V}\Lambda^{2}\tilde{V}^{T}(wy-\tilde{v}_{1})+2(wy-\tilde{v}_{1})^{T}\tilde{V}\Lambda^{2}\tilde{V}^{T}\tilde{v}_{1},

which implies

\displaystyle|\|z\|^{2}-{\rho}_{0}^{2}|\leq

\displaystyle\ \|\tilde{V}\Lambda^{2}\tilde{V}^{T}\|_{op}\inf_{w\in\{\pm 1\}}(\|wy-\tilde{v}_{1}\|_{2}^{2}+2\|\tilde{v}_{1}\|_{2}\|wy-\tilde{v}_{1}\|_{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ {\rho}_{0}^{2}O_{p}(s\lambda)

because
\inf_{w}\|wy-\tilde{v}_{1}\|_{2}=O_{p}(s\lambda)
and
s\lambda\to 0
by our assumption.
Therefore, it also follows that

\absolutevalue{\|z\|_2^{-1}-\rhk^{-1}}=\frac{\absolutevalue{\|z\|_2-\rhk}}{\|z\|_{2}{\rho}_{0}}\leq\frac{\absolutevalue{\|z\|^2_2-\rhk^2}}{\|z\|_{2}{\rho}_{0}(\|z\|_{2}+{\rho}_{0})}\leq\frac{O_{p}(s\lambda)}{{\rho}_{0}^{2}({\rho}_{0}-O_{p}(s\lambda))},

which is
O_{p}(s\lambda)
because
s\lambda\to 0
and
{\rho}_{0}>0
. Hence,

\displaystyle\inf_{w\in\{\pm 1\}}\|w\tilde{z}-\tilde{u}_{1}\|_{2}=

\displaystyle\ \inf_{w\in\{\pm 1\}}\|\tilde{U}\Lambda\tilde{V}^{T}(\|z\|^{-1}wy)-\tilde{U}\Lambda\tilde{V}^{T}({\rho}_{0}^{-1}\tilde{v}_{1})\|_{F}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\ \inf_{w\in\{\pm 1\}}\|\tilde{U}\Lambda\tilde{V}^{T}\|_{op}\norm{\|z\|_2^{-1}wy-\rhk^{-1}\tv_1}_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ {\rho}_{0}\inf_{w\in\{\pm 1\}}\norm{ \|z\|_2^{-1}wy-\rhk^{-1}\tv_1}_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\ \|z\|_{2}^{-1}{\rho}_{0}\inf_{w\in\{\pm 1\}}\|wy-\tilde{v}_{1}\|_{2}+(\|z\|_{2}^{-1}-{\rho}_{0}^{-1})\|\tilde{v}_{1}\|_{2},

which is
O_{p}(s\lambda)
since
\inf_{w\in\{\pm 1\}}\|wy-\tilde{v}_{1}\|_{2}=O_{p}(s\lambda)
by our assumption and we just showed that
\|z\|_{2}^{-1}={\rho}_{0}^{-1}+O_{p}(s\lambda)
.
The proof then follows noting (
119
) implies

\|P_{x}-P_{\tilde{u}_{1}}\|_{F}^{2}\leq 5\|P_{x}-P_{z}\|^{2}_{F}+O_{p}(s\lambda).

21.3
Proof of technical lemmas for Theorem
19.1

Proof 21.9
(of Lemma
19.6
).

Since the eigenvalues of
{\Sigma}_{x}
and
{\Sigma}_{y}
are bounded below by Assumption
2.1
, it suffices to prove that

\|{\Sigma}_{x}^{1/2}(\tilde{F}_{n}-F_{0}){\Sigma}_{y}^{1/2}\|_{F}=O_{p}(\epsilon_{n,u}+\epsilon_{n,v}).

To that end, note that

\displaystyle\|{\Sigma}_{x}^{1/2}(\tilde{F}_{n}-F_{0}){\Sigma}_{y}^{1/2}\|_{F}=

\displaystyle\ \|{\Sigma}_{x}^{1/2}(\overline{\alpha}\overline{\beta}^{T}-\alpha_{0}\beta_{0}^{T}){\Sigma}_{y}^{1/2}\|_{F}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\ \|{\Sigma}_{x}^{1/2}\overline{\alpha}(\overline{\beta}-\beta_{0})^{T}{\Sigma}_{y}^{1/2}\|_{F}+\|{\Sigma}_{x}^{1/2}(\overline{\alpha}-\alpha_{0})\beta_{0}^{T}{\Sigma}_{y}^{1/2}\|_{F}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\ \|{\Sigma}_{x}^{1/2}\overline{\alpha}\|_{2}\|{\Sigma}_{y}^{1/2}(\overline{\beta}-\beta_{0})\|_{2}+\|{\Sigma}_{y}^{1/2}\beta_{0}\|_{2}\|{\Sigma}_{x}^{1/2}(\overline{\alpha}-\alpha_{0})\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\ (\|{\Sigma}_{x}^{1/2}(\overline{\alpha}-\alpha_{0})\|_{2}+\|{\Sigma}_{x}^{1/2}\alpha_{0}\|_{2})\|{\Sigma}_{y}^{1/2}(\overline{\beta}-\beta_{0})\|_{2}

\displaystyle\ +\|{\Sigma}_{x}^{1/2}(\overline{\alpha}-\alpha_{0})\|_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ (\|{\Sigma}_{x}^{1/2}(\overline{\alpha}-\alpha_{0})\|_{2}+1)\|{\Sigma}_{y}^{1/2}(\overline{\beta}-\beta_{0})\|_{2}+\|{\Sigma}_{x}^{1/2}(\overline{\alpha}-\alpha_{0})\|_{2}

Because
\overline{\alpha}=\tilde{U}_{1}
and
\alpha_{0}=U_{1}
, we can write

\displaystyle\|{\Sigma}_{x}^{1/2}(\overline{\alpha}-\alpha_{0})\|_{2}=\|{\Sigma}_{x}^{1/2}(\tilde{U}-U)e_{1}\|_{2}\leq\|{\Sigma}_{x}^{1/2}(\tilde{U}-U)\|_{op}

which is
O_{p}(\epsilon_{n,u})
by Lemma 6.1 of
Gao et al. 2017
. Similarly, we can show that
\|{\Sigma}_{y}^{1/2}(\overline{\beta}-\beta_{0})\|_{2}
is
O_{p}(\epsilon_{n,v})
, which completes the proof.

Proof 21.10
(of Lemma
19.7
).

Consider
A=(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,x}^{(0)})^{1/2}\tilde{F}_{n}(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,y}^{(0)})^{1/2}
.
Since
\tilde{F}_{n}=\overline{\alpha}\overline{\beta}^{T}
,
\|(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,x}^{(0)})^{1/2}\overline{\alpha}\|_{2}=1
, and
\|(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,y}^{(0)})^{1/2}\overline{\beta}\|_{2}=1
,

\|A\|_{op}=\|(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,x}^{(0)})^{1/2}\tilde{F}_{n}(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,x}^{(0)})^{1/2}\|_{op}\leq\|(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,x}^{(0)})^{1/2}\overline{\alpha}\|_{2}\|(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,y}^{(0)})^{1/2}\overline{\beta}\|_{2}=1.

Also, by definition of operator norm, we have

\|A\|_{op}\geq((\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,x}^{(0)})^{1/2}\overline{\alpha})^{T}A((\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,y}^{(0)})^{1/2}\overline{\beta})=1.

Therefore,
\|A\|_{op}=1
.
Second,

A^{T}A=(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,y}^{(0)})^{1/2}\overline{\beta}\overline{\alpha}^{T}\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,x}^{(0)}\overline{\alpha}\overline{\beta}^{T}(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,y}^{(0)})^{1/2}=(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,y}^{(0)})^{1/2}\overline{\beta}\overline{\beta}^{T}(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,y}^{(0)})^{1/2}.

Therefore,

tr(A^{T}A)=tr(\overline{\beta}^{T}\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,y}^{(0)}\overline{\beta})=\overline{\beta}^{T}\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,y}^{(0)}\overline{\beta}=1.

Hence,
A
has only one non-zero singular value, which is one. Thus
\|A\|_{*}=1
.

Proof 21.11
(of Lemma
19.8
).

First note that

\displaystyle\absolutevalue{ \langle A (\tilde D-D)G^T, AEG^T-F\rangle}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \absolutevalue{\tr(G (\tilde D-D)^T A^T(A E G^T-F))}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{\leq}}

\displaystyle\ \|A(\tilde{D}-D)G^{T}\|_{F}\|AEG^{T}-F\|_{F}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(b)}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\ \|\tilde{D}-D\|_{F}\|AEG^{T}-F\|_{F}

where (a) follows because by Cauchy Schwarz inequality and (b) follows
because the Frobenius norm is unitarily invariant
(
Chen et al. 2020
, cf. p. 26)
and
A
and
G
are unitary matrices.
Therefore

\displaystyle\langle A\tilde{D}G^{T},AEG^{T}-F\rangle\geq\langle ADG^{T},AEG^{T}-F\rangle-\|\tilde{D}-D\|_{F}\|AEG^{T}-F\|_{F}.

(120)

Let us denote
c_{i}=A_{i}^{T}FG_{i}

(i=1,\ldots,r)
. We will first show that

\|AEG^{T}-F\|_{F}^{2}\leq 2(1-c_{1})
and then we will show that
\langle ADG^{T},AEG^{T}-F\rangle\geq d_{12}(1-c_{1})
, from which, the proof will show.
For the upper bound on
\|AEG^{T}-F\|_{F}^{2}
, notice that

\displaystyle\|AEG^{T}-F\|_{F}^{2}=

\displaystyle\ \text{tr}\Big((AEG^{T}-F)^{T}(AEG^{T}-F)\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \text{tr}(GE^{2}G^{T})+\|F\|_{F}^{2}-2\text{tr}(F^{T}AEG^{T})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{\leq}}

\displaystyle\text{tr}(E)+\|F\|_{*}^{2}-2Tr(EA^{T}FG)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(b)}}{{\leq}}

\displaystyle\ 2(1-c_{1}).

(121)

Here (a) follows because nuclear norm is greater than the Frobenius norm, and
E^{2}=E
and
G\in\mathcal{O}(q,r)
. Also (b) follows because (i)
\text{tr}(E)=\text{tr}(e_{1}e_{1}^{T})=1
, (ii)
\|F\|_{*}\leq 1
by our assumption on
F
, and (iii)
tr(EA^{T}FG)=tr(e_{1}^{T}A^{T}FGe_{1})=A_{1}^{T}FG_{1}=c_{1}
. We have used the relation
\text{tr}(AB)=\text{tr}(BA)
here.

Now we will establish the lower bound
\langle ADG^{T},AEG^{T}-F\rangle\geq d_{12}(1-c_{1})
. To that end, first note that

\displaystyle\langle ADG^{T},AEG^{T}-F\rangle=

\displaystyle\ \text{tr}(GDA^{T}AEG^{T})-tr(GDA^{T}F)

(122)

It follows that because
A
and
G
are unitary, the first term of (
122
)

\displaystyle\text{tr}(GDA^{T}AEG^{T})=\text{tr}(DEG^{T}G)=\text{tr}(DE)=\text{tr}(e_{1}^{T}De_{1})=D_{11}.

(123)

We will bound the second term of (
122
) by
D_{22}
.To that end, recalling

d_{12}=D_{11}-D_{22}
, and
denoting
D^{\prime}=Diag(0,D_{22},D_{22},D_{33},\ldots,D_{rr})
,
we write
D=d_{12}e_{1}e_{1}^{T}+D^{\prime}
. Hence,

\displaystyle\text{tr}(GDA^{T}F)=

\displaystyle\ d_{12}\text{tr}(Ge_{1}e_{1}^{T}A^{T}F)+\text{tr}(GD^{\prime}A^{T}F)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ d_{12}\text{tr}(e_{1}^{T}A^{T}FGe_{1})+\text{tr}(GD^{\prime}A^{T}F)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ d_{12}c_{1}+\text{tr}(GD^{\prime}A^{T}F)

(124)

Consider an SVD
U_{1}\Lambda^{\prime}V_{1}^{T}
of
F
, which means
U_{1}\in\mathcal{O}(p,r)
,
V_{1}\in\mathcal{O}(q,r)
and
\Lambda^{\prime}=diag(\Lambda^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,\Lambda_{r})
is the diagonal matrix whose diagonal entries are the singular values of
F
. Then

\displaystyle\text{tr}(GD^{\prime}A^{T}F)=

\displaystyle\ \text{tr}(GD^{\prime}A^{T}U_{1}\Lambda^{\prime}V_{1}^{T})=\text{tr}(V_{1}^{T}GD^{\prime}A^{T}U_{1}\Lambda^{\prime})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \sum_{i=1}^{r}e_{i}^{T}V_{1}^{T}GD^{\prime}A^{T}U_{1}\Lambda^{\prime}e_{i}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \sum_{i=1}^{r}(V_{1}^{T}GD^{\prime}A^{T}U_{1}e_{i})^{T}\Lambda_{i}^{\prime}e_{i}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \sum_{i=1}^{r}e_{i}^{T}U_{1}^{T}AD^{\prime}G^{T}V_{1}e_{i}\Lambda^{\prime}_{i}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\ \sup_{1\leq i\leq r}|e_{i}^{T}U_{1}^{T}AD^{\prime}G^{T}V_{1}e_{i}|\sum_{i=1}^{r}\Lambda^{\prime}_{i}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{\leq}}

\displaystyle\ \|U_{1}^{T}AD^{\prime}G^{T}V_{1}\|_{op}\|F\|_{*}

Here (a) uses the fact that
\|F\|_{*}=\sum_{i=1}^{r}\Lambda^{\prime}_{i}
is the sum of the singular values of
F
. Since
U_{1}
,
V_{1}
,
A
, and
G
are unitary matrices, and
\|F\|_{*}\leq 1
by our assumption, the above calculations lead to

\text{tr}(GD^{\prime}A^{T}F)\leq\|U_{1}^{T}AD^{\prime}G^{T}V_{1}\|_{op}\|F\|_{*}\leq\|D^{\prime}\|_{op}\leq D_{22}

(125)

by definition of
D^{\prime}
.
Combining (
122
), (
123
), (
21.11
), and (
125
), we obtain

\langle ADG^{T},AEG^{T}-F\rangle=tr(GDA^{T}AEG^{T})-tr(GDA^{T}F)\geq D_{11}-d_{12}c_{1}-D_{22}=d_{12}(1-c_{1}),

which, in conjunction with (
120
) and (
21.11
), completes the proof.

21.4
Proof of technical lemmas and claims for Supplement
10

Proof 21.12
(of Lemma
20.1
).

Lemma
3.4
implies
\Lambda_{max}(\Phi^{0})=(\Lambda_{min}(H^{0}))^{-1}\leq 2^{-1}M/({\rho}_{0}-\Lambda_{2})
.
Let us denote
\tilde{u}_{i}={\Sigma}_{x}^{1/2}u_{i}

and
\tilde{v}_{i}={\Sigma}_{y}^{1/2}v_{i}

(i=1,\ldots,r)
.
Recall from (
36
) in the proof of Lemma
3.4
that

H^{0}=2{\rho}_{0}D\underbrace{\begin{bmatrix}I_{p}+2\tilde{u}_{1}\tilde{u}_{1}^{T}&-{\Sigma}_{x}^{-1/2}{\Sigma}_{xy}{\Sigma}_{y}^{-1/2}/{\rho}_{0}\\
-{\Sigma}_{y}^{-1/2}{\Sigma}_{yx}{\Sigma}_{x}^{-1/2}/{\rho}_{0}&I_{q}+2\tilde{v}_{1}\tilde{v}_{1}^{T}\end{bmatrix}}_{A}D

where
D=Diag({\Sigma}_{x}^{1/2},{\Sigma}_{y}^{1/2})
. Note that

\Lambda_{max}(H^{0})\leq 2{\rho}_{0}\|D\|^{2}_{op}\|A\|_{op}
.
From the proof of Lemma
3.4
it follows that follows that
\|D\|_{op}\leq M^{1/2}
and
\|A\|_{op}=4
. Therefore,

\Lambda_{max}(H^{0})\leq 8{\rho}_{0}M
and
\Lambda_{min}(H^{0})\geq(8{\rho}_{0}M)^{-1}
.
For
\tau_{j}^{2}
, note that

(\tau_{j}^{2})^{-1}=\Phi^{0}_{j,j}\leq\|\Phi^{0}\|_{op}\leq 2^{-1}M/({\rho}_{0}-\Lambda_{2}),

which implies

\tau_{j}^{2}\geq 2({\rho}_{0}-\Lambda_{2})/M.

Proof 21.13
(of Lemma
20.2
).

That

\max_{1\leq j\leq p+q}\|\Gamma^{0}_{j}\|_{0}
is
O(s)
follows from Condition
4.1
. For the
l_{2}
-norm, note that

\max_{1\leq j\leq p+q}\|\Gamma^{0}_{j}\|^{2}_{2}=1+\max_{1\leq j\leq p+q}\|{\eta^{0}}_{j}\|_{2}^{2}.

Equation
28
implies

\|{\eta^{0}}_{j}\|_{2}\leq\|H^{0}_{-j,-j}\|_{op}\|H_{-j,j}^{0}\|_{2}.

Since
\|H^{0}_{-j,-j}\|_{op}\leq\|H^{0}\|_{op}

and

\|H_{-j,j}^{0}\|^{2}_{2}\leq\|H^{0}_{j}\|_{2}^{2}=\|H^{0}e_{j}\|_{2}^{2}\leq\|H^{0}\|_{op}^{2},

the proof follows from
by Lemma
20.1
.

Proof 21.14
(of Claim
10
).

From the definition of
\Delta_{\Gamma,1}(j)
and
\Delta_{\Gamma}(j)
, it follows that
\sup_{i\in\{1,2\}}\|\Delta_{\Gamma,i}(j)\|_{p}\leq\|\Delta_{\Gamma}(j)\|_{k}
for
k=1,2
. Since the
j
th element of
\Delta_{\Gamma}(j)=0
,

\|\Delta_{\Gamma}(j)\|_{k}^{k}=\|\Delta(j)\|_{k}^{k},

which indicates that there exists absolute constant
C
so that

\max_{i\in\{1,2\}}\|\Delta(j)_{\Gamma,i}\|_{k}\leq C\|\Delta(j)\|_{k},\quad(k=1,2,\ j=1:(p+q)).

(126)

The above relation will be used often times without stating throughout the proof.

We make note of some facts first. First,

|\widehat{x}_{n}^{T}\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,x}\widehat{x}_{n}-{\rho}_{0}|=O_{p}(s^{\kappa}\lambda)

(127)

by Lemma
13.3
. Next, we want to derive a bound on
\Delta_{\Gamma,1}^{T}(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,x}-{\Sigma}_{x})\Delta_{\Gamma,1}
using Lemma
13.12
. To apply this lemma, we have to show that
\|\Delta_{\Gamma,1}(j)\|_{1}=O(s^{1/2})
. To that end, we show that both
\max_{1\leq j\leq p+q}\|\Gamma_{j}^{0}\|_{1}
and
\max_{1\leq j\leq p+q}\|\widehat{\Gamma}_{j}^{0}\|_{1}
are
O_{p}(s^{1/2})
. Because
\|\Gamma_{j}^{0}\|_{1}=1+\|{\eta^{0}}_{j}\|_{1}
, Assumption
10.4
implies
\max_{1\leq j\leq p+q}\|\widehat{\Gamma}_{j}^{0}\|_{1}=O(s^{1/2})
. On the other hand,
\widehat{\Gamma}_{j}
is a solution to (
23
), and therefore
\|\widehat{\Gamma}_{j}^{0}\|_{1}\leq B_{j}
. Since we have taken
B_{j}\leq C_{T}s^{1/2}
, we also have
\max_{1\leq j\leq p+q}\|\widehat{\Gamma}_{j}\|_{1}\leq C_{T}s^{1/2}
. Thus

\max_{1\leq j\leq p+q}\|\Delta_{\Gamma}(j)\|_{1}\leq C^{\prime}s^{1/2}.

(128)

Hence, Lemma
13.12
implies that there exists constant
C>0
depending only on
C^{\prime}
and the distribution of
X
so that so that for large
p
and
n
,

\absolutevalue{ \Delta_{\Gamma,1}(j)^T(\hSx-\Sx)\Delta_{\Gamma,1}(j)}\leq C(s^{1/2}\lambda\|\Delta_{\Gamma,1}(j)\|_{2}^{2}+\lambda\|\Delta(j)\|_{1})\quad(j=1:\ldots,p+q).

(129)

with high probability.

Third, by Lemma
13.15
and Lemma
13.7
, the following holds with high probability for a constant
C
again not depending on
j

:

\displaystyle\absolutevalue{ \hx^T(\hSx-\Sx)\Delta_{\Gamma,1}(j)}\leq C\|\Delta_{\Gamma,1}(j)\|_{1}\|x^{0}\|_{2}\lambda\quad(j=1:\ldots,p+q).

(130)

Fourth, Assumption
2.1
implies that

\displaystyle|\widehat{x}_{n}^{T}{\Sigma}_{x}\Delta_{\Gamma,1}(j)|\leq M\|\widehat{x}_{n}\|_{2}\|\Delta_{\Gamma,1}(j)\|_{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{\leq}}MC\|\Delta_{\Gamma,1}(j)\|_{2}\quad(j=1,\ldots,p+q)

(131)

with high probability where (a) follows by Lemma
13.2
and Lemma
13.7
.
Finally, by Assumption
2.1
,

\max_{1\leq j\leq p+q}\Delta_{\Gamma,1}(j)^{T}{\Sigma}_{x}\Delta_{\Gamma,1}(j)/\|\Delta_{\Gamma,1}(j)\|_{2}^{2}\leq\|{\Sigma}_{x}\|_{op}=M.

(132)

For the rest of the lemma, the constant
C
does not depend on
j
.
Using (
127
) and (
129
), we can a find
C
so that

\displaystyle|T_{1}(\Delta(j);j)|=

\displaystyle\ 2\absolutevalue{(\hx^T\hSx\hx-\rhk)\Delta_{\Gamma,1}(j)^T(\hSx-\Sx)\Delta_{\Gamma,1}(j)}

\displaystyle\ \leq C\Big(s^{\kappa+1/2}\lambda^{2}\|\Delta_{\Gamma,1}(j)\|_{2}^{2}+s^{\kappa}\lambda^{2}\|\Delta_{\Gamma,1}(j)\|_{1}\Big)\quad(j=1:\ldots,p+q).

with high probability for sufficiently large
n
,
p
, and
q
.
Using (
127
) and (
132
), for
T_{2}(\Delta(j);j)
, we can choose
C
to be so large such that for sufficiently large
n
,
p
, and
q
,

\displaystyle|T_{2}(\Delta(j);j)|=

\displaystyle\ 2\absolutevalue{(\hx^T\hSx\hx-\rhk)\Delta_{\Gamma,1}(j)^T\Sx\Delta_{\Gamma,1}(j)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\ Cs^{\kappa}\lambda\|\Delta_{\Gamma,1}(j)\|_{2}^{2}\quad(j=1:\ldots,p+q)

with high probability.
Also, by (
129
), we can obtain a large enough
C
so that

\displaystyle|T_{3}(\Delta(j);j)|=

\displaystyle\ 2\absolutevalue{\Delta_{\Gamma,1}(j)^T(\hSx-\Sx)\Delta_{\Gamma,1}(j)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\ C(s^{1/2}\lambda\|\Delta_{\Gamma,1}(j)\|_{2}^{2}+\lambda\|\Delta_{\Gamma,1}(j)\|_{1})\quad(j=1:\ldots,p+q)

with high probability for large
p
,
q
, and
n
.
Next, observe that (
130
) implies

T_{4}(\Delta(j);j)=4\Big(\widehat{x}_{n}^{T}(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,x}-{\Sigma}_{x})\Delta_{\Gamma,1}(j)\Big)^{2}\leq C\lambda^{2}\|x^{0}\|_{2}^{2}\|\Delta_{\Gamma,1}(j)\|^{2}_{1}\quad(j=1:\ldots,p+q)

with high probability for large
p
,
q
, and
n
.
Since
\max_{1\leq j\leq p+q}\|\Delta_{\Gamma,1}(j)\|_{1}=O_{p}(s^{1/2})
by (
128
) and
s\lambda\to 0
by Fact
1
,

T_{4}(\Delta(j);j)=\lambda\|\Delta_{\Gamma,1}(j)\|_{1}o_{p}(1)
uniformly across the
j
’s.

Now note that

|T_{5}(\Delta(j);j)|=|T_{6}(\Delta_{\Gamma,1}(j))|=4\absolutevalue{\Delta_{\Gamma,1}(j)^T(\hSx-\Sx)\hx\hx^T\Sx\Delta_{\Gamma,1}(j)}.

Using (
130
) and (
131
), we find that for
j=1,\ldots,p+q
,

|T_{5}(\Delta(j);j)|=|T_{6}(\Delta(j);j)|\leq C\lambda\|\Delta_{\Gamma,1}(j)\|_{2}\|\Delta_{\Gamma,1}(j)\|_{1}

for some
C>0
for large
p
,
q
, and
n
.
For
T_{7}(\Delta_{j};j)
, we observe that

|T_{7}(\Delta(j);j)|=4\Delta_{\Gamma,1}(j)^{T}{\Sigma}_{x}(\widehat{x}_{n}\widehat{x}_{n}^{T}-\xi^{0}(\xi^{0})^{T}){\Sigma}_{x}\Delta_{\Gamma,1}(j)\leq M^{2}\|\Delta_{\Gamma,1}(j)\|_{2}^{2}\|\widehat{x}_{n}\widehat{x}_{n}^{T}-x^{0}(x^{0})^{T}\|_{F},

where by Fact
5
and Lemma
13.2
,

\|\widehat{x}_{n}\widehat{x}_{n}^{T}-x^{0}(x^{0})^{T}\|_{F}\leq\frac{\inf_{w\in\{\pm 1\}}\|w\widehat{x}_{n}-x^{0}\|_{2}}{\|x^{0}\|_{2}}=O_{p}(s^{\kappa}\lambda).

(133)

Hence, uniformly over
j=1,\ldots,p+q
,

|T_{7}(\Delta(j);j)|=O_{p}(s^{\kappa}\lambda)\|\Delta_{\Gamma,1}(j)\|_{2}^{2}.

Lemma
13.13
implies that the following holds uniformly over all
j=1,\ldots,p+q
,

T_{8}(\Delta(j);j)=\Delta_{\Gamma,1}(j)^{T}({\Sigma}_{xy}-\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,xy})\Delta_{\Gamma,2}(j)=O_{p}\Big(s^{1/2}\lambda\|\Delta_{\Gamma}(j)\|_{2}^{2}+\lambda\|\Delta_{\Gamma}(j)\|_{1}\Big).

Combining the above pieces, and using the fact that
\|\Delta_{\Gamma}(j)\|_{i}=\|\Delta(j)\|_{i}
for
i\in\mathbb{N}
, we conclude that there exists a
C>0
not depending on
j
such that the following holds with high probability:

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{8}|T_{i}(\Delta(j);j)|\leq

\displaystyle\ C\Big[\{(s+s^{\kappa+1/2})\lambda+s^{1/2}+s^{\kappa}\}\lambda\|\Delta(j)\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\ +(s^{\kappa}\lambda^{2}+\lambda)\|\Delta(j)\|_{1}+\lambda\|\Delta(j)\|_{1}\|\Delta(j)\|_{2}\Big]\quad(j=1,\ldots,p+q).

Now since
\kappa\in[1/2,1]
by Condition
4.1
,
s^{\kappa}=O(s)
.
Because
s\lambda=o(1)
by Fact
1
,

\{(s+s^{\kappa+1/2})\lambda+(s^{1/2}+s^{\kappa})\}\lambda=O(s^{\kappa}\lambda),

s^{\kappa}\lambda^{2}+\lambda\leq\lambda(s\lambda+1)=O(\lambda).

Finally, noting
\max_{1\leq j\leq p+q}\|\Delta(j)\|_{1}=O_{p}(s^{1/2})
by (
128
), we have

\lambda\|\Delta(j)\|_{1}\|\Delta(j)\|_{2}=O_{p}(s^{1/2}\lambda\|\Delta(j)\|_{2}).

Thus
C
can be chosen so that for all sufficiently large
p
,
q
,
n
,

\sum_{i=1}^{8}|T_{i}(\Delta_{\Gamma}(j);j)|\leq C\Big(s^{\kappa}\lambda\|\Delta(j)\|_{2}^{2}+\lambda\|\Delta(j)\|_{1}+s^{1/2}\lambda\|\Delta(j)\|_{2}\Big)\quad(j=1,\ldots,p+q),

with high probability, which completes the proof.

Proof 21.15
(of Claim
11
).

The proof techniques of the current claim will be similar to that of Claim
10
.
We will make often use of the following relation stated in (
126
) of Claim
10
:

\max_{i\in\{1,2\}}\|\Delta(j)_{\Gamma,i}\|_{k}\leq C\|\Delta(j)\|_{k},\quad(k=1,2,\ j=1:(p+q)).

First, using Lemma
13.15
we find that there exists
C>0
so that

\absolutevalue{\Delta_{\Gamma,1}(j)^T(\hSx-\Sx)z_1}\leq C\|\Delta_{\Gamma,1}(j)\|_{1}\|z\|_{2}\lambda\quad(j=1,\ldots,p)

(134)

with high probability for sufficiently large
n
.
Second,
from Assumption
2.1
it follows that

\absolutevalue{\Delta_{\Gamma,1}(j)^T\Sx z_1}\leq M\|z\|_{2}\|\Delta_{\Gamma,1}(j)\|_{2}.

(135)

Third, noting that Lemma
13.2
implies
\inf_{w\in\{\pm 1\}}\|w\widehat{x}_{n}-x^{0}\|_{1}=O_{p}(s^{\kappa+1/2}\lambda)
, and
s^{\kappa+1/2}\lambda\to 0
by Fact
1
, we can find a large enough
C>0
so that with high probability,

\absolutevalue{\hx^T(\hSx-\Sx)z_1}=\inf_{w\in\{\pm 1\}}\absolutevalue{w\hx^T(\hSx-\Sx)z_1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{\leq}}\|x^{0}\|_{1}\|z\|_{2}\lambda\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(b)}}{{\leq}}Cs^{1/2}\|z\|_{2}\lambda.

(136)

where (a) follows from Lemma
13.15

and (b) follows because

\|x^{0}\|_{1}={\rho}_{0}^{1/2}\|\alpha_{0}\|_{1}\leq s^{1/2}\|\alpha_{0}\|_{2}

which is
O(s^{1/2})
by Lemma
13.7
.
On the other hand, by Assumption
2.1
,

\absolutevalue{\hx^T\Sx z_1}\leq M\|\widehat{x}_{n}\|_{2}\|z\|_{2}.

Lemma
13.7
, Fact
1
, and Lemma
13.2
yield
\|\widehat{x}_{n}\|_{2}=O_{p}(1)
, implying

\absolutevalue{\hx^T\Sx z_1}\leq C\|z\|_{2}

(137)

with high probability for sufficiently large
C
,
p
,
q
, and
n
.
For the rest of the proof,
C
should be understood as a large constant whose value changes from line to line.
Using (
127
) and (
134
) we obtain that for sufficiently large
p
,
q
, and
n
:

\displaystyle|T_{1}(z;j)|=

\displaystyle\ \absolutevalue{(\hx^T\hSx\hx-\rhk)\Delta_{\Gamma,1}(j)^T(\hSx-\Sx)z_1}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\ Cs^{\kappa}\lambda^{2}\|\Delta_{\Gamma,1}(j)\|_{1}\|z\|_{2}\quad(j=1,\ldots,p+q)

with high probability.
Similarly, (
127
), when combined with (
135
), leads to

\displaystyle|T_{2}(z;j)|=

\displaystyle\ \absolutevalue{(\hx^T\hSx\hx-\rhk)\Delta_{\Gamma,1}(j)^T\Sx z_1}\leq Cs^{\kappa}\lambda\|\Delta_{\Gamma,1}(j)\|_{2}\|z\|_{2}\quad(j=1,\ldots,p+q),

whereas (
134
) implies

|T_{3}(z;j)|=\absolutevalue{\rhk\Delta_{\Gamma,1}(j)^T(\hSx-\Sx)z_1}\leq C\lambda\|\Delta_{\Gamma,1}(j)\|_{1}\|z\|_{2}\quad(j=1,\ldots,p+q).

We use the bounds in (
130
) and (
136
) to obtain

\displaystyle|T_{4}(z;j)|=

\displaystyle\ \absolutevalue{\rhk\Delta_{\Gamma,1}(j)^T(\hSx-\Sx)\hx\hx^T(\hSx-\Sx)z_1}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\ Cs^{1/2}\lambda^{2}\|\Delta(j)\|_{1}\|z\|_{2}\quad(j=1,\ldots,p+q),

and use (
130
) and (
137
) to show

|T_{5}(z;j)|=\absolutevalue{\Delta_{\Gamma,1}(j)^T(\hSx-\Sx)\hx\hx^T\Sx z_1}\leq C\lambda\|\Delta(j)\|_{1}\|z\|_{2}\quad(j=1,\ldots,p+q)

with high probability for sufficiently large
n
.
Similarly, (
131
) and (
136
) and jointly imply that

|T_{6}(z;j)|=\absolutevalue{z_1^T(\hSx-\Sx)\hx\hx^T\Sx\Delta_{\Gamma,1}(j)}\leq Cs^{1/2}\lambda\|z\|_{2}\|\Delta(j)\|_{2}\quad(j=1,\ldots,p+q)

with high probability for sufficiently large
n
.
Finally,

|T_{7}(z;j)|=\absolutevalue{\Delta_{\Gamma,1}^T\Sx(\hx\hx^T-x^0(x^0)^T)\Sx z_1}\leq M^{2}\|\Delta(j)\|_{2}\|z\|_{2}\|\widehat{x}_{n}\widehat{x}_{n}^{T}-x^{0}(x^{0})^{T}\|_{F},

where the Frobenius norm is
O_{p}(s^{\kappa}\lambda)
by (
133
).
Therefore,

|T_{7}(z;j)|\leq Cs^{\kappa}\lambda\|\Delta(j)\|_{2}\|z\|_{2}\quad(j=1,\ldots,p+q)

with high probability for sufficiently large
n
.
Lemma
13.15
implies

|T_{8}(z;j)|=\absolutevalue{\Delta_{\Gamma,1}(j)^T(\hSxy-\Sxy)z_2}\leq\lambda\|\Delta(j)\|_{1}\|z\|_{2}\quad(j=1,\ldots,p+q)

with high probability for sufficiently large
n
.
Combining the above pieces leads to

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{8}|T_{i}(z;j)|\leq C\|z\|_{2}\lambda((1+s^{1/2}\lambda+s^{\kappa}\lambda)\|\Delta(j)\|_{1}+(s^{1/2}+s^{\kappa})\|\Delta(j)\|_{2})\quad(j=1,\ldots,p+q)

with high probability for sufficiently large
n
.
By Condition
4.1
,
\kappa\in[1/2,1]
, and Fact
1
implies
s^{\kappa+1/2}\lambda=o(1)
. Therefore

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{8}|T_{i}(z;j)|\leq C\|z\|_{2}\lambda(\|\Delta(j)\|_{1}+s^{\kappa}\|\Delta(j)\|_{2})\quad(j=1,\ldots,p+q).

22
Proof of the Lemmas and Facts in Supplement
13

Proof 22.1
(of Lemma
13.1
).

Let

w_{1}^{*}=\inf_{w\in\{\pm 1\}}\|w\widehat{\alpha}_{n}-\alpha_{0}\|_{2}\quad\text{and}\quad w_{2}^{*}=\inf_{w\in\{\pm 1\}}\|w\widehat{\beta}_{n}-\beta_{0}\|_{2}.

Let us define

\widehat{\rho}_{n}^{*}=\dfrac{(w_{1}^{*}\widehat{\alpha}_{n})^{T}\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,xy}(w_{2}^{*}\widehat{\beta}_{n})}{\sqrt{(w_{1}^{*}\widehat{\alpha}_{n})^{T}\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,x}(w_{1}^{*}\widehat{\alpha}_{n})}\sqrt{(w_{2}^{*}\widehat{\beta}_{n})^{T}\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,y}(w_{2}^{*}\widehat{\beta}_{n})}}.

Since
{\rho}_{0}>0
, we have
\|\widehat{\rho}_{n}|-{\rho}_{0}|\leq|\widehat{\rho}_{n}^{*}-{\rho}_{0}|
. Thus it suffices to prove the result for
\widehat{\rho}_{n}^{*}
.
First we show that the rate of
\widehat{\rho}_{n}^{*}
is mainly controlled by the numerator because the denominator converge to
1
in probability. For the sake of simplicity, we will assume that
w_{1}^{*}=1
and
w_{2}^{*}=1
. The proof for the other cases will be identical.

Simple algebra shows that the numerator is bounded above by

\displaystyle|\widehat{\alpha}_{n}^{T}\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,xy}\widehat{\beta}_{n}-\alpha_{0}^{T}{\Sigma}_{xy}\beta|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \bigg|(\widehat{\alpha}_{n}-\alpha_{0})^{T}\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,xy}\widehat{\beta}_{n}+\alpha_{0}(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,xy}-{\Sigma}_{xy})\widehat{\beta}_{n}+\alpha_{0}^{T}{\Sigma}_{xy}(\widehat{\beta}_{n}-\beta_{0})\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\ \bigg|(\widehat{\alpha}_{n}-\alpha_{0})^{T}\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,xy}\widehat{\beta}_{n}\bigg|+\bigg|\alpha_{0}(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,xy}-{\Sigma}_{xy})\widehat{\beta}_{n}\bigg|+\bigg|\alpha_{0}^{T}{\Sigma}_{xy}(\widehat{\beta}_{n}-\beta_{0})\bigg|

The first term can be bounded since

\displaystyle|(\widehat{\alpha}_{n}-\alpha_{0})^{T}\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,xy}\widehat{\beta}_{n}|\leq

\displaystyle\ \absolutevalue{(\ha-\alk)^T(\hSxy-\Sxy)\hb}+\absolutevalue{(\ha-\alk)^T\Sxy\hb}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{\leq}}

\displaystyle\ \|\widehat{\alpha}_{n}-\alpha_{0}\|_{1}|\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,xy}-{\Sigma}_{xy}|_{\infty}\|\widehat{\beta}_{n}\|_{2}+M\|\widehat{\alpha}_{n}-\alpha_{0}\|_{2}\|\widehat{\beta}_{n}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(b)}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\ O_{p}(s^{\kappa+1/2}\lambda^{2})+MO_{p}(s^{\kappa}\lambda)

(138)

where in step (a), we used Assumption
2.1
and (b) uses Condition
4.1
, with
\kappa
as defined in Condition
4.1
.
For the second term, note that Lemma
13.7
and Lemma
13.15
imply

\displaystyle\bigg|\alpha_{0}(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,xy}-{\Sigma}_{xy})\widehat{\beta}_{n}\bigg|\leq

\displaystyle\ \|\beta_{0}\|_{2}\|\alpha_{0}\|_{1}O_{p}(\lambda)=O_{p}(s^{1/2}\lambda).

(139)

For the third term, using Lemma
13.7
and Condition
4.1
,
we have

\displaystyle|\alpha_{0}^{T}{\Sigma}_{xy}(\widehat{\beta}_{n}-\beta_{0})|\leq\|\alpha_{0}\|_{2}\|{\Sigma}_{xy}\|_{op}\|\widehat{\beta}_{n}-\beta_{0}\|_{2}=O_{p}(M^{3/2}s^{\kappa}\lambda).

(140)

Therefore, using the expansion of
|\widehat{\alpha}_{n}^{T}{\Sigma}_{xy}\widehat{\beta}_{n}-\alpha^{T}{\Sigma}_{xy}\beta|
, and combining (
22.1
), (
139
), and (
140
), we have

|\widehat{\alpha}_{n}^{T}\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,xy}\widehat{\beta}_{n}-\alpha^{T}{\Sigma}_{xy}\beta|=O_{p}(s^{\kappa}\lambda)+O_{p}(s^{1/2}\lambda)+O_{p}(s^{\kappa+1/2}\lambda).

Because
\kappa\in\{1/2,1\}
, and
s\lambda\to 0
by Fact
1
, the above term is
O_{p}(s^{\kappa}\lambda)
.
This settles the case for the numerator of
\widehat{\rho}_{n}
, i.e.

|\widehat{\alpha}_{n}^{T}\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,xy}\widehat{\beta}_{n}-\alpha^{T}{\Sigma}_{xy}\beta|=O_{p}(s^{\kappa}\lambda)

(141)

For the denominator, it suffices to show that

\displaystyle\widehat{\alpha}_{n}^{T}\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,x}\widehat{\alpha}_{n}=1+o_{p}(1)

(142)

since the proof for
\widehat{\beta}_{n}^{T}\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,y}\widehat{\beta}_{n}
will be similar. To this end, proceeding as before, we decompose

\displaystyle|\widehat{\alpha}_{n}^{T}\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,x}\widehat{\alpha}_{n}-\alpha_{0}^{T}{\Sigma}_{x}\alpha_{0}|\leq|(\widehat{\alpha}_{n}-\alpha_{0})^{T}\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,x}\widehat{\alpha}_{n}|+|\alpha_{0}^{T}(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,x}-{\Sigma}_{x})\widehat{\alpha}_{n}|+|\alpha_{0}^{T}{\Sigma}_{x}(\widehat{\alpha}_{n}-\alpha_{0})|

(143)

Proceeding in a similar way as we did while proving (
22.1
), we can show that

|(\widehat{\alpha}_{n}-\alpha_{0})^{T}\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,x}\widehat{\alpha}_{n}|=O_{p}(s^{\kappa}\lambda).

The second term can be controlled in the same way as (
139
), to yield

|\alpha_{0}^{T}(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,x}-{\Sigma}_{x})\widehat{\beta}_{n}|=O_{p}(s^{1/2}\lambda).

For the third term, using Lemma
13.7
and Condition
4.1
, we obtain that

\displaystyle|\alpha_{0}^{T}{\Sigma}_{x}(\widehat{\beta}_{n}-\beta_{0})|\leq M\|\alpha_{0}\|_{2}\|\widehat{\beta}_{n}-\beta_{0}\|_{2}=O_{p}(M^{3/2}s^{\kappa}\lambda).

Therefore, (
143
) implies

|\widehat{\alpha}_{n}^{T}\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,x}\widehat{\alpha}_{n}-\alpha_{0}^{T}{\Sigma}_{x}\alpha_{0}|=O_{p}(s^{\kappa}\lambda)+O_{p}(s^{\kappa+1/2}\lambda)+O_{p}(s^{1/2}\lambda).

Since
s\lambda\to 0
by Fact
1
, and
\kappa\in\{1/2,1\}
,
|\widehat{\alpha}_{n}^{T}\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,x}\widehat{\alpha}_{n}-\alpha_{0}^{T}{\Sigma}_{x}\alpha_{0}|=o_{p}(1)
, which, combined with (
141
), implies

|\widehat{\rho}_{n}^{*}-{\rho}_{0}|=O_{p}(s^{\kappa}\lambda)
, and hence, the proof follows.

Proof 22.2
(of Lemma
13.2
).

Using Lemma
13.1
we derive

\displaystyle\|\widehat{\rho}_{n}|^{1/2}-{\rho}_{0}^{1/2}|=\frac{\|\widehat{\rho}_{n}|-{\rho}_{0}|}{|\widehat{\rho}_{n}|^{1/2}+{\rho}_{0}^{1/2}}\leq\frac{\|\widehat{\rho}_{n}|-{\rho}_{0}|}{{\rho}_{0}^{1/2}}={\rho}_{0}^{-1/2}O_{p}(s^{\kappa}\lambda),

which is
O_{p}(s^{\kappa}\lambda)
.
Next,

\displaystyle\inf_{w\in\{\pm 1\}}\|w|\widehat{\rho}_{n}|^{1/2}\widehat{\alpha}_{n}-{\rho}_{0}^{1/2}\alpha_{0}\|_{2}\leq

\displaystyle\ \absolutevalue{|\hro|^{1/2}-\rhk^{1/2}}\|\widehat{\alpha}_{n}\|_{2}+{\rho}_{0}^{1/2}\inf_{w\in\{\pm 1\}}\|w\widehat{\alpha}_{n}-\alpha_{0}\|_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ O_{p}(s^{\kappa}\lambda)O_{p}(1)+{\rho}_{0}^{1/2}O_{p}(s^{\kappa}\lambda)

by Condition
4.1
.
Also,

\displaystyle\inf_{w\in\{\pm 1\}}\|w|\widehat{\rho}_{n}|^{1/2}\widehat{\alpha}_{n}-{\rho}_{0}^{1/2}\alpha_{0}\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\ \absolutevalue{|\hro|^{1/2}-\rhk^{1/2}}\|\widehat{\alpha}_{n}\|_{1}+\inf_{w\in\{\pm 1\}}{\rho}_{0}^{1/2}\|w\widehat{\alpha}_{n}-\alpha_{0}\|_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ O_{p}(s^{\kappa}\lambda)\Big\{\|\alpha_{0}\|_{1}+O_{p}(s^{\kappa+1/2}\lambda)\Big\}+{\rho}_{0}^{1/2}O_{p}(s^{\kappa+1/2}\lambda)

by Condition
4.1
.
Now
\|\alpha_{0}\|_{1}\leq s^{1/2}\|\alpha_{0}\|_{2}=O_{p}(s^{1/2})
by Cauchy Schwarz inequality and Lemma
13.7
. On the other hand, Fact
1

implies
O_{p}(s^{\kappa+1/2}\lambda)=o_{p}(1)
, which leads to

\inf_{w\in\{\pm 1\}}\\
|\widehat{\rho}_{n}|^{1/2}w\widehat{\alpha}_{n}-({\rho}_{0})^{1/2}\alpha_{0}\|_{1}=O_{p}(s^{\kappa+1/2}\lambda).

Since similar results hold for
|\widehat{\rho}_{n}|^{1/2}\widehat{\beta}_{n}
as well, the proof follows.

Proof 22.3
(of Fact
1
).

Because
s^{\kappa}\lambda=n^{-1/4}o(1)
, we have
s^{\kappa}=n^{-1/4}\lambda^{-1}o(1)
, which leads to

\displaystyle s=

\displaystyle\ \frac{n^{1/(4\kappa)}}{(\log(p+q))^{1/(2\kappa)}}o(1),

which implies

\displaystyle s^{\kappa+1/2}=

\displaystyle\ \frac{n^{\frac{\kappa+1/2}{4\kappa}}}{(\log(p+q))^{\frac{\kappa+1/2}{2\kappa}}}o(1),

and

s^{\kappa+1/2}\lambda=\frac{n^{\frac{1/2-\kappa}{4\kappa}}}{(\log(p+q))^{\frac{1}{4\kappa}}}o(1).

Suppose
\kappa>1/2
. Then

s^{\kappa+1/2}\lambda=n^{\frac{1/2-\kappa}{4\kappa}}o(1)=o(1).

Now consider the case when
\kappa=1/2
. Then
s^{\kappa+1/2}\lambda=o((\log(p+q))^{-1/(4\kappa)})
, which is
o(1)
. Because
\kappa\geq 1/2
, we have
s\lambda\leq s^{\kappa+1/2}\lambda
. Therefore
s\lambda=o(1)
also follows.

Proof 22.4
(of Fact
5
).

Let
x^{\prime}=x/\|x\|_{2}
and
y^{\prime}=y/\|y\|_{2}
. Then

\|P_{x}-P_{y}\|^{2}_{F}=\|P_{x^{\prime}}-P_{y^{\prime}}\|^{2}_{2}\leq 2\inf_{w\in\{\pm 1\}}\|wx^{\prime}-y^{\prime}\|_{2}^{2}

where the last equality follows by Fact
4
.
Note that

\|wx^{\prime}-y^{\prime}\|_{2}\leq\|(wx-y)\|_{2}/\|x\|_{2}+\|y\|_{2}(\|x\|_{2}^{-1}-\|y\|_{2}^{-1})

where for any
s\in\{\pm 1\}
,

\displaystyle\absolutevalue{ \|x\|_2^{-1}-\|y\|_2^{-1}}=\frac{\absolutevalue{\|wx\|_2-\|y\|_2}}{\|x\|_{2}\|y\|_{2}}\leq\frac{\|wx-y\|_{2}}{\|x\|_{2}\|y\|_{2}}.

Thus,

\displaystyle\|wx^{\prime}-y^{\prime}\|_{2}\leq 2\|wx-y\|_{2}\|x\|_{2}^{-1}.

Similarly we can show that

\displaystyle\|wx^{\prime}-y^{\prime}\|_{2}\leq 2\|wx-y\|_{2}\|y\|_{2}^{-1}.

Hence, the proof follows.

Proof 22.5
(of Lemma
13.9
).

From Lemma 7 of
Janková & van de Geer 2018
, it follows that for sufficiently large
n
,

\|(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,x}-{\Sigma}_{x})v\|_{\infty}\leq C\|v\|_{2}\lambda

with high probability for some
C
depending only on the subgaussian parameter of
X
. Setting
v=e_{i}

(i=1,\ldots,p)
, it then follows that

|\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,xy}-{\Sigma}_{xy}|_{\infty}=\sup_{1\leq i\leq p}\|(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,xy}-{\Sigma}_{xy})e_{i}\|_{\infty}\leq C\lambda

with high probability.
The above could also be proved directly using Bernstein inequality.

Thus it remains to show that

\|(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,xy}-{\Sigma}_{xy})v\|_{\infty}\leq C\|v\|_{2}\lambda.

To that end, note that

\displaystyle|(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,xy}-{\Sigma}_{xy})v\|_{\infty}\leq\left|\left|\begin{bmatrix}\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,x}-{\Sigma}_{x}&\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,xy}-{\Sigma}_{xy}\\
\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,yx}-{\Sigma}_{yx}&\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,y}-{\Sigma}_{y}\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}0\\
v\end{bmatrix}\right|\right|_{\infty}\leq\|v\|_{2}C\lambda,

where
C
depends only on the subgaussian parameter of the vector
(X,Y)
.
Thus the proof follows.

Proof 22.6
(of Lemma
13.12
).

This lemma follows as a corollary to Lemma 10 of
Janková & van de Geer 2018
, which indicates that there exist
C_{1}
and
C_{2}
depending only on the sub-gaussian parameter of
X
so that

|\widehat{z}_{n}^{T}(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,x}-{\Sigma}_{x})\widehat{z}_{n}|\leq C_{1}\lambda\|\widehat{z}_{n}\|_{1}+C_{2}\bigg(\|\widehat{z}_{n}\|_{1}^{2}\|\widehat{z}_{n}\|_{2}^{2}\lambda^{2}+\|\widehat{z}_{n}\|_{1}\|\widehat{z}_{n}\|_{2}^{2}\lambda\bigg)

with high probability for sufficiently large
n,p
, and
q
.
Now since

\|\widehat{z}_{n}\|_{1}\leq s^{1/2}
,
\|\widehat{z}_{n}\|_{1}\lambda\leq s^{1/2}\lambda=o_{p}(1)
. Thus,

\|\widehat{z}_{n}\|_{1}\lambda(1+\|\widehat{z}_{n}\|_{1}\lambda)\leq 2\|\widehat{z}_{n}\|_{1}\lambda

and hence the result follows.

Proof 22.7
(of lemma
13.12
).

Let us denote
x=(\widehat{z}_{n},\widehat{w}_{n})
. Then writing
t=\widehat{z}_{n}^{T}(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,xy}-{\Sigma}_{xy})\widehat{w}_{n}
we note that

2t=x^{T}(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n}-\Sigma)x-\widehat{z}_{n}^{T}(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,x}-{\Sigma}_{x})\widehat{z}_{n}-\widehat{w}_{n}^{T}(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,y}-{\Sigma}_{y})\widehat{w}_{n}.

Lemma
13.12
indicates that there exist
C
depending only on the subgaussian parameter of
X
so that

\absolutevalue{\widehat z_n^T(\hSx-\Sx)\widehat z_n}+\absolutevalue{\widehat w_n^T(\hSy-\Sy)\widehat w_n}\leq C\lambda\Big(s^{1/2}(\|\widehat{z}_{n}\|_{2}^{2}+\|\widehat{w}_{n}\|_{2}^{2})+(\|\widehat{z}_{n}\|_{1}+\|\widehat{w}_{n}\|_{1})\Big)

with high probability as
n,p,q\to\infty
.
Since
[X^{T}Y^{T}]^{T}
is a sub-Gaussian matrix, Lemma
13.12
can be applied to the term
x^{T}(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n}-\Sigma)x
as well, and the result follows.

Proof 22.8
(of Lemma
13.15
).

We have

\displaystyle|x^{T}(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,x}-{\Sigma}_{x})\widehat{z}_{n}|\leq

\displaystyle\ |x^{T}(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,x}-{\Sigma}_{x})(\widehat{z}_{n}-z_{0})|+|x^{T}(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,x}-{\Sigma}_{x})z_{0}|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\ \|x\|_{1}|\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,x}-{\Sigma}_{x}|_{\infty}\|\widehat{z}_{n}-z_{0}\|_{1}+\|x\|_{1}\|(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,x}-{\Sigma}_{x})z_{0}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ C\Big(\|x\|_{1}\lambda o_{p}(1)+\|x\|_{1}\|z_{0}\|_{2}\lambda\Big)

with high probability for large
n
for some
C>0
depending only the subgaussian parameter of
X
by by Lemma
13.9
and Lemma
13.9
.
Therefore

|x^{T}(\widehat{\Sigma}_{n,x}-{\Sigma}_{x})\widehat{z}_{n}|\leq C\|z_{0}\|_{2}\|x\|_{1}\lambda

with high probability as
n,p,q\to\infty
.

Proof 22.9
(of Fact
7
).

Note that

(a^{T}X,b^{T}X)\sim N_{2}\left(0,\begin{bmatrix}a^{T}{\Sigma}_{x}a&a^{T}{\Sigma}_{x}b\\
b^{T}{\Sigma}_{x}a&b^{T}{\Sigma}_{x}b\end{bmatrix}\right),(z^{T}X,\ d^{T}Y)\sim N_{2}\left(0,\begin{bmatrix}z^{T}{\Sigma}_{x}z&z^{T}{\Sigma}_{xy}d\\
d^{T}{\Sigma}_{yx}z&d^{T}{\Sigma}_{y}d\end{bmatrix}\right)

Therefore, Fact
6
implies that

\text{var}(a^{T}XX^{T}b)=(a^{T}{\Sigma}_{x}a)(b^{T}{\Sigma}_{x}b)+(a^{T}{\Sigma}_{x}b)^{2}

and

\text{var}(z^{T}XY^{T}d)=(z^{T}{\Sigma}_{x}z)(d^{T}{\Sigma}_{y}d)+(z^{T}{\Sigma}_{xy}d)^{2}.

Proof 22.10
(of fact
8
).

Suppose
a\in\mathbb{R}^{p}
and
b\in\mathbb{R}^{q}
. Since
X
and
Y
are sub-Gaussian random vectors,
a^{T}X
and
b^{T}Y
are sub-Gaussian random variables. Therefore Lemma 2.7.5 of
Vershynin 2018
implies that
\|a^{T}XY^{T}b\|_{\psi_{1}}\leq\|a^{T}X\|_{\psi_{2}}\|b^{T}Y\|_{\psi_{2}}
. By definition of the sub-Gaussian norm
\|X\|_{\psi_{2}}
of a random vector
X\in\mathbb{R}^{p}

(
Vershynin 2018
, cf. Definition 3.4.1)
, we have

\|a^{T}X\|_{\psi_{2}}\leq\|a\|_{2}\|X\|_{\psi_{2}}
for any
a\in\mathbb{R}^{p}
.
Therefore,

\|a^{T}XY^{T}b\|_{\psi_{1}}\leq\|a\|_{2}\|b\|_{2}\|X\|_{\psi_{2}}\|Y\|_{\psi_{2}}.

Similarly we can show that
a,c\in\mathbb{R}^{p}
satisfy

\|a^{T}XX^{T}c\|_{\psi_{1}}\leq\|a\|_{2}\|c\|_{2}\|X\|_{\psi_{2}}^{2}.

Proof 22.11
(of Lemma
13.16
).

This lemma follows by straightforward calculation. Note that

\displaystyle\text{var}(T)=

\displaystyle\ \text{var}(a^{T}XX^{T}b)+\text{var}(c^{T}YY^{T}d)+\text{var}(z^{T}XY^{T}d)+\text{var}(b^{T}XY^{T}\gamma)

\displaystyle\ +2\text{cov}(a^{T}XX^{T}b,c^{T}YY^{T}d)-2\text{cov}(a^{T}XX^{T}b,z^{T}XY^{T}d)-2\text{cov}(a^{T}XX^{T}b,b^{T}XY^{T}\gamma)

\displaystyle\ -2\text{cov}(c^{T}YY^{T}d,z^{T}XY^{T}d)-2\text{cov}(c^{T}YY^{T}d,b^{T}XY^{T}\gamma)+2\text{cov}(z^{T}XY^{T}d,b^{T}XY^{T}\gamma)

First, we will find the variance of
a^{T}XX^{T}b
. To that end, note that Fact
7
implies

\displaystyle\text{var}(a^{T}XX^{T}b)+\text{var}(c^{T}YY^{T}d)+\text{var}(z^{T}XY^{T}d)+\text{var}(b^{T}XY^{T}\gamma)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ (a^{T}{\Sigma}_{x}a)(b^{T}{\Sigma}_{x}b)+(a^{T}{\Sigma}_{x}b)^{2}+(c^{T}{\Sigma}_{x}c)(d^{T}{\Sigma}_{y}d)+(c^{T}{\Sigma}_{y}d)^{2}

\displaystyle\ +(z^{T}{\Sigma}_{x}z)(d^{T}{\Sigma}_{y}d)+(z^{T}{\Sigma}_{xy}d)^{2}+(b^{T}{\Sigma}_{x}b)(\gamma^{T}{\Sigma}_{y}\gamma)+(b^{T}{\Sigma}_{xy}\gamma)^{2}.

Now note that

\displaystyle\text{cov}(a^{T}XX^{T}b,c^{T}YY^{T}d)=

\displaystyle\ E[a^{T}XX^{T}bc^{T}YY^{T}d]-E[a^{T}XX^{T}b]E[c^{T}YY^{T}d]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ E\Big[a^{T}XX^{T}bc^{T}E[YY^{T}|X]d\Big]-a^{T}{\Sigma}_{x}bc^{T}{\Sigma}_{y}d

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\ E\Big[a^{T}XX^{T}bc^{T}({\Sigma}_{y}-{\Sigma}_{yx}{\Sigma}_{x}^{-1}{\Sigma}_{xy}+{\Sigma}_{yx}{\Sigma}_{x}^{-1}XX^{T}{\Sigma}_{x}^{-1}{\Sigma}_{xy})d\Big]-a^{T}{\Sigma}_{x}bc^{T}{\Sigma}_{y}d

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ (a^{T}{\Sigma}_{x}b)c^{T}({\Sigma}_{y}-{\Sigma}_{yx}{\Sigma}_{x}^{-1}{\Sigma}_{xy})d+E\Big[a^{T}XX^{T}b(c^{T}{\Sigma}_{yx}{\Sigma}_{x}^{-1}XX^{T}{\Sigma}_{x}^{-1}{\Sigma}_{xy}d)\Big]

\displaystyle\ -a^{T}{\Sigma}_{x}bc^{T}{\Sigma}_{y}d

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(b)}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\ -a^{T}{\Sigma}_{x}bc^{T}{\Sigma}_{yx}{\Sigma}_{x}^{-1}{\Sigma}_{xy}d+a^{T}{\Sigma}_{x}bc^{T}{\Sigma}_{yx}{\Sigma}_{x}^{-1}{\Sigma}_{xy}d

\displaystyle\ +a^{T}{\Sigma}_{xy}cb^{T}{\Sigma}_{xy}d+a^{T}{\Sigma}_{xy}db^{T}{\Sigma}_{xy}c

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ a^{T}{\Sigma}_{xy}cb^{T}{\Sigma}_{xy}d+a^{T}{\Sigma}_{xy}db^{T}{\Sigma}_{xy}c,

where in step (a), we used the fact that

E[YY^{T}|X]=Var(Y|X)+E[Y|X]E[Y|X]^{T},

and

\displaystyle Y|X\sim N({\Sigma}_{yx}{\Sigma}_{x}^{-1}X,\ {\Sigma}_{y}-{\Sigma}_{yx}{\Sigma}_{x}^{-1}{\Sigma}_{xy}),

(144)

and in step
(b), we used Fact
6
.
On the other hand,

\displaystyle\text{cov}(a^{T}XX^{T}b,z^{T}XY^{T}d)=

\displaystyle\ E\Big[a^{T}XX^{T}bz^{T}XY^{T}d\Big]-a^{T}{\Sigma}_{x}bz^{T}{\Sigma}_{xy}d

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ E\Big[a^{T}XX^{T}bz^{T}XE[Y|X]^{T}d\Big]-a^{T}{\Sigma}_{x}bz^{T}{\Sigma}_{xy}d

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\ E\Big[a^{T}XX^{T}bz^{T}X({\Sigma}_{yx}{\Sigma}_{x}^{-1}X)^{T}d\Big]-a^{T}{\Sigma}_{x}bz^{T}{\Sigma}_{xy}d

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ E\Big[a^{T}XX^{T}bz^{T}XX^{T}{\Sigma}_{x}^{-1}{\Sigma}_{xy}d\Big]-a^{T}{\Sigma}_{x}bz^{T}{\Sigma}_{xy}d

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(b)}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\ \Big(a^{T}{\Sigma}_{x}bz^{T}{\Sigma}_{xy}d+a^{T}{\Sigma}_{x}zb^{T}{\Sigma}_{xy}d+a^{T}{\Sigma}_{xy}db^{T}{\Sigma}_{x}z\Big)-a^{T}{\Sigma}_{x}bz^{T}{\Sigma}_{xy}d

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ a^{T}{\Sigma}_{x}zb^{T}{\Sigma}_{xy}d+a^{T}{\Sigma}_{xy}db^{T}{\Sigma}_{x}z

where (a) follows from (
144
) and (b) follows from Fact
6
.
Similarly, we can show that

\displaystyle\text{cov}(a^{T}XX^{T}b,b^{T}XY^{T}\gamma)=a^{T}{\Sigma}_{x}bb^{T}{\Sigma}_{xy}\gamma+a^{T}{\Sigma}_{xy}\gamma b^{T}{\Sigma}_{x}b

\displaystyle\text{cov}(c^{T}YY^{T}d,z^{T}XY^{T}d)=c^{T}{\Sigma}_{yx}zd^{T}{\Sigma}_{y}d+c^{T}{\Sigma}_{y}dd^{T}{\Sigma}_{yx}z

\displaystyle\text{cov}(c^{T}YY^{T}d,b^{T}XY^{T}\gamma)=c^{T}{\Sigma}_{yx}bd^{T}{\Sigma}_{y}\gamma+c^{T}{\Sigma}_{y}\gamma d^{T}{\Sigma}_{yx}b

Finally,

\displaystyle\text{cov}(z^{T}XY^{T}d,b^{T}XY^{T}\gamma)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ E[z^{T}XY^{T}db^{T}XY^{T}\gamma]-z^{T}{\Sigma}_{xy}db^{T}{\Sigma}_{xy}\gamma

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ E\Big[z^{T}XX^{T}bd^{T}E[YY^{T}|X]\gamma\Big]-z^{T}{\Sigma}_{xy}db^{T}{\Sigma}_{xy}\gamma

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\ E\Big[z^{T}XX^{T}bd^{T}\Big({\Sigma}_{y}-{\Sigma}_{yx}{\Sigma}_{x}^{-1}{\Sigma}_{xy}+{\Sigma}_{yx}{\Sigma}_{x}^{-1}XX^{T}{\Sigma}_{x}^{-1}{\Sigma}_{xy})\gamma\Big]-z^{T}{\Sigma}_{xy}db^{T}{\Sigma}_{xy}\gamma

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ E\Big[z^{T}XX^{T}bd^{T}{\Sigma}_{y}\gamma\Big]-E\Big[z^{T}XX^{T}bd^{T}{\Sigma}_{yx}{\Sigma}_{x}^{-1}{\Sigma}_{xy}\gamma\Big]

\displaystyle\ +E\Big[z^{T}XX^{T}bd^{T}{\Sigma}_{yx}{\Sigma}_{x}^{-1}XX^{T}{\Sigma}_{x}^{-1}{\Sigma}_{xy}\gamma\Big]-z^{T}{\Sigma}_{xy}db^{T}{\Sigma}_{xy}\gamma

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(b)}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\ z^{T}{\Sigma}_{x}bd^{T}{\Sigma}_{y}\gamma-z^{T}{\Sigma}_{x}bd^{T}{\Sigma}_{yx}{\Sigma}_{x}^{-1}{\Sigma}_{xy}\gamma+\Big(z^{T}{\Sigma}_{x}bd^{T}{\Sigma}_{yx}{\Sigma}_{x}^{-1}{\Sigma}_{xy}\gamma

\displaystyle\ +z^{T}{\Sigma}_{xy}\gamma b^{T}{\Sigma}_{xy}d+z^{T}{\Sigma}_{xy}db^{T}{\Sigma}_{xy}\gamma\Big)-z^{T}{\Sigma}_{xy}db^{T}{\Sigma}_{xy}\gamma

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ z^{T}{\Sigma}_{x}bd^{T}{\Sigma}_{y}\gamma+z^{T}{\Sigma}_{xy}\gamma b^{T}{\Sigma}_{xy}d,

where (a) follows from (
144
) and (b) follows from Fact
6
. Thus
\text{var}(T)
equals

\displaystyle(a^{T}{\Sigma}_{x}a)(b^{T}{\Sigma}_{x}b)+(a^{T}{\Sigma}_{x}b)^{2}+(c^{T}{\Sigma}_{x}c)(d^{T}{\Sigma}_{y}d)+(c^{T}{\Sigma}_{y}d)^{2}

\displaystyle\ +(z^{T}{\Sigma}_{x}z)(d^{T}{\Sigma}_{y}d)+(z^{T}{\Sigma}_{xy}d)^{2}+(b^{T}{\Sigma}_{x}b)(\gamma^{T}{\Sigma}_{y}\gamma)+(b^{T}{\Sigma}_{xy}\gamma)^{2}

\displaystyle\ +2(a^{T}{\Sigma}_{xy}c)(b^{T}{\Sigma}_{xy}d)+2(a^{T}{\Sigma}_{xy}d)(b^{T}{\Sigma}_{xy}c)+2(z^{T}{\Sigma}_{x}b)(d^{T}{\Sigma}_{y}\gamma)+2(z^{T}{\Sigma}_{xy}\gamma)(b^{T}{\Sigma}_{xy}d)

\displaystyle\ -2(a^{T}{\Sigma}_{x}z)(b^{T}{\Sigma}_{xy}d)-2(a^{T}{\Sigma}_{xy}d)(b^{T}{\Sigma}_{x}z)-2(a^{T}{\Sigma}_{x}b)(b^{T}{\Sigma}_{xy}\gamma)-2(a^{T}{\Sigma}_{xy}\gamma)(b^{T}{\Sigma}_{x}b)

\displaystyle\ -2(c^{T}{\Sigma}_{yx}z)(d^{T}{\Sigma}_{y}d)-2(c^{T}{\Sigma}_{y}d)(d^{T}{\Sigma}_{yx}z)-2(c^{T}{\Sigma}_{yx}b)(d^{T}{\Sigma}_{y}\gamma)-2(c^{T}{\Sigma}_{y}\gamma)(d^{T}{\Sigma}_{yx}b)
